HERO
H1: Become a Life Impactor
Subline:
 1M+ entrepreneurs to impact by 2030.
Supporting line:
 1 Action = 1 Life Impacted
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: The number that matters
"Success is not just what you earn. It is how many lives you impact."
Most communities measure activity — members joined, events held, rooms filled.
We measure impact.
Every referral given. Every collaboration created. Every person guided. One action, one life impacted, tracked in real time on the Unity App.
________________


H2: Why one million
India runs on MSMEs. They employ crores of people, build cities and sustain families — largely without support systems, without visibility, and without anyone to call when things become difficult.
Every honest business story deserves respect and visibility.
One million is not a marketing number. It is a working target large enough to matter and specific enough to build toward, one Circle and one relationship at a time.
By 2030.
________________


H2: How it is built
Not through campaigns. Through arithmetic.
18 Circles. 10 forms of collaboration. 20–40 entrepreneurs in every room.
One Peer helps three entrepreneurs this year. Those three help three more. A Circle of forty, giving consistently, changes hundreds of businesses across a decade.
Multiply that across cities and countries, and one million stops being an ambition and becomes a matter of time.
[ See the 10 Ways ]


________________


H2: What impact means here
Impact is not money and it is not charity.
Impact is confidence, visibility and support.
If one entrepreneur feels less alone — impact has begun.
If one business finds clarity — impact has begun.
If one founder finds the right peer — impact has begun.
It looks ordinary from the outside. A forty-minute conversation that prevents an eighteen-month mistake. An introduction that lands a first significant client. A vendor recommendation that saves three months of searching. A phone call on a difficult night from someone who understands exactly what that night feels like.
None of these make headlines. All of them change the direction of a business, and often of a family.
Numbers are milestones. Lives are the real metric.
________________


H2: The impact multiplies
A single business is never a single life.
When one entrepreneur grows, a team keeps its jobs and then grows. Suppliers gain a reliable customer. A family gains stability. An employee eventually leaves and starts their own business.
That is why we count lives rather than businesses.
________________


H2: Where we are
[Live counters]
[X] lives impacted · [X] Peers · [X] Circles · [X] cities
Every number is compiled from actions logged and confirmed in the Unity App.
________________


H2: Your part in it
You do not join this mission separately. You join it the day you become a Peer.
Every introduction you make, every entrepreneur you mentor, every hour you give to someone in a difficult month becomes part of what this community gives to a million people.
Your Impact Score is your share of it.
[ Download Unity App ]


H2: How progress is measured
A mission that cannot be measured is only a statement.
Every contribution inside Peers Global is logged in the Unity App and confirmed by the Peer who received it. Those confirmed contributions build each Peer's Life Impact Score — and together they form the community's progress toward one million.
What we track:
* Confirmed collaborations across all 10 Ways
* Lives impacted through those collaborations
* Peers contributing actively
* Circles, cities and countries where the community is present
Progress is published openly, including when it is slower than we would like. A number nobody can verify is not a measurement.
[ Understand the Currency ]
________________


H2: What this means for a Peer
You do not join this mission separately. You join it the day you become a Peer.
Every introduction you make, every entrepreneur you mentor, every hour you give to someone in a difficult month becomes part of what this community gives to one million people.
Your contribution is recorded. Confirmed by the person you helped.
Your contribution is recognised. Through Peer Standing across the community.
Your contribution is rewarded. Through Peers Coin, redeemable in the Peers Global Marketplace.
And it is counted toward something considerably larger than your own business.
That is the quiet arrangement at the centre of this community. You help other entrepreneurs. The community helps you. And together the number moves.
________________


 
________________


H2: Join the mission
One million lives will not be reached by an organisation. It will be reached by entrepreneurs choosing, one at a time, to help another entrepreneur.
There is a Circle where your experience is exactly what someone needs. There is an entrepreneur who is one introduction away from a different year.
Your first contribution can happen this month.
[ Join Peers Global ] [ Find Your Circle ]

If confirmed-contribution tracking is not live yet in Unity, publish this page without the counter block and add it when the data is real. An unverified number on a page about honest measurement is the one thing that would undermine it.
Whether "one million lives" or "one million entrepreneurs" is the canonical mission. The 1 Million Entrepreneurs International Forum uses entrepreneurs; this page uses lives. Both are defensible, but the site must use one consistently everywhere.
Developer instructions
Counters pull from confirmed contributions in the impact tables, cached hourly. Never hardcode.
The counter block should degrade cleanly — if data is unavailable, hide the block rather than showing zeros.

Subline:
 The room where your business finds the right people.

 Every Peer belongs to a Circle. It is where relationships begin and where collaboration actually happens.

________________




H2: What a Circle is
A Circle brings together the right entrepreneurs around a common industry, goal, territory or business need.
20 to 40 curated entrepreneurs, growing toward a stable 40–50. Large enough that every business category is represented and every meeting has real substance. Small enough that the room genuinely knows your business.
It is the basic unit of Peers Global and the place where every Peer belongs.
________________


H2: Category exclusivity
No competition inside your Circle.
Each business category is held by one member. If you hold the seat for industrial packaging, you are the only industrial packaging business in that room.
Three things follow from it.
You can speak openly. Nobody is studying your pricing or waiting for you to name a client.
You get the full flow. Every requirement in your category comes to you, because there is no one else to send it to.
Giving costs you nothing. With no competitor in the room, helping another Peer is straightforward. That is why generosity is normal here.
At 40–50 members, a Circle covers a genuinely wide range of categories — which is what makes it possible for the room to solve almost anything between them.
________________


H2: Two kinds of Circle
Industry Circles
Built around a shared sector. Manufacturing, real estate, healthcare, technology, professional services and more.
Best for depth. In an Industry Circle, the people around you understand your market, your margins, your regulatory pressure and your customer without needing it explained. Referrals flow easily, and specialist knowledge is shared at a level that is difficult to find anywhere else.
[ Browse Industry Circles ]
Purpose Circles
Built around a shared goal or stage of business rather than a single sector. Scaling, exporting, succession, first-generation entrepreneurship, women-led business and more.
Best for breadth. In a Purpose Circle, the people around you are solving the same problem from twelve different industries, which produces solutions you would never have reached inside your own sector.
[ Browse Purpose Circles ]
Most Peers eventually belong to one of each. One for depth. One for perspective.
________________




H2: The rhythm
12 monthly meetings a year. The same Peers, a fixed schedule, the same four-part agenda in every Circle in every city.
60+ Collaboration Roundtables across the year.
150+ new introductions in a typical year.
Unlimited collaboration through the Unity App between meetings.
[ See the Meeting Agenda ]
________________


H2: Join more than one Circle
Members can join more than one Circle based on industry, territory, goals and business needs.
Your first Circle is an Industry Circle and becomes your primary Circle. Most Peers add a goal or purpose Circle alongside it — depth in their sector, perspective from outside it.
[ Browse by Industry ] [ Browse by Purpose ]
________________


H2: Local to global
Your Circle connects to your city, your district, your state, your country and the wider community.
Engage with city, state, national and international Circles across countries through the Unity App.
A relationship built in one room can open a door two thousand kilometres away.
[ See the Territory ]
________________

Goal & Purpose Circles — grouped by what you are building

H2: What makes a Circle different
Monthly Circle Mini-Conferences — knowledge sessions, industry panels, case studies and collaboration discussions. Not routine meets.
Trusted Collaborations — members actively share vendor requirements, project needs, partnerships, team needs, cross-referrals and tie-ups.
Confidential Forum — a private space where members discuss challenges, decisions and solutions.
Your Impact Score — every action logged, confirmed and counted.
MindMeld — cross-Circle city meetups across industries.
Leaders Retreats — state-level and national.
________________


H2: What happens inside a Circle
Over a year in a Circle, a Peer typically gives and receives across all ten forms of collaboration — referrals, mentorship, joint ventures, knowledge, problem solving, vendor connections, funding access, visibility, execution support, and support through a difficult month.
Every one of those contributions is logged in the Unity App and confirmed by the Peer who received it.

H2: If there is no Circle near you
The map is not finished. It is being built by entrepreneurs, one city at a time.
Every Circle that exists today began because one person decided to start it. If there is no Circle in your city, or none in your industry, that is an opening rather than a closed door.
We provide the structure, the systems, the training, the technology and the support of the wider community. You bring the leadership and the first group of the right people.

H2: Common questions
How many Peers are in a Circle?
 Enough that every business category is represented, and few enough that everyone genuinely knows everyone. A Circle is built for depth of relationship, not size.
How often does a Circle meet?
 On a fixed rhythm set by the Circle, so that Peers can plan around it and attendance stays consistent.
Can I join more than one Circle?
 Yes. Many Peers hold a seat in an Industry Circle and a Purpose Circle.
What if my category is already taken?
 You may join a different Circle in your city, join the waiting list for that seat, or start a new Circle. A Circle Director will tell you honestly which is realistic.
Do I have to bring referrals every meeting?
 Contribution takes ten forms, and referrals are only one of them. Knowledge, introductions, problem solving and support all count.
What does it cost?
 Membership details are on the Membership page, with tiers and inclusions published openly.
[ See Membership ]

HERO
H1: Industry Circles
Subline:
 A room where nobody needs your business explained to them.
Supporting line:
 Nine sectors. Each with its own Circle, its own language and its own opportunities.
[ Find a Circle Near You ] [ Visit as a Guest ]
Live band: [X] Industry Circles · [X] Cities · [X] Seats currently open
________________


H2: Why sector matters
There is a particular relief in explaining a problem to someone who already understands it.
No background required. No translating your business into simpler terms. You describe a payment cycle, a compliance change, a customer who has gone quiet — and the person across from you lived through the same thing this year.
That is what an Industry Circle gives you.
The people around you know your market, your margins, your seasonality, your regulatory pressure and your buyers. Conversations start at the level where they usually end. Advice is specific rather than general. And when someone makes an introduction, it is to a person who genuinely matters in your sector.
Depth is the advantage.
________________


H2: The nine Industry Circles
Manufacturing & Engineering Circle
 For manufacturers, engineering firms, industrial suppliers, machinery businesses and production units. Peers here share vendor networks, tackle supply chain and compliance together, connect on large contracts, and open buyer relationships across sectors. One of the strongest Circles for joint bids and capacity sharing.
Real Estate, Construction & Infrastructure Circle
 For developers, contractors, architects, material suppliers, brokers and infrastructure businesses. A sector built entirely on relationships, capital timing and land access — which is exactly why a trusted room matters more here than almost anywhere. Peers connect on projects, funding, approvals and joint development.
Technology, IT & Digital Services Circle
 For software companies, IT services, digital agencies, SaaS founders and technology consultancies. Peers share client access across industries, collaborate on projects too large for one team, exchange talent and hiring knowledge, and open partnerships with businesses that need what they build.
Healthcare, Wellness & Life Sciences Circle
 For hospital owners, clinics, diagnostics, pharma, medical devices, wellness businesses and life sciences ventures. A sector with distinct regulation, long approval cycles and specialised capital needs. Peers here navigate all three together and open referral flows across the healthcare ecosystem.
Education, Training & Skill Development Circle
 For schools, colleges, training institutions, edtech, coaching businesses and skill development organisations. Peers collaborate on curriculum, placement networks, faculty, technology and government programmes, and open institutional relationships to one another.
Events, Fashion, Apparel & Lifestyle Circle
 For event companies, fashion labels, apparel manufacturers, retail brands, hospitality and lifestyle businesses. A creative, seasonal, brand-driven sector where visibility and the right introduction change everything. Peers here collaborate on campaigns, retail access, production capacity and market entry.
CSR, NGO, Impact & Nation-Building Circle
 For social enterprises, NGOs, foundations, CSR heads and impact-driven organisations. Peers connect corporate CSR budgets with genuine implementation partners, share programme knowledge, collaborate on scale, and bring business discipline to social outcomes.
Franchise & Licensing Circle
 For franchisors, franchisees, licensing businesses and multi-outlet operators. A model built on systems, territory and trust. Peers share territory intelligence, operational systems, vendor networks and expansion partners, and connect brands with operators.
Sustainable & ESG Business Circle
 For renewable energy, waste management, sustainable products, green technology and ESG-focused businesses. A young sector with policy complexity, specialised funding and long horizons. Peers here share regulatory knowledge, connect to green capital, and open institutional buyers.
[ Find Your Industry Circle ]
________________


H2: What an Industry Circle gives you
Referrals that fit — requirements inside your sector flow directly to you.
Buyers you cannot reach alone — Peers serving your industry from different angles open their customer relationships.
Suppliers already tested — vendor recommendations from Peers operating in the same conditions.
Specialist knowledge — regulatory changes, pricing shifts and market conditions, understood by people living inside them.
Sector-level opportunities — large contracts, joint bids and partnerships no single business could take alone.
Honest benchmarking — an accurate sense of where your business stands, from people with no reason to flatter you.
[ Explore the 10 Ways of Collaboration ]
________________


H2: One seat per category
Inside every Industry Circle, each business category holds a single seat.
If you hold the seat for logistics in the Manufacturing Circle, you are the only logistics business in that room. Every requirement in your category comes to you, and no one across the table is studying your pricing.
Complementary businesses within one sector. Never overlapping ones.
[ See Open Seats ]

 Each card: Circle name · sector · city · rhythm · next meeting · seats open

H2: Who joins an Industry Circle
* Your business operates in a defined sector with its own cycles and language
* Most of your opportunities come from within your industry
* You want specialist knowledge rather than general business advice
* You are looking for buyers, suppliers or partners inside your market
* You want visibility among the people who matter in your sector
Many Peers hold a seat here and a second in a Purpose Circle — depth in their own sector, perspective from outside it.
[ Browse Purpose Circles ]
________________


H2: Common questions
Will my competitor be in the same room?
 No. Each business category holds one seat, and Circles are composed of complementary rather than competing businesses.
My business spans two sectors. Which Circle?
 Join the one where most of your opportunities originate. A Circle Director will help you decide.
What if my industry is not listed?
 Speak to a Circle Director. You may fit a broader sector Circle, or be well positioned to found a new one.
What if the seat for my category is taken?
 Join a different Circle in your city, join the waiting list for that seat, or start a new Circle. A Director will tell you honestly which is realistic.
Can I be in an Industry Circle and a Purpose Circle?
 Yes. This is the most common pattern among established Peers.

HERO
H1: Purpose Circles
Subline:
 Ten industries. One shared ambition.
Supporting line:
 Circles built around where you are going, not what you sell.
[ Find a Circle Near You ] [ Visit as a Guest ]
Live band: [X] Purpose Circles · [X] Cities · [X] Seats currently open
________________


H2: The ambition you share is bigger than the product you sell
Some challenges belong to a sector. Most do not.
Taking a company public. Entering an export market for the first time. Building a family business into its next generation. Raising your first round. Growing a business at thirty with no template to follow.
None of these are industry problems. A manufacturer, a hospital owner and a software founder pursuing the same goal are pursuing the same goal.
Purpose Circles bring those entrepreneurs into one room.
When ten people from ten industries look at your challenge, you get ten genuinely different approaches. Someone solved your hiring problem with a method from a completely unrelated sector. Someone structured a deal in a way your industry has never considered.
Perspective is the advantage.
________________


H2: The ten Purpose Circles
Import, Export & Global Trade Circle
 For entrepreneurs trading across borders — exporters, importers, trading houses and businesses entering international markets. Peers share buyer networks in specific countries, documentation and compliance knowledge, freight and logistics partners, and open markets they already operate in.
Startup Founders Circle
 For founders building early-stage ventures. Peers work through product, hiring, capital, go-to-market and the specific pressure of the first years. Access to investors, mentors and first customers, alongside people at the same stage in the same year.
SME IPO Goal Circle
 For established SMEs preparing to list. One of the most specific and demanding journeys a business can take — compliance readiness, governance, auditors, merchant bankers, valuation and timing. Peers here are walking the same road, some ahead, some behind, all sharing what they learn.
Investors Circle
 For investors, angel networks, family offices and entrepreneurs deploying capital. Peers share deal flow, co-investment opportunities, diligence knowledge and sector intelligence, and connect capital with businesses across the wider community.
Global Expansion (Cross-Border) Circle
 For businesses expanding operations into new countries — entity setup, local partners, hiring, regulation and market entry. Peers who have already entered a market open it for those arriving next.
MSME Entrepreneurs Circle
 For the businesses that form the backbone of this economy. Peers work on growth, formalisation, credit access, digitisation, government schemes and market expansion — with visibility and support that MSMEs rarely receive elsewhere.
Family Business Circle
 For family-owned enterprises navigating succession, governance, next-generation leadership, professionalisation and the particular complexity of business among relatives. Peers here understand a set of conversations that cannot happen anywhere else.
Young Entrepreneurs Circle (Below 35)
 For entrepreneurs building early in life. Peers share the specific challenges of leading people older than you, building credibility, managing rapid growth, and constructing a network from scratch — alongside access to senior mentors across the community.
Leadership & Transformation Circle
 For entrepreneurs working on themselves as leaders — building second-line leadership, professionalising an owner-driven company, culture, systems and the transition from operator to leader. The Circle for founders who have realised the business will not grow past the person running it.
Sustainable & ESG Goal Circle
 For entrepreneurs across any industry building sustainability into their business — emissions, supply chain, reporting, green capital and ESG readiness. Peers share standards knowledge, connect to specialist funding, and open institutional buyers who require it.
[ Find Your Purpose Circle ]
________________


H2: What a Purpose Circle gives you
Solutions from outside your industry — approaches your sector has never tried.
Shared stage, shared pressure — Peers facing the same decisions in the same year.
Cross-industry partnerships — joint ventures visible only when different sectors sit together.
A wider network by default — your Circle connects you into ten industries at once.
Fresh eyes on old problems — nobody in the room is bound by how your industry has always done it.
Peers who understand the transition — growth, succession, listing and expansion are lonely stages, and considerably less so alongside people going through them.
[ Explore the 10 Ways of Collaboration ]
________________

 Each card: Circle name · purpose focus · city · rhythm · next meeting · seats open

	Specific to your industry

	Unsure? A Circle Director will help you decide based on your business and where it currently stands.

H2: Common questions
Do Purpose Circles also follow one seat per category?
 Yes. Industries are varied deliberately, and each business category still holds a single seat.
Will referrals still happen across different industries?
 Yes, and often more of them. Ten industries means ten separate customer bases, and a requirement one Peer cannot serve is usually served by another in the room.
Can I move between Circles as my business changes?
 Yes. Many Peers move from Startup Founders to Leadership & Transformation, or from MSME Entrepreneurs to SME IPO Goal, as they pass from one stage to the next.
Can I join both an Industry and a Purpose Circle?
 Yes. This is the most common pattern among established Peers.
What if my purpose is not listed?
 Speak to a Circle Director. If enough entrepreneurs share the same ambition, that is a Circle worth founding.

PRODUCTION NOTES
Word count: ~600 of copy · Page type: Form + conversion · Phase: 1
Purpose of this page
Lead capture and app download. Nothing else. Every element serves one of those two actions.
No Circle listings. No seat availability. No city coverage displayed. The visitor never learns what does not exist yet — they only learn that someone will personally help them find their place.
App-first strategy — apply across the whole site
Set this as a standing rule for every page from here onward:
Primary CTA on informational pages: Download Unity App
Primary CTA on decision pages: Submit Enquiry or Apply
Every page carries at least one app CTA
The reasoning: a form gives you one lead who then waits. An app download gives you an engaged prospect who explores, sees the community, and converts themselves. It also removes the pressure of a form from anyone who is still deciding.
Pages to update with an app CTA: homepage, /the-idea, /circles, /circles/industry, /circles/purpose, /10-ways-of-collaboration, /culture-and-code, /1-million-mission.
Add a persistent app download strip in the footer sitewide and a dismissible app banner on mobile.
Form design
Six steps, progressive disclosure — one section visible at a time with a progress bar. A long form shown all at once loses people at the scroll.
Mobile-first. Large tap targets. Selection buttons rather than dropdowns wherever possible.
Capture on submit regardless of completion. If someone abandons after step two, you still have a name, a number and a city. Save partial submissions.
The industry list mirrors your nine Industry Circles exactly, so leads self-classify into your real structure.
Developer instructions
Form writes to the CRM with all fields tagged for routing — city, industry, Circle preference, urgency.
Routing logic: by city where a Regional Director exists, by industry where an Industry Director exists, otherwise to central. Notify the assigned Director immediately.
The 48-hour promise must be operationally real. Build an escalation alert if a lead sits unassigned past 24 hours.
App download links should use deferred deep linking so a user who downloads from this page lands on the Circle exploration screen, not a generic home screen.
Track separately: form submissions, app downloads from this page, and the ratio between them. That ratio tells you which path your traffic actually prefers.

PAGE: THE CIRCLE MEETING EXPERIENCE
PAGE: THE CIRCLE MEETING EXPERIENCE
URL: /circle-meeting-experience
 Menu: Circles → The Circle Meeting Experience
Meta title: Inside a Circle Meeting | The Peers Global Agenda
Meta description: The four-part Peers Global Circle meeting agenda — Gratitude & Life Impact Round, Brand Showcase, Impact Mentor Masterclass and Collaboration Roundtables.
Primary keyword: business circle meeting agenda
Secondary keywords: business networking meeting format, entrepreneur group meeting structure, what happens in a business circle
Primary CTA: Download Unity App
Secondary CTA: Visit as a Guest
Internal links: /circles · /circles/find · /10-ways-of-collaboration · /culture-and-code · /unity · /the-currency
________________


HERO
H1: Inside a Circle Meeting
Subline:
 Four parts. One purpose. Every Circle, every city, every month.
Supporting line:
 Here is the exact agenda, so you know what to expect before you walk in.
[ Download Unity App ] [ Visit as a Guest ]
________________


H2: Why the agenda never changes
Put twelve entrepreneurs in a room without structure and you get a pleasant conversation that produces nothing.
The agenda is what turns goodwill into outcomes.
Every Circle meeting follows the same four segments, in the same order, in every city and every country. A Peer who has attended a meeting in one city recognises immediately what is happening in another.
That consistency is deliberate. Nobody has to wonder when it is their turn, how to ask for something, or whether their contribution will be noticed. The agenda holds the room so the people in it can focus on each other.
________________


H2: THE AGENDA
01 · Gratitude & Life Impact Round
Every Peer declares their impact.
"This month I impacted 12 lives."
The meeting opens with what people gave, not with what they want.
Each Peer states the impact they created since the last meeting — the referrals given, the introductions made, the knowledge shared, the entrepreneurs they stood beside. Contributions are logged and confirmed in the Unity App, and this is the moment the room hears them out loud.
Why it comes first:
It sets the tone for everything that follows. Before any Peer asks for anything, the room has already heard an hour of giving. Asking becomes natural because generosity is already in the air.
It also makes contribution visible. In a large community, giving that goes unseen slowly stops happening. Here, it is the first thing anyone hears.
And it changes how Peers think between meetings. When you know you will declare your impact in front of the room, you look for opportunities to help all month.
[ Understand Life Impact ]
________________


02 · Brand Showcase
Four minutes to present. Structured for collaboration, not applause.
One Peer takes the floor to present their business properly — what they do, who they serve, what they are building, and specifically what they need from the room.
Four minutes, structured deliberately. This is not a pitch and the room is not an audience. The format is built so that by the end, every Peer present knows exactly how to help — which introduction to make, which client to mention, which supplier to recommend.
Why it works:
Most business rooms let people describe themselves vaguely and then wonder why nothing happens. A structured showcase converts this is my business into this is what I need and here is who can give it to me.
Peers leave this segment with actions, not impressions.
________________


03 · Impact Mentor Masterclass
One expert. One subject. Twenty minutes of applicable insight.
A senior Peer or invited expert teaches one thing properly.
Not a motivational talk. Not a general overview. One subject, taught by someone who has done it, with enough depth that a Peer can act on it the same week.
Topics come from what the Circle actually needs — a market entry, a compliance change, a hiring system, a pricing model, a funding route, a technology shift.
Why twenty minutes:
Long enough to teach something real. Short enough that it stays practical. Nobody in this room has time for theory, and nobody needs another inspirational session.
Every masterclass is delivered by a Peer or a mentor from within the community. The knowledge stays inside the ecosystem, and the person teaching it earns Life Impact for the contribution.
________________


04 · Collaboration Roundtables
Where referrals, deals and partnerships are born every month.
The working part of the meeting.
Peers break into focused roundtables and get into actual collaboration — referrals given, introductions made, requirements matched, partnerships explored, problems solved together.
This is where the ten Ways of Collaboration happen in practice. A Peer needing market access finds someone who already operates there. A Peer needing a vendor gets three tested recommendations. Two Peers realise they should be building something together.
Why it closes the meeting:
Everything before it was preparation. The impact round established generosity. The showcase told the room what someone needs. The masterclass gave everyone something new. The roundtables are where all of it converts into commitments.
Peers leave with named actions, scheduled one-to-ones, and introductions already promised.
[ See the 10 Ways of Collaboration ]
________________


H2: What happens between meetings
The meeting creates the commitments. The month delivers them.
Peer-to-Peer meetings. Two Peers meet one to one, outside the Circle, to properly understand each other's business. Most real collaboration begins here.
The Unity App. Contributions are logged and confirmed. One-to-ones are booked. Requirements are shared across Circles and cities.
Follow-through. An introduction promised at a roundtable becomes a call the following week. A referral becomes a client. And it all gets declared at the next Gratitude & Life Impact Round.
The meeting is monthly. The Circle works all month.
[ See How Unity Works ]
________________


H2: What guests experience
If you visit a Circle, here is exactly what will happen.
You will be welcomed by name. Someone will meet you, introduce you to Peers before the meeting starts, and make sure you are never standing alone.
You will be introduced to the room. Briefly and warmly, during the opening.
You will watch the full agenda. All four segments. You will see exactly how the room works and who would be around you.
You will be asked to present. Introduce yourself during the round table collaboration.
You will not be sold to. Nobody will pressure you, and nobody will ask you to decide anything on the day.
You will be contacted once afterwards. A Circle Director will call to hear what you thought. Once. Whatever you decide is respected.
Most entrepreneurs visit, take a week to think, and then apply. That is exactly how it should work.
[ Visit a Circle ]
________________


H2: What is not allowed
The agenda only works if certain things never happen inside it.
No selling from the floor. The Brand Showcase is the only presentation in the meeting, and it belongs to one Peer at a time.
No phones. For the full duration. Attention is the minimum contribution.
No breaking confidence. What is said in the room stays in the room, without exception.
No dominating the room. Every Peer gets the same time. Nobody gets more because their business is larger.
No pitching guests. A guest is there to observe, not to be surrounded.
[ Read the Peers Code ]
________________


H2: Common questions
How long is a Circle meeting?
 Long enough for all four segments to run properly, and short enough that busy business owners attend consistently. Your Circle Director will confirm the exact timing.
How often does a Circle meet?
 On a fixed monthly rhythm, so Peers can plan around it well in advance.
What if I have nothing to declare in the Impact Round?
 Say so honestly. Some months you give more, some months less. Over a year it balances, and the room knows the difference between an honest quiet month and someone who never gives.
Does every Peer get a Brand Showcase?
 Yes, on rotation, so every Peer gets the room's full attention on a scheduled basis.
Can I deliver a Masterclass?
 Yes. Teaching something you know well is one of the highest forms of contribution here, and it earns Life Impact.
Are meetings online or in person?
 Both formats exist across the community. Your Circle Director will confirm the format for your Circle.
Can I bring a guest?
 Yes. Bringing the right entrepreneur into your Circle is itself a contribution.
________________


CLOSING
Four segments. The same people. Every month.
That is how strangers become Peers, and how Peers become the reason your business grows.
Build Your Business. Build Your Relationships. Build Your Circle.
[ DOWNLOAD UNITY APP ] [ VISIT A CIRCLE ]

HERO
H1: Start a Circle
Subline:
 Some entrepreneurs look for the right room. Others build it.
Supporting line:
 Every Circle in this community exists because one person decided it should.
[ Apply to Found a Circle ] [ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: The room that does not exist yet
Somewhere in your city there are twenty entrepreneurs who would change each other's businesses if they were ever in the same room.
They are not. They pass each other at events, exchange cards, and go back to solving the same problems separately. The ecosystem exists. The room does not.
A Circle Founder is the person who builds it.
Not because someone asked them to, and not because it is easy, but because they can see what that room would produce and they are unwilling to wait for someone else to create it.
________________


H2: What a Circle Founder actually does
You decide what the Circle is for.
 An industry that has no room in your city. A purpose that a group of entrepreneurs around you are all pursuing alone. You define it.
You bring the first Peers.
 The founding group. Chosen deliberately — complementary businesses, one seat per category, entrepreneurs who will give as well as take. This is the most important work you will do, because the first twelve people set the culture for everyone who follows.
You set the standard from meeting one.
 How the room behaves in its first three meetings becomes how it behaves for years. A Founder holds that standard when it would be easier not to.
You run the first year.
 The rhythm, the agenda, the follow-through, the difficult conversation when someone is not contributing. Founding is not a title. It is a year of consistent work.
You build something that outlasts you.
 Eventually a Circle Director takes the day-to-day rhythm, and the Circle continues without depending on you. That is the objective.
[ Read the Circle Founder Role ]
________________


H2: What we provide
You do not build this alone.
The structure. The complete Circle model — meeting agenda, seat system, roles, rituals and standards. Everything already tested across the community.
The systems. The Unity App, member management, contribution tracking, attendance, and the Peer directory from day one.
The training. Founder induction covering how to compose a Circle, how to recruit the founding group, how to run the four-segment agenda, and how to handle the situations every new Circle encounters.
The support. A Regional Executive Director working alongside you, plus access to Founders who have already done it and will tell you honestly what to expect.
The brand. You launch under Peers Global — the credibility, the recognition system, the wider community, and the ability to tell a prospective Peer they are joining something with reach beyond one room.
The network. Your Circle plugs into the city, the region, the country and the wider community from its first meeting. Your Peers are never limited to the twelve people you recruited.
________________


H2: What you provide
Leadership. Someone has to hold the standard, and in the first year that is you.
The first group. Your credibility in your city or industry is what brings the founding Peers in. We cannot recruit them for you.
Consistency. A Circle is built by showing up every month without exception, especially in the early months when momentum is fragile.
Judgement. Deciding who belongs and, harder, who does not. A Founder who fills seats quickly builds a weaker Circle than one who fills them carefully.
Time. Real, recurring time. Founding a Circle is not a passive role and should not be taken on by anyone treating it as a title.
________________


H2: Who should start a Circle
This suits you if:
* You have built something already — a business, a team, a community
* People in your city or industry already come to you for introductions
* You give without being asked, and have done so for years
* You can hold a standard when it is uncomfortable
* You finish what you begin
* You want to build something beyond your own company
This does not suit you if you are looking for a title, a customer list, or a channel to sell into. A Circle led by someone selling to it collapses within a year, and every Peer in the room knows it before the Founder does.
________________


H2: Why entrepreneurs choose to found a Circle
Founding a Circle changes the person who does it, usually more than it changes anyone else in the room.
You become a better leader. Running a room of accomplished entrepreneurs — holding a standard among people who do not report to you — develops something no course teaches.
You build the strongest network in your city. The Founder of a Circle knows every Peer in it, and every Peer knows exactly who brought them together.
Your standing rises across the community. Founding is one of the highest contributions in Peers Global, recognised at city, regional and national level.
You gain access upward. Circle Founders work alongside Directors and Advisors across the community, and see the whole ecosystem rather than one room in it.
And you impact lives at scale. A Circle of thirty entrepreneurs, giving consistently for five years, changes hundreds of businesses. That is a legacy no single company produces on its own.
[ Explore the Leadership Path ]
________________


H2: How it works
1. You apply.
 Tell us about your business, your city or industry, and why you want to build this room.
2. We talk properly.
 A conversation with a Regional Executive Director about the opportunity, what founding involves, and whether it fits where you are in your own business.
3. You are inducted.
 Founder training — the model, the systems, the agenda, and how to build the founding group.
4. You build the founding Peers.
 With support, guidance and Unity from day one.
5. Your Circle launches.
 First meeting, first Give and Ask, first impact declared. And a room exists that did not exist before you.
[ Apply to Found a Circle ]
________________


H2: Common questions
Do I need to be a Peer already?
 Not necessarily. Many Founders join Peers Global specifically to build a Circle. What matters is standing in your city or industry and the ability to bring the right entrepreneurs together.
How many Peers do I need to launch?
 Enough that every meeting has depth and the room feels complete. Your Regional Executive Director will guide you on the founding group for your specific Circle.
How long does it take from application to first meeting?
 That depends mostly on how quickly you can bring the founding group together. Some Founders launch in weeks, others take a few months to compose the room properly. The careful ones build stronger Circles.
Can I start a Circle in an industry that already exists elsewhere?
 Yes. An Industry Circle in one city is entirely separate from the same industry in another.
What if I want to start a Circle for a purpose that does not exist yet?
 That is exactly how new Purpose Circles begin. If enough entrepreneurs share the ambition, it is worth building.
Can I found a Circle alongside running my business?
 Yes — every Circle Founder is a working business owner. The role is designed around that reality.
What happens after the first year?
 A Circle Director typically takes on the day-to-day rhythm while you continue as Founder. Many Founders go on to build a second Circle or move into wider leadership.

There is a room your city needs and does not have.
You already know who should be in it.
Build Your Business. Build Your Relationships. Build Your Circle.

 This community is built for a particular kind of entrepreneur.

 Not the largest business in the room. The one most willing to help the others in it.
[ Download Unity App ] [ Apply for Membership ]
________________


H2: What we actually look for
Revenue is not the qualification here. Neither is the age of your business, the size of your team, or the industry you operate in.
What matters is character.
A Circle works because of who is in the room, and one wrong seat costs more than three empty ones. So the question a Circle Director is really asking is not how big is your business — it is what kind of person are you when nobody is keeping score.
Six things tell us most of what we need to know.
________________


H2: You belong here if
You already give without being asked
Look at the last year. How many introductions did you make for someone who could not do anything for you in return? How many times did you share something that took you years to learn? If giving is already how you operate, this community will feel like home immediately.
You can be corrected by an equal
Someone in your Circle will eventually tell you that you are wrong about something in your own business. The Peers who grow fastest here are the ones who hear that properly. Those who defend first learn slowest.
You are willing to be honest about the hard parts
The difficult quarter. The partner who has become a problem. The decision you are not sure about. A Circle only produces value when people speak truthfully, and that begins with your willingness to go first.
You think in decades, not quarters
The relationships here compound. A Peer you help this year may open a market for you in the fourth. If you are looking for something that pays back in ninety days, this will frustrate you.
You take responsibility for outcomes
Not for luck, not for markets, but for what you do about them. Entrepreneurs who explain their situation through circumstances rarely fit here, because the room is full of people who changed theirs.
You want to build something beyond your own business
Most Peers arrive for their business. The ones who stay for decades discover they wanted to build something larger, and this is where they found the room to do it.
________________


H2: This is probably not for you if
We would rather say this clearly than let someone discover it after joining.
You are here primarily to sell. A Circle is not a room of prospects. Peers recognise a seller within two meetings, and that person's business never recovers its reputation in the community.
You want the network without the contribution. Access here is earned through what you give. A Peer who takes without giving will find that doors quietly stop opening, and nobody will explain why.
You cannot commit to consistent attendance. Trust is built by the same people meeting the same people. Irregular attendance does not slow that down — it prevents it.
You need to be the most important person in the room. Every Peer here has built something. Nobody gets more time or deference because their business is larger.
You are looking for immediate returns. Some Peers see meaningful business in their first quarter. Many do not. The ones who join expecting month-one results usually leave before the relationships mature.
None of this makes someone a poor entrepreneur. It simply makes this the wrong community for them, and that is worth knowing early.
________________


H2: What we do not require
A minimum revenue. A first-generation entrepreneur three years in can contribute as much as someone running a two-hundred-crore business — sometimes more, because they are closer to the problems others in the room are facing.
A particular industry. Nineteen Circles across industries and ambitions. If you run a real business, there is a room for you.
An existing network. Many Peers join precisely because they do not have one. That is what this community builds.
A title or a degree. Nobody here is measured by either.
English fluency. Peers Global operates across languages. Nobody should be excluded from a room because of the school they attended.
________________


H2: The honest test
If you are unsure, ask yourself one question.
When someone in your industry succeeds, what is your first reaction?
If it is genuine interest — how did they do that, who helped them, what could I learn — you will do well here.
If it is comparison or resentment, this community will not work for you, and the room will sense it long before you do.
That is the whole filter. Everything else is detail.
________________


H2: We decline applications
Not many, but enough that acceptance means something.
The most common reason is not the quality of the business. It is a mismatch of intent — someone looking for a sales channel rather than a community, or someone who cannot commit the time the room requires.
When we decline, we say so directly and explain why. Occasionally we suggest applying again later, when the timing is better for their business.
A Circle Director would rather have an honest conversation now than an unhappy Peer in six months, and an empty seat is always better than the wrong one.
[ See Criteria & Process ]
________________


H2: Still unsure
That is reasonable, and there is an easy way to find out.
Download the Unity App and see the community from the inside. Look at the Peers, the industries, the cities and how collaboration actually works here. Take as long as you want.
Then visit a Circle. Watch a full meeting. See the room. You will know within an hour whether these are your people.
Most entrepreneurs know before the meeting ends.
[ Download on App Store ] [ Get it on Google Play ]
________________

Build Your Business. Build Your Relationships. Build Your Circle.

Word count: ~1,050 · Page type: Static · Phase: 1
What this page is for
This is a filter, not a sales page. It should reduce your application volume and increase your application quality.
That is not a cost. Every wrong Peer in a Circle damages the experience of everyone else in that room, and Circle Directors spend more time managing one poor fit than recruiting three good ones. A page that turns the wrong people away before they apply saves the community more than it costs in leads.
It also works as persuasion for exactly the right reader. An accomplished entrepreneur who sees a community stating clearly who it declines takes that community more seriously than one that welcomes everyone.
Content ownership
Concept
	Owner page
	Treatment here
	The persuasion case for joining
	/membership
	Not repeated
	The formal application bar and steps
	/membership/criteria
	Linked only
	The Peers Code — behavioural rules
	/culture-and-code
	Not restated
	The value system — shared beliefs
	/membership
	Not restated
	Pricing and tiers
	/membership/tiers
	Not mentioned
	The critical distinction with /membership/criteria: this page is about the person. That page is about the process — business stage, seat availability, references, decision timeline, what happens after you apply. One asks are you this kind of entrepreneur. The other answers what is the formal bar and what happens next. No sentence should appear on both.
The unique material here is the six belonging traits, the five disqualifiers, what is not required, and the honest test.
SEO structure
* One H1, seven unique H2s
* Primary keyword who can join business community in H1 area, meta title, meta description
* The You belong here if section is a strong featured-snippet candidate — keep the six traits clearly headed
* No FAQ schema here; it sits on /membership/faq and /membership/criteria
* This page will attract long-tail searches like is business networking worth it and how to choose a business community — real acquisition traffic from people in evaluation mode
Design direction
Text-led, minimal imagery. This page works through directness, and photography dilutes it. Wide margins, generous type, plenty of space.
This is probably not for you if should be visually equal in weight to the belonging section, not tucked away. Its prominence is the point — it signals confidence.
The honest test deserves a full-width block, the question set large in navy and gold. It is the most memorable moment on the page and the line people will quote back.
What we do not require should feel warm. It is the section that gives a smaller or younger entrepreneur permission to apply, and it directly reflects the founder's own story.
Note on the language line
English fluency is included deliberately. It connects to the founder's own experience and is a genuine barrier that keeps capable entrepreneurs out of business communities in India. Confirm it is operationally true — that Circles genuinely run in regional languages where needed — before publishing it.
Developer instructions
App download strip before the closing CTA.
Track exits from this page. A high exit rate here is a success signal, not a failure — it means the filter is working. Measure application quality after this page goes live, not application volume.

H2: What no subscription buys

PRODUCTION NOTES
The two-part model, resolved


	Peers Global subscription
	Circle Experience Fee
	What it buys
	The platform and the community
	A seat in a specific room
	Amount
	₹18,000 / ₹1,00,000
	Varies by Circle and city
	Includes
	App, directory, connections, recognition, Marketplace, event eligibility
	12 meetings, 60+ roundtables, masterclasses, category locked
	Approval
	None — open to qualifying entrepreneurs
	Circle Director + Membership Experience Committee
	Timing
	Immediate
	Usually within a week
	Pages that must now be corrected: /membership and /membership/benefits both currently list twelve meetings and 60+ roundtables as subscription inclusions. Say the word and I'll rewrite both against this model.
The commercial risk worth naming
At ₹18,000 for platform access alone, the subscription has to feel worth it on its own. A prospect who pays and then discovers the meetings cost more will feel misled, however clearly it was stated.
Two things protect you:
Make the platform genuinely valuable — the directory, connections and Marketplace have to deliver on their own for someone who never joins a Circle.
Show the Circle Experience Fee range publicly. Even "Circle Experience Fees typically range from ₹X to ₹Y depending on city" removes the fear of an unknown second bill. Hidden second costs are the fastest way to lose a careful buyer. Send me the range and I'll add it.
On "freedom, not outcome"
Writing Charter honestly as freedom rather than guaranteed benefits is the right call and unusually confident. It filters for entrepreneurs who will actually use it, and it protects you from a Charter Peer who paid ₹1 lakh and expected coverage to arrive automatically.
Hold this line in sales conversations too. If a Circle Director promises media coverage to close a Charter subscription, the page becomes a liability.
Remaining open
Circle Experience Fee range. The single most important number still missing.
Legal. Non-refundable terms and Code-violation cancellation both need to be explicit in the Membership Agreement, not only on the website. Get your lawyer to draft the clauses.

HERO
H1: Criteria & Process
Subline:
 Two steps. Joining Peers Global, and joining a Circle.
Supporting line:
 The first is open to every entrepreneur who meets the criteria. The second is by approval.
[ Download Unity App ] [ Apply for Membership ]
________________


H2: How joining works
Step one — you join Peers Global.
Your annual subscription makes you part of the Peers Global family. It gives you the platform, the Unity App, the community, the recognition system and access to everything the ecosystem offers.
This is open to every entrepreneur who meets the criteria below.
Step two — you join a Circle.
A Circle is a different commitment. It is a specific room, with a limited number of seats, where one business holds each category. Joining one requires a request, an approval and a separate Circle Experience Fee.
Why the two are separate: the platform is designed to include every serious entrepreneur. A Circle is designed to protect the people already in it. Both matter, and they work differently on purpose.
________________


H2: PART ONE — Joining Peers Global
The criteria
Open to any entrepreneur who meets these five.
01 · You own or lead a real business
 Founder, co-founder, partner, director or CEO. You make decisions that carry consequences.
No minimum revenue. No minimum age of business.
02 · You are here to contribute
 What you intend to give matters more than what you hope to receive. This is the criterion that shapes everything else.
03 · You accept the Peers Code
 Give first. Show up. Tell the truth. Protect the room. Respect every Peer. Carry the culture.
04 · You represent your business honestly
 What you tell the community about your business, your role and your work must be true.
05 · You conduct yourself professionally
 Inside the app, in meetings, and anywhere you represent Peers Global.
[ Read the Peers Code ]
What we do not require
A minimum turnover — contribution is not a function of revenue
A particular industry — nineteen Circles across sectors and ambitions
An existing network — many Peers join precisely because they lack one
A degree or title — neither measures anything here
English fluency — the community operates across languages [CONFIRM]
 An invitation — you can subscribe directly
The process
1. Explore. Download the Unity App and see the community from the inside.
2. Subscribe. Choose your membership and complete your subscription.
3. You are a Peer. Full access to the platform, the community and the Unity App.
Timeline: same day
[ See Membership Options ]
________________


H2: PART TWO — Joining a Circle
Why this step is separate
A Circle holds one business per category. When you take a seat, that category closes to everyone else in that room.
That is a significant thing to hand to someone. It is why every Circle request is reviewed rather than accepted automatically — the entrepreneurs already in that room are trusting the process to protect what they have built.
The approval process
Step 1 · You raise a request
From inside the Unity App or through the website, you request to join a specific Circle. You state your business, your category and why that Circle.
Timeline: a few minutes
________________


Step 2 · Your request is reviewed
The Circle Director, the Circle Chairs and the Membership Committee review it together.
What they assess:
* Whether your business category is genuinely available in that Circle
* Whether your business complements rather than competes with those already seated
* Whether you can commit to the meeting rhythm
* What you bring to that specific room
This is a considered decision made by people who know the Circle, not an automated check.
Timeline: [CONFIRM — how many days?]
________________


Step 3 · You visit the Circle
Where possible, you attend a meeting as a guest before the seat is confirmed. You see the room, and the room sees you.
You will not be asked to present, and you will not be sold to.
Timeline: the Circle's next meeting date
[ See What Happens in a Meeting ]
________________


Step 4 · Your request is approved
You are informed of the decision directly, either way. If your category is unavailable or the fit is not right, you are told why and shown the alternatives.
Timeline: [CONFIRM]
________________


Step 5 · You pay the Circle Experience Fee
Once approved, you pay the Circle Experience Fee for that Circle. This covers the hospitality and experience of the twelve meetings across your year.
Timeline: on approval
[CONFIRM: fee amount, whether it varies by Circle or city, what it covers, whether it is annual]
________________


Step 6 · Your category is locked
On payment, your seat is confirmed and your business category is locked in that Circle for your membership year. Nobody else in that room can hold it.
You are added to the Circle in Unity, introduced to the Peers, and your onboarding begins.
Timeline: immediate
________________


What happens next
Your first ninety days are structured rather than improvised. You are introduced to the Circle, connected to a Peer who guides you through the early months, and helped to make your first contribution early.
The Peers who start giving in their first month grow fastest here.
[ Apply Now ]
________________


H2: Joining more than one Circle
Many Peers hold a seat in an Industry Circle and a seat in a Purpose Circle — depth in their sector, perspective from outside it.
Each Circle follows the same process. A separate request, a separate approval, and a separate Circle Experience Fee.
[CONFIRM: any limit on the number of Circles a Peer may join]
________________


H2: If your category is taken
We will tell you immediately rather than leaving you waiting.
Three real options:
Another Circle. Your category may be open in a different Circle in your city.
The waiting list. You will be told honestly how long that seat is likely to take, and if it is unlikely to open this year we will say so.
Found a Circle. If your industry or ambition has no room in your city, that is an opening rather than a closed door.
Your Peers Global subscription remains fully active throughout. You retain the app, the community and everything else while you wait.
[ Start a Circle ]
________________


H2: Common questions
Can I join Peers Global without joining a Circle?
 Yes. Your subscription gives you the platform, the app and the community. A Circle is an additional step you take when the right room is available.
Is the Circle Experience Fee separate from my subscription?
 Yes. Your subscription is for Peers Global. The Circle Experience Fee covers the hospitality and experience of that Circle's twelve meetings.
What if my Circle request is declined?
 You are told why, and shown the alternatives. Your subscription is unaffected.
Can I request a specific Circle?
 Yes. Name it when you raise your request.
What if I am not sure which Circle suits me?
 Raise it with a Circle Director. That conversation is exactly what they are for.
How long does Circle approval take?
 [CONFIRM]
Do I pay the Circle Experience Fee before or after approval?
 After. You are never asked to pay for a seat that has not been confirmed.

PRODUCTION NOTES
Word count: ~1,250 · Type: Static · Phase: 1
This changes three other pages
The two-part model is a structural clarification, and pages already written now contradict it.
/membership/tiers — must be corrected. It currently lists "a seat in a Circle" inside the ₹18,000 subscription. That is now wrong. The subscription buys the platform; the Circle is separate and separately paid.
/membership and /membership/benefits — must be corrected. Both currently list twelve meetings and 60+ roundtables as subscription inclusions. Those come with a Circle, not with the subscription.
Recommended fix across all three: present subscription inclusions and Circle inclusions as two clearly separated groups. Say I should, and I will rewrite those three pages against the corrected model in one pass.
Why this model is stronger
A larger front door. Any qualifying entrepreneur can join, download the app and become part of the community immediately. That serves the app-first strategy directly — no gatekeeping at the point of first commitment.
A protected inner room. Circles stay selective because the approval sits where selectivity actually matters.
No dead ends. An entrepreneur whose category is taken stays a Peer with full platform access instead of being turned away. You keep the relationship and the revenue while the seat opens.
Honest pricing. ₹18,000 for a platform reads differently from ₹18,000 for twelve meetings. Separating the two makes both numbers defensible.
What I need to complete this page
1. Circle Experience Fee — amount, whether it varies by Circle or city, what it covers, annual or otherwise
2. Approval timeline — how many days from request to decision
3. Circle limit — maximum number of Circles a Peer may hold
4. Membership Committee — is this the right name, and does it sit at Circle, city or national level?
SEO structure
* One H1, seven H2s, sub-steps as H3
* HowTo schema on both processes — strong candidate for how to join a business circle
* FAQPage schema for the seven questions
* The two-part structure is genuinely distinctive and will read as unusually transparent to anyone comparing communities
Design direction
Build this as two clearly separated halves, visually distinct. Part One in light treatment, Part Two in navy. The reader should see immediately that these are two different things.
Timelines beside every step. Publishing them is a confidence signal that costs nothing and builds real trust.
Step 6 — your category is locked — deserves the strongest visual moment. It is the payoff of the entire process and the thing a prospect actually wants.
Developer instructions
Circle requests must be a workflow in the system, not an email. Request → Director review → Chairs → Committee → approval → payment link → seat locked → added to Circle in Unity.
Category locking must be enforced at database level. Two Peers holding the same category in one Circle is the single failure that would damage trust most.
Circle Experience Fee must be a separate transaction from subscription, with its own invoice, so a member's records are unambiguous.
________________

H1: Member FAQ
Subline:
 Straight answers, including to the questions most communities avoid.
