H2: 1 · Before you join
Can I try Peers Global before subscribing?
 Download the Unity App and look around. You can see the community, understand how collaboration works here and get a real sense of the culture before committing to anything.
Do I need an invitation?
 No. You can subscribe directly if you meet the criteria.
Do I need a business of a certain size?
 No. There is no minimum revenue and no minimum age of business. What matters is that you own or lead a real business and that you intend to contribute.
Is Peers Global only for entrepreneurs in India?
 No. Peers Global is a global community. It was founded in India, and India remains its largest and fastest-growing base, but the platform, the Circles and the collaboration model were built from the start for entrepreneurs anywhere in the world.
The Unity App connects Peers across countries from day one. Circles like Import, Export & Global Trade and Global Expansion exist specifically for entrepreneurs building across borders.
Designed in Bharat. Built for the world.
Can I join from outside India?
 Yes. Your subscription gives you the platform, the Unity App and the full community wherever you are based.
Are there Circles in my country?
 The community is expanding country by country. If there is no Circle where you are yet, your subscription still gives you full platform access — and founding the first Circle in a new country is one of the most significant contributions anyone can make here.
How is this different from what I already do?
 You will know within one Circle meeting. Attend as a guest and decide from what you see.
________________


H2: 2 · Who the membership belongs to
Is this a business membership or an individual membership?
 Individual. Always.
Your membership belongs to you, not to your company. It follows the person, not the letterhead.
Peers Global exists to grow entrepreneurs, not to list companies. The relationships you build here are yours. The trust you earn is yours. Contribution is recorded against you personally, and the standing you build is your own.
A business does not learn, contribute or lead. A person does.
Can two partners from the same business join?
 Yes. Both are welcome, and both join in their own right.
Each partner holds their own membership, builds their own relationships, earns their own Life Impact Score and develops as their own kind of leader. [CONFIRM: can both hold seats in the same Circle, given one seat per category?]
Can my membership be transferred to someone else?
 No. Membership is individual and cannot be transferred to another person, including a business partner, a family member or a successor. It also cannot be sold with a business.
________________


H2: 3 · Subscription and payment
Is the Unity App free?
 Yes. The app is free to download and use. A subscription unlocks the full community, the premium features and everything the platform is built to give you.
What does my subscription include?
 Your subscription makes you part of Peers Global — the platform, the Unity App, the community and the recognition system. Joining a Circle is a separate step with its own approval and its own subscription.
Is there a joining fee?
 Yes. A one-time joining fee applies in addition to your annual subscription.
Can I pay in instalments?
 In some countries, yes — where our payment gateway supports it. Options vary by country and are shown at checkout for your location.
Is GST included in the price?
 No. Prices are exclusive of tax. GST applies in India, and equivalent taxes apply in other countries according to local law. The final amount including tax is shown before you pay.
Can I move from Peer to Charter Peer?
 Charter is an upgrade, not a transfer. You subscribe to Charter Peer alongside your existing Peer subscription, which continues unaffected. The Charter subscription is charged in full, with no deduction against your existing subscription.
What is your refund policy?
There are no refunds.
Membership here is not a service delivered on a schedule. It is access to a community, and the value arrives differently for every Peer. Some receive a business-changing introduction in their first month. For others the relationships take a year to mature, and then produce something far larger.
Because value cannot be measured by time elapsed, it cannot be refunded by time remaining.
This is why we encourage every entrepreneur to download the app, explore the community and visit a Circle before subscribing. Decide before you pay, not after.
[ Read Full Membership Terms ]
________________


H2: 4 · Subscription dates and renewal
When does my membership year start?
 Your Peers Global subscription begins the day you subscribe and runs for twelve months.
Your Circle subscription is separate and begins the day you join that Circle.
Do the two dates have to match?
 No, and understanding this matters.
If you subscribe to Peers Global in January and join a Circle in April, your platform subscription expires the following January while your Circle subscription runs to the following April.
Your platform subscription must remain active for your Circle membership to continue. In this example you would renew Peers Global in January so your Circle membership continues uninterrupted. If the platform subscription lapses, the Circle membership is cancelled with it.
Both dates are always visible in the Unity App.
How do I renew my Peers Global subscription?
 Directly in the Unity App. You will receive notifications in your profile well before your renewal date.
How do I renew my Circle subscription?
 Circle renewal is subject to Circle approval. Your Circle Director and the Circle reconfirm your seat, and you renew from there.
________________


H2: 5 · Circles
Can I join Peers Global without joining a Circle?
 Yes. Many Peers start on the platform, spend time in the community, and request a Circle when the right room is available.
What is a primary Circle?
 The first Circle you join becomes your primary Circle, and it must be an Industry Circle.
Your primary Circle is your home in this community. It appears on your profile across the Unity App, and it is the membership everything else is built on.
Your primary Circle must be renewed for you to remain in any other Circle. Relationships need years to produce their full value, and a community where entrepreneurs move rooms every twelve months never builds the depth that makes any of it work.
How many Circles can I join?
 Two, as standard. One Industry Circle — always your primary — and one Purpose Circle.
An Industry Circle is mandatory. Beyond those two, you may join additional Purpose Circles if you have multiple goals, subject to Circle Director approval.
Each Circle requires its own request, its own approval and its own subscription.
Can I change Circles?
 No. Your primary Circle is a long-term commitment and it stays with you.
It changes only if the Circle itself does not renew your membership. In that case your second Circle becomes your primary Circle, or you select which of your Circles becomes primary.
What if my business category is already taken?
 You will be told immediately, along with the alternatives — another Circle where the category is open, the waiting list with an honest timeline, or founding a new Circle. Your Peers Global subscription remains fully active either way.
What happens if my Circle becomes inactive?
Circles do not close.
A Circle that loses momentum is rebuilt by the Peers inside it, with support from the Circle Director and the wider community. Members are not transferred out, and a Circle is not abandoned because a difficult period arrived.
That is the commitment made to everyone who takes a seat.
What if my Circle is not delivering value?
 Speak to your Circle Director. Most often the issue is fit. Occasionally the issue is that a Peer has not yet started giving, which is the honest conversation a good Director will have with you.
Are meetings online or in person?
 Both formats exist across the community. Your Circle Director will confirm the format for your Circle.
________________


H2: 6 · Time and commitment
How much time does this actually take?
 Twelve meetings a year per Circle, plus one-to-one conversations between them. Be honest with yourself about it — this is the commitment most people underestimate.
What if I have to miss a meeting?
 Tell your Circle Director in advance. Occasional absence is understood. Sustained absence prevents trust from forming, and a Circle notices.
Can I send someone from my team in my place?
 Yes. Substitution is allowed. Send someone who can represent your business properly, and let your Circle Director know in advance.
Can I bring team members to a meeting?
 Yes. If a masterclass or a meeting theme would benefit your team, they can attend as visitors on payment of the visitor fee. Speak to your Circle Director.
What if I travel frequently?
 Speak to a Circle Director before joining. Substitution helps, and a Charter Peer's cross-city access changes the picture significantly.
Do I have to bring referrals every month?
 No. Contribution takes ten forms and referrals are only one. Knowledge, introductions, problem solving and support all count equally.
________________


H2: 7 · The Unity App
Do I need the app to be a member?
 Technically no. Practically yes. The community runs on it, contributions are logged in it, and Peers who do not use it get considerably less from their membership.
Is my information visible to everyone?
 No. Your profile is private by default.
Your full profile becomes visible to a Peer once you connect with them. Until then your details remain confidential. Nothing on your profile is public.
Can I see other Peers' contact details?
 As a Peer, you discover Peers across the community and send a connection request. Details are exchanged once you connect. A Charter Peer can view full profiles and contact details directly across the community.
________________


H2: 8 · Life Impact, Standing and Peers Coin
How is my Life Impact Score calculated?
 Through contributions across the ten Ways of Collaboration, each confirmed by the Peer who received the value. You cannot award yourself impact.
Can I buy Peers Coin?
 No. Every coin was earned by helping another entrepreneur.
Where can I spend Peers Coin?
 In the Peers Global Marketplace — entrepreneurial growth tools, learning programmes, business resources, community experiences and merchandise.
Does my membership level affect my standing?
 No. Standing comes from contribution alone. A Peer who gives generously carries more weight in any room than a Charter Peer who does not.
What happens to my Life Impact Score if I leave?
 It expires. Your Life Impact Score, Peer Standing and Peers Coin are tied to active membership, and all three end when your subscription does.
Standing reflects an active, contributing member of this community. It is not a permanent credential.
________________


H2: 9 · Leadership
Can any Peer become a leader?
 Yes. Circle Founder, Circle Director, Industry Director and Regional Executive Director are open to every Peer, and every one of them follows contribution.
Does Charter membership guarantee a leadership role?
 No. Charter Peers are approached first when a role opens, but every role is earned through contribution and every Peer is eligible.
How long before I could lead something?
 There is no fixed period. It depends on what you contribute and how the community comes to know you.
What does a leadership role require in time?
 Real, recurring time. This is discussed openly with any candidate before a role is offered.
________________


H2: 10 · Leaving and returning
Can I leave before my year ends?
 Yes, at any time. Your subscription cancels immediately on departure, and no refund applies for the remaining period.
What if I violate the Peers Code?
 Your membership is cancelled and your subscription ends immediately.
Breaking the confidence of a Circle, using the room to sell, misrepresenting Peers Global, or treating a Peer with disrespect are the situations that lead here. They are uncommon, and they are handled directly by Circle Directors — but they are handled, because the honesty inside a Circle depends on everyone having agreed to the same standard.
[ Read the Peers Code ]
What happens if I do not renew?
 Your membership simply ends at the close of your year. Access to the platform, the app and your Circle ends, and your Life Impact Score, Peer Standing and Peers Coin expire with it.
There is no penalty for not renewing. Circumstances change, and some years are not the right year for this.
Can I rejoin later?
 Yes, at any time. You apply as a new member. Your Circle request goes through the standard approval process, your subscription starts fresh, and your Life Impact Score begins again from zero.
Why does my record start from zero?
 Because standing here reflects who you are in this community now, not who you were three years ago.
A Peer's record is a live picture of what they are contributing. Rejoining means becoming an active part of the room again — and the record rebuilds quickly for anyone who gives.
Is my seat held for me?
 No. When you leave, your business category becomes available in that Circle and another entrepreneur may take it. If that seat is filled when you return, you will be shown the alternatives.
Subscription and payment
Is there a joining fee?
 No. There is no joining or admission fee. You pay your annual Peers Global subscription, and a Circle Experience Fee if and when you join a Circle.
What does my ₹18,000 subscription include?
 Your Peers Global subscription is a platform subscription.
It gives you the Unity App, access to the community, the Peer directory, the ability to connect with entrepreneurs across cities and countries, the recognition system, and everything the platform is built to give you.
It does not include Circle membership. Circle meetings, Collaboration Roundtables and Impact Mentor Masterclasses come with a Circle, which is a separate step with its own approval and its own fee.
What is the Circle Experience Fee?
 A separate fee paid when you join a Circle, covering the experience and hospitality of that Circle's twelve meetings across your year.
It varies by Circle and by city. Each Circle sets its own fee based on its city, its format and its meeting experience. You are told the exact amount before you pay, once your Circle request is approved.
Can I pay in instalments?
 In some countries, yes — where our payment gateway supports it. Options vary by country and are shown at checkout for your location.
Is GST included?
 No. Prices are exclusive of tax. GST applies in India, and equivalent taxes apply elsewhere according to local law. The final amount including tax is shown before you pay.
What is your refund policy?
All fees are non-refundable. The Peers Global subscription, the Circle Experience Fee and any event fee.
Membership here is not a service delivered on a schedule. It is access to a community, and value arrives differently for every Peer. Some receive a business-changing introduction in their first month. For others the relationships take a year to mature, and then produce something far larger.
Because value cannot be measured by time elapsed, it cannot be refunded by time remaining.
This is why we encourage every entrepreneur to download the app, explore the community and visit an event before subscribing. Decide before you pay.
[ Read Full Membership Terms ]
________________


Who the membership belongs to
Can two partners from the same business join?
 Yes. Both are welcome, and both join in their own right.
Both partners can also hold seats in the same Circle, since they represent the same business. Each holds their own membership, builds their own relationships, earns their own Life Impact Score and develops as their own kind of leader.
Is this a business membership or an individual membership?
 Individual. Always.
Your membership belongs to you, not to your company. Peers Global exists to grow entrepreneurs, not to list companies. The relationships you build are yours, the trust you earn is yours, and contribution is recorded against you personally.
A business does not learn, contribute or lead. A person does.
________________


Circles
How long does Circle approval take?
 Usually within a week.
Your request is reviewed by the Circle Director and the Circle's Membership Experience Committee. Timing depends on the Circle's meeting rhythm and its Director's availability, and you will be told where your request stands.
Who approves my Circle request?
 The Circle Director together with the Membership Experience Committee for that Circle. Every Circle has its own committee, made up of Peers who know that room.
________________


Visiting and events
How do I visit a Circle or attend an event?
 Through the Unity App. Open Events, choose what you want to attend, and register there. Every event shows its own fee and details.
Can I bring team members to a meeting?
 Yes, as registered visitors. They register through the Unity App like any other attendee, and the applicable event fee applies.
________________


Charter Peer
How many meetings can a Charter Peer attend outside their own Circle?
 Twelve cross-region meetings a year, in addition to your own Circle meetings.
What is the Charter room?
 A separate Circle for Charter Peers, where entrepreneurs building at national and international scale discuss business, collaboration and the direction of the community.
Do Charter Peers get free event access?
 No. Every event carries a fee, for Charter Peers and Peers alike. Charter Peers may receive preferential rates on some events, and Charter-only events such as annual retreats are organised by the District Director and regional team.
Are Charter media benefits guaranteed?
 No. Charter Peers receive first priority for media coverage, features and speaking opportunities. Priority is not a guarantee, and nothing is committed in advance.
What does Charter participation in the community's direction involve?
Charter gives you freedom, not an outcome.
Freedom to walk into any Circle in any city. Freedom to reach any Peer directly. Freedom to be considered first when a leadership role opens or a stage needs a speaker.
What you do with that freedom is entirely yours. A Charter Peer who uses it builds relationships across the country in a year. One who does not will find that nothing arrives on its own.
Charter opens doors. It does not walk through them for you.

QR code on desktop. A desktop visitor cannot tap a store button. Without a QR bridge, every desktop visit to this page is lost.
Tracking
Page views → app downloads → subscriptions. That funnel is now your entire acquisition measurement.

PAGE: THE LEADERSHIP LADDER
PAGE: THE LEADERSHIP LADDER
URL: /leadership
 Menu: Leadership → The Leadership Ladder
Meta title: Leadership at Peers Global | Built and Led by Entrepreneurs
Meta description: Leadership at Peers Global is earned through contribution. Circle Founder, Circle Director, Industry Director, Regional Executive Director — and who you become in each.
Primary keyword: business community leadership roles
Secondary keywords: become a circle founder, entrepreneur leadership development India, business community director
Primary CTA: Download Unity App
Secondary CTA: Apply to Lead
________________


HERO
H1: Leadership
Subline:
 Influence is not given. It is built, one act of service at a time.
Supporting line:
 Every leadership role at Peers Global is held by a working business owner, and every one of them is earned.
[ Download Unity App ] [ Apply to Lead ]
________________


H2: Why entrepreneurs take these roles
Not for a title. Nobody outside this community will be impressed by one.
They take them because something changes in a person who becomes responsible for others.
You run a business. You lead people who report to you, who depend on you, who follow because of the position you hold. That is management, and you are probably good at it.
Leading a room of accomplished entrepreneurs is a different thing entirely.
Nobody in that room has to listen to you. They have their own businesses, their own success, their own opinions. You hold no authority over them. If you want them to follow a standard, show up consistently, or change how they behave, you have to earn it every single month.
That is influence. And it can only be built one way — by serving people who owe you nothing.
The entrepreneurs who take these roles come out different at the other end. More patient. Better at listening. Harder to rattle. Capable of holding a difficult conversation without damaging a relationship.
They become the kind of person other people follow.
________________


H2: What leadership here actually develops
Influence without authority. The rarest leadership skill there is, and the only one that transfers everywhere. Learn it here, and you take it back into your own company, your family and every room you enter.
The capacity for difficult conversations. Telling a respected entrepreneur that their behaviour is affecting the room. Doing it kindly, directly, and in a way that keeps the relationship. Most business owners avoid this their whole lives.
Real listening. You cannot lead a room of thirty entrepreneurs by talking. You lead by understanding what each of them needs, often before they say it.
Patience with people. A Circle grows at the speed of its slowest relationship. Learning to work with that rather than against it changes how you run your own team.
Judgement about people. Choosing who belongs in a room, and having the strength to say no. This one skill will serve your own hiring for the rest of your career.
A standing you did not have before. Not the title. The reputation that follows someone who built something for others and asked for nothing first.
________________


H2: Leadership follows contribution
Nobody arrives here as a Director.
Every leadership role is held by someone who first sat in a Circle as a Peer, gave consistently, and earned the trust of the entrepreneurs around them.
Leadership cannot be purchased at any membership level. Charter Peers are approached first when a role opens, but the role itself is earned through what you have given — recorded in your Life Impact Score, confirmed by the Peers you helped, and visible in your standing across the community.
The Peers who give the most are the Peers who lead.
________________


H2: THE ROLES
Each one asks something different of you, and each one changes you differently.
________________


Circle Founder
You build the room that does not exist yet.
Somewhere in your city there are twenty entrepreneurs who would change each other's businesses if they were ever in the same room. A Founder is the person who puts them there.
You define what the Circle is for, bring the founding Peers, set the culture from the first meeting, and carry the first year.
Who you become:
 The person who built something. Not for a client, not for revenue — for a group of people who needed a room and did not have one. You will recruit entrepreneurs who owe you nothing, hold a standard nobody is forcing them to keep, and watch relationships form that would never have existed without you.
The hardest part: the first three meetings, when there is no momentum, no proof, and only your own conviction holding it together.
[ Read the Circle Founder Role ]
________________


Circle Director
You hold the standard.
The rhythm, the agenda, the quality of participation, the welcome given to every new Peer, and the conversation nobody else wants to have.
Who you become:
 The person a room relies on. You will learn to correct someone you respect without losing them. To notice the Peer who has gone quiet and reach out before they drift. To keep a room disciplined without making it cold.
When a Circle is healthy, there is almost always a Director doing quiet, consistent work behind it — and almost nobody sees that work.
The hardest part: leading people who are more successful than you, and doing it without either deference or ego.
[ Read the Circle Director Role ]
________________


Industry Director
You carry a sector across the community.
You hold one industry across every Circle and every city — connecting Peers in the same sector who would never otherwise meet, building depth, and opening opportunities no single Circle could produce.
Who you become:
 A recognised voice in your industry, not because you claimed it but because you spent years connecting the people in it. Entrepreneurs across the country begin to know your name, and your understanding of your own sector deepens from seeing it from thirty angles instead of one.
The hardest part: thinking about an entire industry when you also have a business inside it to run.
[ Read the Industry Director Role ]
________________


Regional Executive Director
You carry a territory.
Supporting Circles and cities across a region, helping new Circles launch, holding consistency of culture across every room, and representing Peers Global in that part of the world.
Who you become:
 Someone who thinks in ecosystems. You stop measuring a good month by your own business and start measuring it by how many entrepreneurs across your region had a better one. That shift is permanent, and it changes how you see everything afterwards.
The hardest part: you are responsible for rooms you are not in, led by people you cannot control, in cities you cannot visit every month.
[ Read the Regional Executive Director Role ]
________________


Ambassador
You carry the community outward.
Representing Peers Global in your industry, your city and your network. Introducing entrepreneurs who genuinely belong here. Speaking for what this community stands for in rooms where it is not yet known.
Who you become:
 The person whose recommendation carries weight. You are trusted with the reputation of a community, which means every introduction you make is a statement about your own judgement.
The hardest part: saying no to someone who wants in and should not be here.
________________


Global Advisor
You guide the whole.
Senior entrepreneurs who counsel the community itself. Not running a Circle, but bringing decades of experience to decisions about where Peers Global goes and what it must never compromise as it grows.
Who you become:
 Someone whose experience outlives their own company. Most business knowledge dies with the business that produced it. This is where yours keeps working.
The hardest part: guiding without controlling, and letting people make mistakes you can see coming.
________________


H2: What every leader shares
They give more than they take. Every leader here was a contributor long before they were anything else.
They hold the standard when it is uncomfortable. Anyone can enforce a rule when it is easy. Leadership here is what happens when the person who needs correcting is someone you like.
They build things that outlast them. A Founder's objective is a Circle that no longer depends on them. That is true at every level.
They run their own business. Nobody leads full time here, and every role is designed around that reality.
________________


H2: What it will cost you
Real, recurring time. Every month, including the months when it is inconvenient.
Difficult conversations. With Peers you like, about behaviour that needs to change.
Consistency when nobody is watching. The work that holds a Circle together is mostly invisible. It is noticed only when it stops.
Patience. A Circle takes a year to find its rhythm. Some months you will do everything right and see nothing move.
This is not a title. Anyone considering a role primarily for the recognition should not take one. The room will know within a quarter, and so will you.
Every candidate discusses the real commitment openly before any role is offered.
________________


H2: The influence you build here
Influence is the ability to move people who have no obligation to follow you.
You cannot buy it. You cannot be given it. It accumulates slowly, through consistency, through service, and through people watching how you behave when there is nothing in it for you.
A leadership role at Peers Global is a place to build it deliberately.
And when you have built it here — among entrepreneurs who owe you nothing and chose to follow anyway — you carry it into every room you walk into for the rest of your life.
________________


H2: How to begin
1. Join the community. Download the Unity App and subscribe.
2. Join a Circle. Take your seat and start giving.
3. Contribute consistently. Your record builds itself.
4. Express interest. Tell your Circle Director, or apply directly.
5. Have the conversation. An honest discussion about the role, the time, and whether it fits where your own business currently is.
Many leaders were approached rather than applying. Contribution is visible here, and people notice.
[ Apply to Lead ] [ Start a Circle ]
________________


CLOSING
The community you want to belong to has to be built by someone.
Build Your Business. Build Your Relationships. Build Your Circle.

Both answers resolve cleanly, and they're not contradictory once separated.
Executive Director is the role. Territory is the level. Area ED (within a district), District ED, State ED, Country ED. My original page was right — District ED is one level of it.
Circle Founder builds, Circle Director runs. That's the operational sequence for a Circle. Your brochure's pathway numbering is the individual's growth journey — serve on Powerhouse, run an existing Circle, then go on to found new ones. Both are true and they describe different things.
The site needs both, stated separately.

H2: THE LEADERSHIP PATHWAY
A clear path from member to ecosystem owner.
01 · Member
 Where every journey begins.
02 · Powerhouse
 The leadership team executor. Serving on a committee, holding a role, and learning to lead inside the room.
03 · Circle Director
 Runs the Circle, month on month. Holds the culture, mentors the Chairs, and grows the room.
04 · Circle Founder
 Launches new Circles. Convenes entrepreneurs where no room existed.
05 · Industry Director
 Sector ecosystem owner for the city. Holds one industry across every Circle in it.
06 · Executive Director
 Regional ecosystem builder. Carries a territory — an Area, a District, a State or a Country.
________________


H2: How a Circle is actually led
The pathway above is an individual's journey. Inside any single Circle, the sequence works differently.
The Circle Founder comes first. They convene the room, bring the founding entrepreneurs, set the standard and carry it to launch.
The Circle Director takes over after launch. They run it month on month — the culture, the rhythm, the Chairs, the growth.
Both roles continue. The Founder remains the Founder. The Director holds the day-to-day.
Most entrepreneurs serve as Powerhouse, then direct a Circle, and only then go on to found new ones. By that point they know exactly what a room needs, because they have run one.
________________


H2: THE ROLES
Powerhouse
The leadership team of a Circle.
Three committees — Business Growth, Membership Experience, and Events & Impact — each led by a Chair with three Leaders beneath them. Fourteen roles in every Circle.
Who you become: the person the room relies on for something specific. You learn to hold a responsibility among people who outrank you commercially, and you find out whether leading is something you actually want.
This is where leadership begins here. Almost every Circle Director served on a committee first.
[ See Circle Roles ]

Circle Director
Runs the Circle, month on month.
The culture, the rhythm, the standard, the growth and the health of the room. Chairs the meetings, mentors the three Chairs and nine Leaders, and has the conversation nobody else will have.
Who you become: the person who develops other leaders. You will mentor entrepreneurs who owe you nothing and who will carry what you taught them into rooms you will never sit in.
A Circle becomes what its Director makes it.

Circle starts from Day 1 — first member. Structure begins immediately. Official launch is a celebration, not the beginning.

Industry Director

Regional ecosystem builder.

Area Executive Director — a defined area within a district

Country Executive Director — a nation and the whole ecosystem within it

PRODUCTION NOTES
Word count: ~1,450 · Type: Static · Phase: 1
The reframe
Influence, not income. Becoming, not receiving.
The page now opens with why entrepreneurs take these roles rather than with the roles themselves — and the answer is that leading people who owe you nothing changes the person doing it.
That framing does something practical: it makes leadership feel earned and difficult, which attracts entrepreneurs who want to grow and repels those looking for a title.
Three additions carrying the weight:
"Influence without authority." This is the honest description of what these roles actually require, and it is the leadership skill most business owners never develop because their own company never demands it.
"Who you become" per role. Each role now answers the question a serious candidate is really asking. The Regional ED line — you stop measuring a good month by your own business — is the kind of shift that makes someone want the role for the right reason.
"The hardest part" per role. Naming the difficulty raises the value of the role and filters honestly. A role that sounds easy attracts people who quit when it is not.
No income language, anywhere
Not on this page, not on the five role pages, not in decks that circulate among general members.
This has to hold in conversation too. If a Circle Director recruits a Founder by mentioning revenue, the whole position collapses. Brief every Director on this before recruitment begins.
Compensation is discussed privately, after a candidate has been assessed and is genuinely being considered.
Design direction
Real leaders, named and photographed, in every role section. Name, business, city. This page fails entirely with stock imagery — its job is proving real entrepreneurs hold these roles.
Who you become should be visually distinct in each role block — a tinted panel or gold left border — so a skimming reader gets the emotional argument even if they read nothing else.
The hardest part sits quieter, in smaller type. Its presence matters more than its prominence.
The influence you build here deserves a full-width block with no imagery. It is the emotional close of the page.
SEO structure
* One H1, eight H2s, six H3s for the roles
* Primary keyword in H1 area, meta title, meta description
* This page becomes the internal hub for the leadership section
* Phase 2: each role page targets its own search — become a circle founder, business community director role
Developer instructions
Pull current leaders from the leaders table by role. Where a role has no holder in a region, show the description without a placeholder photograph — an empty frame reads as an empty organisation.

HERO
H1: The Powerhouse
Subline:
 The leadership team of a Circle.
Supporting line:
 Fourteen entrepreneurs hold every Circle together. This is where leadership at Peers Global begins.
[ Download Unity App ] [ Apply to Lead ]
________________


H2: Stage 02 of the pathway
Every leader in this community started here.
The Powerhouse is the leadership team of a Circle — the entrepreneurs who took a role, held a responsibility, and made the room work.
Almost every Circle Director served on a committee first. So did most Circle Founders, Industry Directors and Executive Directors.
It is the most reachable leadership role here, and it is where you find out whether leading is something you actually want.
________________


H2: THE THREE COMMITTEES
Each committee is led by a Chair with three Leaders beneath them.
________________


Business Growth Committee
Responsible for what the Circle produces.
Chair, Business Growth Committee — leads the committee and is accountable for the collaboration the Circle generates
Business Growth Leader — drives referrals, introductions and business flow. Makes sure the Collaboration Roundtables produce named outcomes rather than conversation
Recognition & PR Leader — ensures contribution is recognised in the room, in the community and in the media. Works with VyapaarJagat and the Peers Global media ecosystem
Cross-Border Collaborations Leader (Unity) — connects the Circle beyond its own city, opening collaboration across regions and countries through the Unity App
________________


Membership Experience Committee
Responsible for who is in the room, and how they experience it.
Chair, Membership Experience Committee — leads the committee and reviews every request to join the Circle
Member Success Leader — looks after every Peer's experience. The new member finding their footing, the established one whose contribution has quietened, the entrepreneur going through a difficult year
Peers Board of Advisory Leader — connects Peers to the senior advisors and mentors available across the community
Peers Brand Showcase Leader — runs the Brand Showcase. The rotation, the preparation, and making sure each Peer's four minutes produce actions rather than impressions
________________


Events & Impact Committee
Responsible for what the Circle does beyond the meeting.
Chair, Events & Impact Committee — leads the committee and is accountable for the Circle's events and its impact
Events & Experience Leader — runs the meetings and events. Venue, format, flow, and the experience of everyone in the room
Skill Development Leader — programmes the Impact Mentor Masterclasses. Chooses the subject the room needs and finds the person who has genuinely done it
Partnership & Visitor Leader — manages guests, visitors and partnerships. The first person a visiting entrepreneur meets
________________


H2: Project Chairs
Beyond the standing committees, a Circle Director may appoint Project Chairs for specific initiatives — a major event, a community programme, a partnership, a special project.
Nine Project Chair roles are available.
A Circle that only runs its twelve meetings is a Circle that is not growing. Project Chairs are how a room takes on something ambitious, and how more Peers get a first taste of leading.
________________


H2: Who you become
You will hold a responsibility among people who outrank you commercially.
Nobody on your committee reports to you. Nobody has to attend the meeting you called, deliver the thing they promised, or take your view seriously. If you want the work done, you earn it.
That is the beginning of influence, and almost no business owner gets to practise it inside their own company.
What serving develops:
The habit of following through — a committee role is small enough to do properly and visible enough that people notice when you do not
Working with people you cannot instruct — the single most transferable leadership skill there is
Knowing the room — a Powerhouse member knows every Peer in their Circle, and every Peer knows them
Clarity about what you want next — some discover they want to run a Circle. Others discover they do not, and that is equally useful to know
________________


H2: Why serve
Your standing rises. Service is the highest-recognised form of contribution in this community.
You know everyone. The Peers running a Circle know every person in it.
It is where the path opens. Circle Director, Circle Founder, Industry Director, Executive Director — every one of them is reached through here.
It costs less than you think. A committee role is real work, but it is defined work. You are not carrying a Circle alone.
[ Explore the Leadership Pathway ]
________________


H2: How to join the Powerhouse
1. Be a Peer in the Circle.
2. Contribute. Your record in the room is what makes you a candidate.
3. Tell your Circle Director which role interests you.
4. Have the conversation. About the role, the time, and the fit.
5. Take the role.
Most Powerhouse members were approached rather than volunteering. Contribution is visible here, and people notice.

PAGE: CIRCLE FOUNDER
URL: /leadership/circle-founder
 Corrected
Meta title: Circle Founder | Peers Global
Meta description: A Circle Founder convenes the entrepreneurs of a city or an industry and builds a room where none existed. The role, the influence, the platform.
Primary keyword: become a circle founder
Primary CTA: Download Unity App
________________


HERO
H1: Circle Founder
Subline:
 Leading the leaders.
Supporting line:
 Every entrepreneur in your Circle is already a leader in their own right. Convening them, and holding their respect, is a different order of leadership entirely.
[ Download Unity App ] [ Apply to Lead ]
________________


H2: The most significant role in this community
A Circle Founder does not join a room. They create one.
You bring together the entrepreneurs of an industry or a city — accomplished business owners who did not know each other, who now meet every month because you convened them. You set the standard they operate by. You decide who belongs. You host the platform where their businesses grow.
This is the role from which everything at Peers Global begins. No Circle exists without a Founder. No city has a community until someone builds the first room in it.
It is also the most visible position an entrepreneur can hold in their own market.
H2: The room nobody has built
Somewhere in your city there are twenty entrepreneurs who would change each other's businesses if they were ever in the same room.
They are not. They pass each other at events, exchange cards, and go back to solving the same problems separately. The ecosystem exists. The room does not.
A Circle Founder is the person who builds it.
________________


H2: Circle starts from Day 1
"Circle doesn't start at 25 members. Circle starts from Day 1 — first member. Even if three people are there, it is still a Circle. Structure starts from Day 1."
This is the principle the whole role rests on.
You do not wait until you have a full room to begin. You run the format from your first meeting with your first members, and the room grows around a standard that was there from the beginning.
The official launch is a celebration, not the start.
________________




H2: What the role carries
You convene the industry
Every month, a room of established entrepreneurs assembles because you called it. Twelve occasions a year on which the people who matter in your sector are in one place, at your invitation.
You host the platform
The Circle meeting is your event. You shape its focus and decide who stands in front of it.
You invite the leaders
Senior industry figures, respected entrepreneurs and subject experts, invited as guests and speakers. That invitation comes from a position of hosting rather than asking — and it opens relationships that would not otherwise have opened.
You carry media and visibility
Coverage through VyapaarJagat.com, features across the Peers Global media ecosystem, and your Circle's own magazine and Coffee Table Book.
A Founder becomes visible in a way most business owners never do — not through advertising, but as the convenor of something that matters in their market.
You represent the community
At regional and national gatherings, in your city, and in your industry.
________________


H2: Leading the leaders
Every entrepreneur you convene runs their own company. Every one is used to being the most senior person in the room.
None of them has to listen to you.
You hold no authority over any of them. If you want that room to arrive on time, give before they ask, and protect what is shared, you earn it every single month.
In your own company, people follow because of the position you hold. Here, they follow because of who you are.
This is the highest form of leadership there is, and almost no business owner ever gets to practise it.
Founders come out different. Steadier. Better at reading a room. Able to move accomplished people without ever raising their voice.
________________


H2: What a Circle Founder does
Defines the Circle. The industry or purpose, the standard, the character of the room.
Convenes the founding members. Complementary businesses, category exclusivity, entrepreneurs who will give as well as take. The founding group sets the culture for the next decade.
Runs the format from Day 1. Fixed day, fixed time, the four-part agenda, from the very first meeting.
Builds the leadership team. By Stage 3, the Powerhouse is formed and the Circle no longer depends on you alone.
Carries it to launch. Stage 5, at 40+ members, with media, awards and industry positioning.
Hands over the running. A Circle Director takes the month-on-month after launch. You remain the Founder.
[ See Circle Roles ]
________________


H2: Who this role is for
This is for you if:
* You have built something already — a business, a team, a reputation
* Entrepreneurs in your city or industry already come to you for introductions
* You can list fifteen serious business owners who would take your call
* You can hold a standard among people who outrank you commercially
* You finish what you begin
This is not for you if you are looking for a title, a customer list, or a channel to sell into. A Circle led by someone selling to it does not survive its first year, and the room recognises it long before the Founder does.
________________


H2: What the community provides
The structure — the complete Circle model, the four-part agenda, the seat system, roles and standards
The Circle Founder Playbook — the full build guide, plus a 30-day quick-start covering exactly what to do in your first month
The systems — the Unity App, member management, contribution tracking and the Peer directory from Day 1
Founder induction — how to convene the founding group, run the agenda, and handle what every new Circle encounters
Regional support — an Executive Director alongside you, and access to Founders who have already done it
The platform and the brand — you convene under Peers Global, with its credibility and national reach
Media and visibility — coverage through VyapaarJagat.com and the wider ecosystem
________________


H2: What you bring
Standing in your market. Your credibility is what brings the founding entrepreneurs in.
Leadership. Someone holds the standard, and in the first a few months that is you.
Consistency. Every month, from Day 1.
Judgement. About who belongs in the room you are building.
________________


H2: How to become a Circle Founder
1. Become a Peer. Peers Global membership is required.
2. Apply. Your business, your city or industry, and the Circle you intend to build.
3. Meet an Executive Director. An honest conversation about the opportunity and the role.
4. Complete Founder induction. Including the 30-day quick-start guide.
5. Hold your first meeting. With your first members. Structure from Day 1.
6. Build through the stages. To official launch at 40+.
[ Apply to Found a Circle ]
________________


H2: Common questions
Do I need to be a Peer already?
 Yes. Peers Global membership is required.
How many entrepreneurs do I need to start?
 Your first meeting can be five people. A Circle starts on Day 1 with your first member. Official launch happens at 40+.
How long from application to first meeting?
 Between five days and two months, depending on how quickly you convene your first members.
How long to official launch?
 Roughly two months for a Founder working consistently. Some take longer, and take stronger Circles to launch for it.
Can I found a Circle while running my business?
 Yes. Every Circle Founder is a working business owner.
Can I start a Circle in an industry that exists elsewhere?
 Yes. An Industry Circle in one city is entirely separate from the same industry in another.
What happens after launch?
 A Circle Director takes on the month-on-month running while you continue as Founder. Many Founders go on to build a second Circle or move into wider leadership.

Meta description: The Circle Director builds and grows a Circle, mentors its Chairs and its Peers, and carries its culture. The role, the standing, and who it is for.
Primary keyword: circle director role
Secondary keywords: business community leadership India, entrepreneur mentor role, business circle leadership

HERO
H1: Circle Director
Subline:
 You grow the Circle, and everyone in it.
Supporting line:
 A Circle Director carries the culture of a room, mentors its Chairs and its Peers, and is responsible for the lives that room changes.
[ Download Unity App ] [ Apply to Lead ]
________________


H2: The Circle grows around its Director
A Circle Founder brings a room into existence. A Circle Director raises it.
The Director is responsible for what that Circle becomes — its culture, its discipline, its energy, and the number of entrepreneurs whose businesses change because of it. Every Peer in the room, every Chair on every committee, and every guest who walks through the door encounters the Circle the Director has shaped.
Where a Circle is generous, disciplined and productive, its Director built it that way.
This is the most rewarding position in the community, because it is the one where you can see what you built, in the room, every month.
________________


H2: You lead the leaders who lead the Circle
A Circle is not run by one person. It is run by its Chairs and its committees, and the Director develops all of them.
Three committees. Three Chairs. One Director behind them.
The Chairs hold the working life of the Circle — membership experience, collaboration, growth and the events the Circle runs. They are entrepreneurs learning to lead, and the Director is the person who develops them.
What that means in practice:
You mentor the Chairs. They come to you before decisions and after mistakes. You teach them how to hold a standard, how to run a committee, how to handle a difficult Peer. Several will go on to become Directors and Founders themselves.
You develop the Powerhouse. Every committee member is a leader in formation. A Director who invests in them builds a Circle that eventually runs without needing them.
You guide the Peers. The new Peer who has not yet found their footing. The established one whose contribution has quietened. The entrepreneur going through a difficult year who has stopped coming.
Nothing in business gives you this. You develop leaders you did not hire, who owe you nothing, and who will carry what you taught them into rooms you will never sit in.
________________


H2: What a Circle Director is responsible for
The culture
 The Peers Code is upheld in that Circle by its Director. How the room treats a new Peer, whether confidentiality holds, whether people give before they ask — all of it reflects the Director.
The standard
 Every meeting follows the Peers Global format. The four-part agenda, run properly, every month, in every city. A Director is why a Peer visiting from another city recognises the room immediately.
The Chairs and the committees
 Appointing them, mentoring them, and making sure each committee is genuinely active rather than nominal.
The impact
 The Circle exists to change businesses. A Director watches the Gratitude & Life Impact Round every month and knows whether the room is truly producing — and acts when it is not.
The events
 Circle meetings, masterclasses, guest sessions and the Circle's contribution to city and regional gatherings.
The health of the room
 Attendance, energy, contribution, seats filled and relationships forming. A Director sees the room as a whole and grows it.
The growth
 A Circle that is not growing is not healthy. Seats filled with entrepreneurs who strengthen it, and the room's reputation rising in its market.
[ See the Meeting Agenda ]
________________


H2: What the role carries
You chair the room
Twelve meetings a year, each attended by established business owners, each opened and led by you.
You decide who stands in front of it
The Impact Mentor Masterclass is yours to programme. You choose the subject the room needs and the person who teaches it — which gives you a natural reason to approach senior figures in your industry, offering them a room of serious entrepreneurs rather than asking a favour.
You host the guests
Industry leaders, prospective Peers, media and senior entrepreneurs invited to see the Circle. Each of them meets you first, in a room you are leading.
You carry visibility
Your Circle's meetings, collaborations and outcomes are covered through VyapaarJagat.com and the Peers Global media ecosystem. As Director, you are the face of that room to your city and your industry.
You represent the Circle
To the community, at city and regional gatherings, and to every entrepreneur considering joining it.
________________


H2: Who you become
Every entrepreneur in your Circle runs their own company. None of them has to follow you.
They have their own success and their own alternatives. You hold no authority over any of them. If you want that room disciplined, generous and consistent, you earn it through how you behave in front of them.
That is the difference between management and influence. In your own company people follow because of your position. Here, they follow because of who you are.
What a Director develops:
The ability to develop other leaders. Mentoring three Chairs and a committee of entrepreneurs is leadership training of a kind money cannot buy. You will take it straight back into your own company.
The capacity for a difficult conversation. Told directly, kindly, and in a way that keeps the relationship. Most business owners avoid this their entire lives.
Real listening. You lead thirty entrepreneurs by understanding what each of them needs, often before they say it.
Judgement about people. Who fits, who is drifting, who is ready to lead. This one skill will serve your own hiring for the rest of your career.
Presence. The ability to hold a room without dominating it.
________________


H2: Who this role is for
This is for you if:
* You get satisfaction from watching other people grow
* You have led people before and want to lead where authority does not apply
* You can hold a standard among entrepreneurs who outrank you commercially
* You are consistent — the same person in month nine as in month one
* You notice when someone has gone quiet
* You want standing in your market that comes from service
This is not for you if you want the position more than the responsibility. A Circle recognises the difference within a quarter.
________________


H2: What the community provides
Director induction — running the four-part agenda, holding the standard, mentoring Chairs, integrating new Peers, and growing a Circle
The systems — the Unity App, attendance, contribution tracking and member management
Regional support — a Regional Executive Director alongside you, and access to Directors across the community
The platform and the brand — you lead under Peers Global, with its structure, credibility and national reach
Media and visibility — coverage of your Circle across the Peers Global media ecosystem
________________


H2: What you bring
Consistency. Every month, including the months when it is inconvenient.
Standards. Held even when the person who needs correcting is someone you like.
Generosity with your time. The Chairs and the Peers will need you.
Attention. To the room, and to each person in it.
________________


H2: How to become a Circle Director
1. Become a Peer. Peers Global membership is required.
2. Join a Circle and contribute. Directors come from within. Your record in the room is what makes you a candidate.
3. Serve as a Chair. Most Directors led a committee first.
4. Express interest. To your Circle Founder or Regional Executive Director. Many Directors were approached rather than applying — contribution is visible here.
5. Complete Director induction.
6. Take the chair.
[ Apply to Lead ]
________________


H2: Common questions
Do I need to be a Peer already?
 Yes. Peers Global membership is required, and Directors are almost always drawn from within the Circle they go on to lead.
Can I be Director of a Circle I did not found?
 Yes. This is the standard route. A Founder builds the room and a Director takes on its growth, often after the first year.
Do I need to have served on the Powerhouse first?
 It is the usual path and the best preparation, though not an absolute requirement.
Can I be Director of a Circle I did not found?
 Yes. This is the standard route. The Founder builds and launches the room; a Director takes on its running.
Can a Founder also be the Director?
 Yes, particularly before launch. Most Founders hand the chair to a Director once the Circle is established.
How much time does it take?
 The meetings, the preparation, and time with your Powerhouse and Peers between them. The commitment is consistency rather than volume.
Can I direct a Circle while running my business?
 Yes. Every Director is a working business owner.
Can a Founder also be the Director?
 Yes, particularly in the first year. Most Founders eventually hand the chair to a Director while continuing as Founder.
How much time does the role take?
 The meetings, the preparation, and time with your Chairs and Peers between them. The commitment is consistency rather than volume, and it is discussed openly before any role is offered.
Can I direct a Circle while running my business?
 Yes. Every Director is a working business owner.
How long is the term?
 [CONFIRM — a stated term makes the commitment clear and succession normal]

PRODUCTION NOTES
Word count: ~1,350 · Type: Static · Phase: 1
What changed
The mentoring dimension was missing entirely, and it is the heart of the role.
Added: the Chairs and the three committees, mentoring the Powerhouse, guiding the Peers, responsibility for culture and format, responsibility for the Circle's impact and events, and responsibility for growth.
Reframed throughout as privilege rather than burden. The signature line is now you grow the Circle, and everyone in it. The developmental sections lead; the demands appear only in what you bring, briefly.
The paternal quality you described — a Director as the father of the Circle — is carried through the growth language rather than stated as a metaphor. A Circle becomes what its Director makes it does that work at the close.
The strongest section
"You lead the leaders who lead the Circle."
Mentoring three Chairs and a full committee of entrepreneurs is a benefit no other role page can claim, and it is the most attractive thing about this position for anyone who genuinely wants to develop as a leader.
The closing line of that section — you develop leaders who owe you nothing and who will carry what you taught them into rooms you will never sit in — is the emotional core of the page.
Two things I need
The three committees, named. I have written three Chairs of three committees generically because I do not have their names. Give me the actual committee names and their remits and I will write them in properly — this is the most concrete part of the role and it deserves specifics rather than a placeholder.
Term length. A stated term makes the commitment clear and succession routine. An open-ended role is harder to accept and much harder to end.
Design direction
A Circle Director photographed with their Chairs, not alone. The role is about developing others, and the image should show that.
Second image: a Director chairing a meeting, mid-sentence, room in view.
You lead the leaders who lead the Circle gets a full-width block. It is the section that will convert.
The seven responsibilities work as seven blocks with strong icons — they show scope, which is what makes the role feel significant rather than administrative.
SEO structure
* One H1, nine H2s
* Primary keyword circle director role in H1 area, meta title, meta description
* FAQPage schema for the seven questions

H1: Industry Director
Subline:
 You carry an industry.
Supporting line:
 One sector, across every Circle, every city and every country in the community.

H2: One sector, one person responsible for it
A Circle Director leads a room. An Industry Director leads a sector.
You hold one industry across the entire community — every Circle in every city where entrepreneurs from that sector sit. Manufacturing. Real estate. Healthcare. Technology. Education. Whichever industry you have spent your career inside.
Your responsibility is that industry's strength within Peers Global. The depth of expertise available in it. The connections between entrepreneurs who would never otherwise meet. The opportunities that only appear when a whole sector is organised rather than scattered.
You are the person that industry looks to inside this community.
________________


H2: What the role carries
You convene an entire sector
Not one room. Every entrepreneur in your industry, across every city in the community. Manufacturers in three states. Healthcare businesses in six cities. A network you assemble and lead.
Very few people in any industry hold that position, and almost nobody holds it by appointment. It is built.
You create sector-level opportunity
Large contracts that need several businesses. Joint bids. Shared capacity. Collective market access. Opportunities that no single Circle could produce and no single business could pursue alone.
An Industry Director is the person who sees them and puts the right entrepreneurs together.
You set the industry agenda
What your sector learns inside Peers Global. Which regulatory change needs explaining. Which market shift needs preparing for. Which masterclass runs across every Circle where your industry sits.
You speak for your industry
At regional and national gatherings, in Peers Global media, and to the wider community, you are the voice of that sector. Coverage through VyapaarJagat.com, features, podcasts and platforms across the ecosystem.
A recognised industry voice is one of the most valuable positions a business owner can hold, and it is almost impossible to acquire through marketing. It is earned by connecting people for years.
You engage the industry's leaders
Senior figures, associations, institutions and experts in your sector — approached as the Industry Director of a national community, with a room of serious entrepreneurs to offer them.
That is a very different conversation from approaching them as one business owner.
________________


H2: Who you become
You will see your own industry from thirty angles instead of one.
Every entrepreneur in your sector across the community brings you their view — the manufacturer in another state facing a cost pressure you have not yet met, the business three years ahead of you, the one entering a market you are considering.
You will understand your own industry better than almost anyone operating inside it. Not from research, but from listening to it every month.
Your name will begin to travel. Entrepreneurs in your sector will know it in cities you have never visited, because you connected two of them last year and they told a third.
And the influence you build is portable. It is not tied to your company or your position in it. Sector standing follows the person, and it stays with you wherever your business goes.
________________


H2: What an Industry Director does
Connects Peers across the sector. Entrepreneurs in the same industry, in different Circles and cities, who should know each other.
Builds depth. Expertise, knowledge and specialist capability available to every Peer in that industry.
Creates opportunity. Contracts, partnerships, joint capability and market access at a scale beyond any single Circle.
Programmes learning. Masterclasses, sessions and content for the sector across the community.
Supports Industry Circles. Working with Circle Directors wherever your industry sits.
Represents the sector. To the community, to the industry, to institutions and to media.
Grows the industry inside Peers Global. More entrepreneurs from your sector, in more cities, in more Circles.
[ See Industry Circles ]
________________


H2: Who this role is for
This is for you if:
* You have spent years in one industry and understand it properly
* Entrepreneurs in your sector already come to you for perspective
* You think about your whole industry, not only your own business
* You want standing in your sector that is built rather than bought
* You can work across Circles and cities without controlling any of them
* You are generous with what you know
This is not for you if you would use the position to advance your own business inside the sector. An Industry Director who serves themselves is visible immediately, and the sector stops trusting them long before anyone says so.

Media and visibility — sector coverage, features and speaking platforms across the Peers Global media ecosystem
________________


H2: What you bring
Deep sector knowledge. Years inside the industry, not a passing interest in it.
Generosity. You will give far more than you take, particularly in the first year.
A wide view. The ability to think about an industry while running a business inside it.
Consistency. A sector is built over years.
________________


H2: How to become an Industry Director
1. Become a Peer. Peers Global membership is required.
2. Join a Circle in your industry and contribute. Your record in the sector is what makes you a candidate.
3. Express interest. To your Circle Director or Regional Executive Director. Many Industry Directors were approached rather than applying.
4. Have the conversation. About the sector, the role, and whether it fits where your own business currently is.
5. Complete induction.
6. Take the industry.
[ Apply to Lead ]

H2: Common questions
Do I need to be a Peer already?
 Yes. Peers Global membership is required.
Do I need to be a Circle Director first?
 No, though many Industry Directors have led a Circle. What matters most is depth in the sector and standing among the entrepreneurs in it.
How many Industry Directors are there per sector?
 One holds an industry. 
Can I be an Industry Director and a Circle Director?
No
How much time does the role take?
 It is a connecting role rather than a meeting-attendance role. The commitment is discussed openly before any appointment.
Can I lead an industry while running a business inside it?
 Yes. Every Industry Director is a working business owner in the sector they carry.
What if my industry has few Peers so far?
 Then you are building it. That is the role, and the sector will carry your name because you were the one who started it.

	The leading people who do not report to you argument does not repeat here. It is fully owned by /leadership/circle-founder and /leadership/circle-director, where the room dynamic makes it literal. This page's development claim is different — seeing your industry from thirty angles, and building standing that is portable.
The strongest sections
"You will understand your own industry better than almost anyone operating inside it."
This is the argument that converts. It is a commercial benefit to the candidate's own business, arriving as a by-product of service rather than as a reward. Every serious operator will recognise its value immediately.
"Sector standing follows the person."
The second strongest. An Industry Director builds a reputation that is not tied to their company — which matters enormously to an entrepreneur thinking about the next twenty years rather than the next quarter.
The disqualifying line matters here more than on other role pages
An Industry Director who serves themselves is visible immediately.
This role carries more potential for self-dealing than any other — a Director who routes every sector opportunity to their own business. Naming it publicly sets the expectation before anyone takes the role.
Two things to confirm
Scope of an Industry Director. One per sector nationally, or one per region? This becomes significant as the community expands internationally, and a candidate will ask.
Whether the role can be held alongside a Circle Directorship. Both are demanding, and the answer affects who applies.

Who you become gets a full-width block. It is the section that will convert.

HERO
H1: Executive Director
Subline:
 You carry territory.
Supporting line:
 Area. District. State. Country. Wherever you hold, the entrepreneurial ecosystem inside it becomes your responsibility.
[ Download Unity App ] [ Apply to Lead ]
________________


H2: Four levels. One responsibility.
An Executive Director carries a territory and everything Peers Global becomes inside it.
Area Executive Director — a defined area within a city, and the Circles inside it
District Executive Director — a full city or district, its Circles, and the community across them
State Executive Director — a state, its districts and cities, and the leaders who hold them
Country Executive Director — a nation, and the entire Peers Global ecosystem within it
The scope changes at each level. The responsibility does not.
You are accountable for what the community becomes where you hold it — how many entrepreneurs it reaches, how strong its Circles are, whether the culture holds, and how many lives it changes.
________________


H2: Where the community actually grows
Circles do not appear on their own. Cities do not build themselves.
Every Circle in a territory exists because an Executive Director identified the opportunity, found the right Founder, supported them through the launch, and held the standard afterwards.
An Executive Director is where the map moves.
You decide where the next Circle opens. You identify who should lead it. You take Peers Global into a city where nobody has heard of it, and eighteen months later there is a community there with a Founder, Directors, Chairs and a room full of entrepreneurs whose businesses have changed.
That did not happen without you.
________________


H2: What the role carries
You build the ecosystem
Not one room. Every Circle, every Founder, every Director and every Peer in your territory. You are building the entrepreneurial infrastructure of a place.
You develop the leaders who lead
Circle Founders and Circle Directors across your territory report to you in practice. You identify them, appoint them, mentor them and hold them to the standard.
This is leadership at one remove. You are responsible for rooms you are not in, led by people you did not train from scratch, in cities you cannot visit every month. Doing it well requires developing leaders rather than managing them.
You represent Peers Global in your territory
To the business community, to institutions, to industry bodies, to government where relevant, and to media. In your territory, you are Peers Global.
You engage at the highest level
Senior business figures, associations, chambers, institutions and public officials — approached as the Executive Director of a national community with real presence in their market.
At State and Country level, those conversations are with people most business owners never get a meeting with.
You carry visibility across the territory
Regional and national events, coverage through VyapaarJagat.com and the Peers Global media ecosystem, and platforms across the community. An Executive Director is a public figure in their territory's business landscape.
You host the territory's events
Conclaves, regional gatherings, summits and celebrations. Bringing together every Circle in your territory, and the entrepreneurs, guests and speakers who attend them.
________________


H2: Who you become
You stop measuring a good month by your own business.
You start measuring it by how many entrepreneurs across your territory had a better one. How many Circles grew. How many Founders you developed. How many businesses changed because a room existed that you made happen.
That shift is permanent, and it changes how you see everything afterwards — including your own company.
What the role develops:
Ecosystem thinking. The ability to see a whole market as a system of relationships rather than a set of transactions. Very few business owners ever acquire it, and it changes how you approach expansion, partnership and competition for the rest of your career.
Leading through leaders. You cannot do the work yourself. You succeed only if the Founders and Directors under you succeed, which forces you to become genuinely good at developing people.
Institutional standing. Executive Directors deal with chambers, associations, institutions and officials. That access, and the credibility that comes with it, is difficult to build any other way.
Judgement at scale. Choosing who founds a Circle in a city you visit twice a year is a different order of judgement from choosing a hire you supervise daily.
________________


H2: What an Executive Director does
Expands the territory. Identifying where the next Circle should open, and making it happen.
Appoints and develops Founders. Finding the right entrepreneur to build each room, and supporting them through the first year.
Supports Circle Directors. Mentoring the people who lead the rooms, and holding the standard across all of them.
Protects the culture. The Peers Code holds identically in every Circle in the territory, and the Executive Director is why.
Ensures the format. Every Circle runs the Peers Global agenda properly, so a Peer visiting from another city recognises the room.
Drives impact. The territory exists to change businesses. An Executive Director watches whether it is doing so, and acts when it is not.
Hosts territory events. Conclaves, gatherings and summits across the region.
Represents the community. To business, institutions, industry and media within the territory.
[ See the Territory Structure ]
________________


H2: Who this role is for
This is for you if:
* You have built and led organisations, not only teams
* You think about markets and ecosystems rather than single transactions
* You get satisfaction from developing leaders who then outgrow your guidance
* You can hold a standard across places you are not physically present
* You have standing in your territory that opens doors
* You want to build something with permanence in the place you live
This is not for you if you need to control the detail. An Executive Director who tries to run every Circle personally will hold a territory of three rooms instead of thirty.
________________


H2: What the community provides
Executive Director induction — the role, the systems, territory development, and how to build and support Circles at scale
The full leadership structure — Founders, Directors, Industry Directors and Chairs across your territory
The systems — the Unity App, member management, contribution tracking and territory reporting
National support — other Executive Directors, the founding team and the wider leadership of the community
The platform and the brand — you build under Peers Global, with its structure, credibility and national reach
Media and visibility — territory coverage, features and speaking platforms across the Peers Global media ecosystem
________________


H2: What you bring
Standing in your territory. Your credibility is what opens the first doors in a new city.
The capacity to develop leaders. The role succeeds only through other people.
Consistency over years. A territory is built slowly and then all at once.
Judgement. About cities, about Founders, and about when to intervene and when to let a leader learn.
________________


H2: How to become an Executive Director
1. Become a Peer. Peers Global membership is required.
2. Lead something first. Most Executive Directors founded or directed a Circle before taking a territory.
3. Express interest. To your existing Executive Director or the leadership of the community.
4. Have the conversation. About the territory, the role, the scope and whether it fits where your own business currently is.
5. Complete induction.
6. Take the territory.
[ Apply to Lead ]
________________


H2: Common questions
Do I need to be a Peer already?
 Yes. Peers Global membership is required.
Do I need to have led a Circle first?
 It is the usual path and the best preparation. Founding or directing a Circle teaches you what you will later be asking others to do.
Which level would I start at?
 Most Executive Directors begin at Area or District level and grow with the territory they build. State and Country appointments come from proven territory leadership.
How is a territory defined?
 By the community, based on business geography rather than administrative boundaries. It is agreed clearly before any appointment.
Can I hold a territory while running my business?
 Yes. Every Executive Director is a working business owner. The role is built around developing leaders rather than doing the work yourself.
How much time does it take?
 More at the start, when the first Circles are being founded, and progressively less as leaders develop under you. Discussed openly before any appointment.
Can I be an Executive Director and a Circle Director?
 [CONFIRM]
What if there are no Circles in my territory yet?
 Then you are building from the beginning. That is the most demanding version of this role and the one that leaves the deepest mark — every Circle in that territory will exist because you started it.
________________


CLOSING
There is a city, a state or a country where this community does not exist yet.
Someone has to be the reason it does.
Build Your Business. Build Your Relationships. Build Your Circle.

Word count: ~1,300 · Type: Static · Phase: 1
The four levels
Written as one page covering Area, District, State and Country rather than four pages, because the responsibility is identical and only the scope changes. Four near-identical pages would duplicate content and dilute all of them.
Consider renaming in the navigation from Regional Executive Director to Executive Director. The four levels are Area, District, State and Country — none of which is called Region. The slug can stay /leadership/regional-ed for continuity, or change to /leadership/executive-director if the site is not yet live.
Content ownership across the four Director roles


	Circle Director
	Industry Director
	Executive Director
	Scope
	One room
	One sector, everywhere
	One territory, all sectors
	Signature
	You grow the Circle, and everyone in it
