	No development claim repeats across the three pages. Each answers who do I become differently, which is what keeps them distinct for both readers and search.
The strongest sections
"An Executive Director is where the map moves."
This is the argument. Everything else at Peers Global happens inside rooms that already exist. This is the only role responsible for rooms that do not exist yet.
"You stop measuring a good month by your own business."
The emotional core. It describes a genuine change in a person, and it will be recognised immediately by anyone who has already begun feeling it.
"Leadership at one remove."
The honest description of what makes this role hard — responsible for rooms you are not in, led by people you cannot supervise. Framed as a discipline to develop rather than a burden to bear.
Two things to confirm
Whether Executive Director can be held alongside a Circle Directorship. A candidate will ask, and different Executive Directors will answer differently unless it is settled.
How territories are defined and awarded. The page says "by the community, based on business geography." If there is a formal process, state it — territory is the most valuable thing this community grants, and clarity protects everyone.
Design direction
Photograph an Executive Director with the Founders and Directors of their territory. A group, not a portrait. The role is about leaders developed, and the image should say so.
Second image: an Executive Director hosting a regional conclave, room in view.
The four levels — Area, District, State, Country — deserve a clear visual progression near the top of the page. It shows scope and it shows a path.
Who you become gets a full-width block, no imagery.
SEO structure
* One H1, eight H2s
* Primary keyword executive director role in H1 area, meta title, meta description
* FAQPage schema for the eight questions
* Phase 2: territory-specific pages — executive director Gujarat, country director India — carry high intent and almost no competition

PAGE: AMBASSADOR
URL: /leadership/ambassadors
 Menu: Leadership → Ambassadors
Meta title: Ambassador | Peers Global
Meta description: Ambassadors carry Peers Global into rooms where it is not yet known. The role, the standing it builds, and who it is for.
Primary keyword: peers global ambassador
Secondary keywords: business community ambassador role, entrepreneur network ambassador India
Primary CTA: Download Unity App
Secondary CTA: Apply to Lead
Internal links: /leadership · /leadership/circle-founder · /leadership/circle-director · /the-citizens · /culture-and-code · /leadership/apply
________________


HERO
H1: Ambassador
Subline:
 You carry the name.
Supporting line:
 An Ambassador represents Peers Global in rooms where it is not yet known, and brings in the entrepreneurs who belong here.
[ Download Unity App ] [ Apply to Lead ]
________________


H2: The community travels through people
Peers Global does not grow through advertising.
It grows because a respected entrepreneur says to another respected entrepreneur: you should be part of this.
That sentence carries more weight than any campaign, and it only carries weight when the person saying it has something to lose by saying it wrongly.
An Ambassador is trusted with exactly that.
You represent this community in your industry, your city and your network. You speak for what it stands for. And you decide who hears about it from you.
________________


H2: What the role carries
You are trusted with the community's reputation
Every entrepreneur you introduce to Peers Global forms their first impression of it through you. That trust is not given lightly, and holding it is itself a statement about your standing.
Your recommendation carries weight
Ambassadors are chosen because their word already means something in their market. The role formalises that — and every introduction you make becomes a visible exercise of your own judgement.
You open institutional doors
Associations, chambers, industry bodies, institutions and partner organisations. An Ambassador approaches them as the representative of a national community rather than as an individual business owner.
You speak for the community
At events, on panels, in your industry and in your network. Ambassadors are the community's voice in rooms where Peers Global has not yet arrived.
You carry visibility
Coverage and features across the Peers Global media ecosystem, and a platform at community events. An Ambassador becomes publicly associated with something respected.
You shape who joins
The entrepreneurs you bring in strengthen the community. The ones you decline to bring protect it. Both matter equally.
________________


H2: Who you become
The person whose recommendation means something.
That is not a small thing. In business, most introductions are transactional and everyone knows it. An introduction from someone who has nothing to gain, and who is putting their own credibility behind it, is rare enough that people remember who made it.
An Ambassador builds a reputation for judgement.
Over time, entrepreneurs in your network stop asking whether Peers Global is worth their time and start asking whether you think they belong in it. That shift — from advocate to arbiter — is the standing this role produces.
What it develops:
Discernment. You will meet entrepreneurs who want in and should not be here. Learning to recognise that quickly, and to decline it gracefully, is a skill that improves every relationship you have.
The confidence to represent. Speaking for something larger than your own business teaches you to hold a position in a room without defending it.
A wider network by design. The role puts you in front of institutions, associations and senior figures you would have no natural reason to meet.
________________


H2: What an Ambassador does
Represents Peers Global in their industry, their city and their network.
Introduces the right entrepreneurs. People who will strengthen the community, not simply fill it.
Declines the wrong ones. Quietly, and without apology.
Connects institutions. Associations, chambers, industry bodies and partner organisations.
Speaks at events. In rooms where the community is not yet present.
Upholds the Peers Code publicly. An Ambassador's conduct is the community's conduct, everywhere they are seen.
________________


H2: Who this role is for
This is for you if:
* Your word already carries weight in your industry or city
* People come to you for introductions and take them seriously
* You have judgement about people and are willing to use it
* You can say no to someone who wants in and should not be here
* You want standing built on being trusted rather than on being known
* You believe in this community enough to put your name behind it
This is not for you if you would use the position to build your own client list. An Ambassador who introduces people for their own benefit is recognised quickly, and the trust the role depends on does not survive it.
________________


H2: What the community provides
Ambassador induction — the role, the standards, and how to represent the community properly
The platform and the brand — you represent Peers Global, with its structure, credibility and national reach
Materials and support — everything needed to speak about the community accurately
Access — to leaders across the community, and to events and platforms
Media and visibility — features and coverage across the Peers Global media ecosystem
________________


H2: What you bring
Standing. Your credibility is what makes the role work.
Judgement. About who belongs and who does not.
Conduct. You are the community wherever you are seen.
Generosity. Ambassadors introduce far more than they extract.
________________


H2: How to become an Ambassador
1. Become a Peer. Peers Global membership is required.
2. Contribute. Ambassadors are chosen from entrepreneurs whose record and conduct are already visible in the community.
3. Express interest. To your Circle Director or Executive Director. Most Ambassadors were approached rather than applying.
4. Have the conversation. About the role, the standard it requires, and what representing the community involves.
5. Complete induction.
6. Carry the name.
[ Apply to Lead ]
________________


H2: Common questions
Do I need to be a Peer already?
 Yes. Peers Global membership is required, and Ambassadors are chosen from within the community.
How much time does the role take?
 Considerably less than a Circle role. Ambassadors represent rather than operate, and the commitment is opportunistic rather than scheduled.
Can I be an Ambassador alongside another leadership role?
 [CONFIRM]
How many Ambassadors are there?
 The role is held by a limited number of Peers. It depends on trust rather than on coverage.
Is there a target for how many people I bring in?
 No. An Ambassador who introduces three entrepreneurs who genuinely belong here has done more than one who introduces thirty who do not.
What if someone I introduce does not work out?
 That happens, and it is not held against you. Judgement is a practice, not a guarantee.
________________

Some people are known. Fewer are trusted.
Build Your Business. Build Your Relationships. Build Your Circle.

HERO
H1: Global Advisory Board
Subline:
 You guide the whole.
Supporting line:
 Senior entrepreneurs who counsel this community on where it goes, how it grows, and what it must never compromise.
[ Download Unity App ] [ Meet Our Advisors ]
________________


H2: Every institution needs a memory
Communities lose themselves gradually.
A standard is relaxed once for a good reason. A shortcut is taken because growth demands it. Nobody notices any single decision, and then five years later the thing that made it worth building is gone.
Global Advisors exist to prevent that.
They do not run Circles. They do not hold territory. They bring decades of experience to the decisions that shape where Peers Global goes — and they are the people who say we do not do that here when it needs saying.
An Advisory Board is the memory and the conscience of an institution.
________________


H2: What the role carries
You shape the direction
Where the community expands. What it builds. Which opportunities it takes and which it declines. Advisors are consulted on the decisions that matter.
You protect what must not change
Growth creates pressure to compromise. An Advisor's most valuable contribution is often a single sentence at the right moment, from someone with nothing to prove and no position to defend.
You develop the leadership
Founders, Directors and Executive Directors across the community draw on Advisors for counsel. Your experience reaches entrepreneurs you will never meet.
You represent the community at the highest level
To institutions, industry bodies, partners and media. An Advisory Board is how an organisation is taken seriously by people who have seen many organisations.
________________


H2: Who you become
Someone whose experience outlives their own company.
Most business knowledge dies with the business that produced it. Thirty years of judgement, of mistakes paid for, of instincts about people and markets — almost all of it disappears when a founder steps back.
This is where yours keeps working.
Through Founders who build rooms you will never sit in. Through Directors who handle a situation the way you told them to. Through decisions made properly because you were in the conversation.
That is a different kind of legacy from anything a balance sheet records.
The hardest part: guiding without controlling. Letting leaders make mistakes you can see coming, because that is how they learn — and being available when they arrive at the consequence.
________________


H2: What a Global Advisor does
Counsels the community on direction, growth and strategy.
Protects the culture as the community scales.
Mentors senior leadership — Founders, Directors and Executive Directors.
Opens institutional relationships at a level the community could not reach alone.
Represents Peers Global where seniority and standing are what carry a conversation.
________________


H2: Who this role is for
This is for you if:
* You have built and led something significant over decades
* You are at a stage where guiding matters more to you than operating
* You can advise without needing to control the outcome
* You have judgement about people, markets and institutions that took a career to acquire
* You want your experience to continue working after your own business no longer needs it
Advisors are invited rather than appointed. The Advisory Board is composed deliberately, and its members are approached by the founding leadership of the community.
________________

[Named Advisors — photograph, business, city, and one line on what they advise on.]
[POPULATE from the leaders table. Do not publish this section with placeholder profiles — an unnamed advisory board is worse than none.]

Can I apply to join the Advisory Board?
 Advisors are invited. If you believe you would contribute, speak to the leadership of the community.
Do Advisors need to be Peers?
 [CONFIRM]
How much time does it require?
 Considerably less than an operating role. Advisors are consulted rather than scheduled.
Do Advisors run Circles?
 No. The role is deliberately separate from operations, which is what allows an Advisor to see the whole clearly.

PRODUCTION NOTES
Ambassador — word count ~1,000 · Phase 1
The role is about trust, not reach. Written throughout as whose recommendation means something rather than as a growth or referral role. That framing attracts entrepreneurs with genuine standing and repels anyone looking for a business development channel.
The FAQ on targets is deliberate. An Ambassador who introduces three entrepreneurs who genuinely belong here has done more than one who introduces thirty who do not. Publishing that removes any suggestion of a numbers role.
Confirm: whether an Ambassador can hold another leadership role simultaneously.
Global Advisory Board — word count ~800 · Phase 2
Publish only when the board is real.
This page is the single most credibility-sensitive page on the site. A visitor who finds an Advisory Board section with no named advisors, or with people who lend a name but do nothing, will discount everything else the site claims.
Two conditions before publishing:
Real advisors, named, photographed, with their business and city, and one line on what they advise on.
Enough of them to look like a board. Three named senior entrepreneurs is a board. One is a placeholder.
Until both are true, keep the page unpublished and leave the nav item inactive. That is what the phase field in menu_items is for.
The role is written as invitation-only, which is honest and also protects you from applications you would have to decline.
Confirm: whether Advisors must be Peers, or whether the board can include senior figures from outside the community.
Design direction
Ambassador: photograph an Ambassador introducing someone at an event, or on a stage speaking about the community. The role is about representation and the image should show it.
Advisory Board: formal, restrained, portrait-led. This is the page where visual seniority matters. Real photographs, consistent treatment, generous space.

H2: What happens next
1. Your interest is reviewed.
 By the Executive Director for your territory, or the relevant leadership for the role.
2. You have a conversation.
 An honest discussion about the role, what it requires, and whether it fits where your own business currently is. You should ask as many questions as they do.

 The model, the systems, the standards and the practical work of the role.

H2: What we look for
A record of giving. Every leader here contributed long before they led.
Standing in your market. Enough that entrepreneurs take your call and your recommendation.
The capacity to hold a standard. Including with people you like, and people more successful than you.
Consistency. The same person in month nine as in month one.
A reason beyond yourself. Anyone taking a role primarily for the title should not take one. The community recognises the difference within a quarter.

H2: Common questions
Do I need to be a Peer first?
 Yes. Peers Global membership is required for every leadership role.
Do I need leadership experience in a community before?
 No. Running a business, leading a team or holding a committee role all count. What matters most is your record of contribution and your standing among entrepreneurs.
Can I hold a leadership role while running my business?
 Yes. Every leader at Peers Global is a working business owner, and every role is designed around that.
What if I am not sure which role fits?
 Select not sure yet. That conversation is exactly what these discussions are for.
How long does the process take?
 It varies by role and territory. You will be contacted and given a clear timeline.
What if I am not selected?
 You will be told directly and honestly why. In many cases it is timing rather than suitability, and you are welcome to revisit it later.
Is there any cost to holding a leadership role?
 No. You maintain your Peers Global membership as any Peer does. The role itself carries no fee.

PRODUCTION NOTES
Word count: ~600 · Type: Form · Phase: 1
Why a form here, when joining is app-only
Membership is a subscription with no approval gate, so the app handles it end to end.
Leadership is different. Every role requires assessment and a conversation before appointment, so a form and a human follow-up are the correct mechanism. There is no way to automate the judgement involved.
The two paths do not conflict — this form is for people who are already Peers, or who will become one first.
Phase 2: move this into the Unity App entirely. A Peer expressing leadership interest from inside the app, with their contribution record already attached, is a much stronger signal than a cold web form. Until then, the web form is right.
The question that matters most
"Have you built or led something beyond your own business?"
This single field will predict success in a leadership role better than anything else on the form. Someone who has run a committee, built an association chapter or led a community group already understands leading people who do not report to them.
Make it prominent and read it first when reviewing applications.
The founding-specific block
Asking how many entrepreneurs a candidate could convene, with the twenty-five minimum stated directly beneath it, does two things.
It qualifies immediately — someone who answers fewer than 10 has told you the conversation will be about readiness rather than appointment.
And it sets the expectation before the call, so a Regional Executive Director is not delivering an unwelcome surprise.
One thing worth stating clearly
"Is there any cost to holding a leadership role?"
The answer is no, and publishing it removes a real hesitation. Some entrepreneurs assume leadership in a paid community means paying more. Answering it plainly costs nothing and removes the doubt.
Content ownership
This page owns the form and nothing else. Each role gets one line and a link. All persuasion, all detail and all development claims live on the five role pages.
If this page starts explaining what a Circle Founder does, it is duplicating /leadership/circle-founder and both pages weaken.
SEO structure
* One H1, six H2s
* Primary keyword apply leadership role peers global in H1 area, meta title, meta description
* FAQPage schema for the seven questions
* Low search volume, high intent. This page exists to convert people arriving from the role pages, not to acquire traffic
Design direction
The five roles as cards, side by side. Equal weight, each linking to its full page. Many visitors arrive here still deciding.
Progressive disclosure on the form. One section at a time with a progress bar. The founding block appears only when Circle Founder is selected.
What we look for sits after the form, not before. Someone who has already submitted reads it as confirmation; someone reading before submitting may talk themselves out of it.
Developer instructions
Routing: by city to the Executive Director where one exists, by industry to the Industry Director where relevant, otherwise to central leadership.
Escalation alert if an application sits unassigned past 48 hours. A candidate for a leadership role who is ignored will not apply twice.
Save partial submissions.
Capture city and role on every application. Repeated Founder interest from an uncovered city is direct evidence of where to expand next — this form is one of your best expansion signals.

PRODUCTION NOTES
Community, never network — apply everywhere
This is a vocabulary rule for the whole site, not a fix to one page.
Never write: network, networking app, professional network, business network, networking platform.
Always write: community, global community, the Peers, this community.
The one permitted exception is describing what someone already has elsewhere — "an existing network" on /who-belongs-here, referring to contacts a person brings with them. That is accurate and it does not describe Peers Global.

	Footer
	Networking Tips was already removed — confirm it stays out
	Meta descriptions
	Several use "network" — sweep all of them
	/membership/benefits is the important one. Networking with 250+ entrepreneurs is exactly the framing the whole site works to avoid.
Why this matters commercially, not only editorially
Every business group in India describes itself as a network. The word carries a specific and unattractive association — rooms of people exchanging cards, extracting what they can, and moving on.
Community carries obligation in both directions. It implies belonging, standards and permanence, which is what your model actually delivers and what your pricing requires people to believe.
A prospect deciding on ₹18,000 for a network is comparing you to free alternatives. A prospect deciding on ₹18,000 for membership of a community is making a different kind of decision.
The section added
"One community, every country."
Global from day one now appears on the app page rather than only in the FAQ. Unity is the proof of that claim — it is the thing that actually connects a Peer in Gujarat to one in Dubai, and it should say so.

H2: Common questions
How do I find events near me?
 Open the Unity App and browse by city. The calendar shows everything happening across the community.
Can I attend if I am not a member?
 Yes, a few events. Circle meetings and masterclasses accept guests. Register through the app.
Are events free for members?
 No. Every event carries a fee, for Peers and Charter Peers alike. Fees are shown before you register.
Can I bring my team?
 Yes, as registered visitors on payment of the visitor fee. Speak to your Circle Director.
Can I host or sponsor an event?
 Speak to your Circle Director or Executive Director.
Are events included in my membership?
 Circle meetings are covered by your Circle Experience Fee. Other events carry their own fees, shown before you register.
Are events online or in person?
 Both formats run. Each listing states its format.
Can I attend events outside my own Circle?
 Yes. Charter Peers can attend Circle meetings in any city, up to twelve cross-region meetings a year.

PRODUCTION NOTES
Word count: ~900 · Type: DB-driven · Phase: 1
The empty calendar problem
This is the most likely way this page damages you.
A visitor who lands on an events page showing nothing scheduled concludes the community is inactive. That single impression is harder to recover from than almost anything else on the site.
Three rules for the developer:
Never display an empty calendar. If nothing is scheduled, show past events with photographs plus a line stating that dates are published monthly in the app.
Never show a city with no events as an empty state. Show the nearest active city instead.
Cache and check. If the events feed fails, hide the calendar block entirely rather than rendering it blank.
Everything routes to the app
Consistent with the single-path decision. There is no registration form on this page — the calendar is a browsable view, and registration happens in Unity.
One consequence to plan for: a visitor who wants to attend a guest meeting must download an app first. That is friction at exactly the moment someone is most willing to act.
Mitigate it: the app link from this page should deep-link straight to the event they were looking at, so the download feels like a step toward the thing rather than away from it. Without that, this page will leak visitors.
Content ownership
Concept
	Owner page
	Treatment here
	The four-part meeting agenda
	/circle-meeting-experience
	Named, linked
	What a Circle is
	/circles
	Not covered
	Charter cross-region access
	/membership/tiers
	One FAQ line
	Awards and recognition in full
	/awards (Phase 2)
	One block, will link
	App features
	/unity
	Not repeated
	The unique material is the seven event types and the guest experience.
SEO structure
* One H1, six H2s, seven H3s for the event types
* Primary keyword entrepreneur events India in H1 area, meta title, meta description
* Event schema on every individual event listing — this is the strongest structured-data opportunity on the site and puts events into Google's event results
* FAQPage schema for the seven questions
* Phase 2: city event pages at /events/city/{slug}. Business events in Ahmedabad is a high-volume local search with real acquisition value
Design direction
Photography carries this page. Real events, real rooms, real people. An events page without images of actual events is not credible.
The calendar sits high — most visitors arrive here to see what is happening, not to read about event types.
Filters: city, type, date, open to guests. Fast, and working properly on mobile.
The seven event types work as cards below the calendar, each with a photograph.
Attending as a guest deserves prominence. It is the conversion section of this page.
Developer instructions
Calendar renders from the events table with city, type, date, format and guest-eligibility filters.
Every event listing carries Event schema with date, location, organiser and offers.
Deep-link app CTAs to the specific event.
Track which cities are searched with no results. That is direct evidence of where demand exists ahead of supply.

PRODUCTION NOTES
Word count: ~700 · Type: DB-driven · Phase: 1
This page is the proof, and it is fragile
Every other page on this site makes claims. This is the only page that evidences them.
Which means a weak version of this page undermines the whole site. A visitor who reads the manifesto, the ten Ways and the currency system, then arrives here and finds three vague testimonials, concludes that none of it is real.
Four rules, non-negotiable:
Real names on both sides. Two Peers, two businesses, two cities. Never a member in the manufacturing sector — anonymity reads as fiction.
A number in every outcome. Revenue, contracts, employees, cities entered, months saved. Without a number it is a testimonial again.
A real photograph of the two of them together. Not headshots side by side. The two people, in the same frame.
Written consent from both, on file. Before publication, without exception.
Three real stories beat twelve written ones
If you have three, publish three. The page will still work.
If you have one, publish one and build the page around it. A single fully evidenced story with names, numbers and a photograph does more than a grid of twelve vague ones.
Do not launch this page with placeholder or composite stories. It is the one page where a fabrication would be detectable and fatal.
The section that fills the gap at launch
The kinds of stories this community produces is written to carry the page while the story library is still small. It describes what happens here without claiming specific instances, which keeps the page useful and honest on day one.
Remove it once you have twelve or more real stories. By then the stories themselves make the argument.
Content ownership
Concept
	Owner page
	Treatment here
	The 10 Ways explained
	/10-ways-of-collaboration
	Named, linked
	Life Impact and confirmation
	/the-currency
	One section, linked
	The 1 Million Mission
	/1-million-mission
	One block, linked
	What a Circle is
	/circles
	Not covered
	SEO structure
* One H1, seven H2s
* Primary keyword entrepreneur success stories India in H1 area, meta title, meta description
* Article schema on every individual story page at /stories/{slug}
* Individual story pages are the real SEO asset — each one targets a specific industry, city and collaboration type
* Phase 2: /stories/city/{slug} and /stories/circle/{slug} for local search
Design direction
Photography is the page. Two people, together, real. A story grid of text cards with no faces will not convince anyone.
Each card leads with the photograph, then the two names and businesses, then one line on what they built.
The Before / What happened / Now structure should be visually consistent across every story. A reader learns the pattern in the first story and reads the rest faster.
Show the Way of Collaboration as a visible tag on every card. It reinforces the framework and lets people filter by what they need.
Developer instructions
Stories render from a stories table: both Peer IDs, businesses, cities, Circle, Way of Collaboration, before, action, outcome, outcome number, photograph, consent flags for both Peers, published status.
Both consent flags must be true before a story can publish. Enforce it at database level, not in the admin interface.
Submission flows through the Unity App, with the second Peer prompted to confirm before anything reaches review.
Track which stories are read most. That tells you which collaboration types resonate, and it should inform what you commission next.

ABOUT US
PAGE: OUR STORY
URL: /our-story
 Menu: About → Our Story
Phase: 1
Meta title: Our Story | Peers Global
Meta description: How Peers Global began, what it has become, and where it is going. The story of a community built so entrepreneurs would not have to build alone.
Primary keyword: peers global story
Secondary keywords: entrepreneur community India history, business community founded
Primary CTA: Download Unity App
Secondary CTA: Read The Idea
Internal links: /the-idea · /founder · /1-million-mission · /the-territory · /circles · /apply
________________


HERO
H1: Our Story
Subline:
 It began with one observation, in a hospital corridor.
Supporting line:
 Entrepreneurs do not fail only because of business problems. They fail because they fight alone.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: Before there was a community
Dr. Pravin Parmar grew up in a farmer family in Botad.
Not a wealthy one. Uncertainty, hard work, seasons deciding whether there would be income that year, and dignity without money. No education in the family, but values in it.
He also saw something in that village that took thirty years to fully understand. When a season failed, nobody faced it alone. People carried each other. In Botad that was ordinary. Everywhere else, it turned out to be rare.
He could not speak English, and when he decided to learn, the school told him no. He stood up anyway — not really about English, but about deciding that his background would not decide his ceiling.
He paid his own fees by teaching other students while he was still studying. He built a company, was right about the product and early to the market, and eventually admitted to himself that it could run his present but not build his future. He closed it.
Then a family crisis put him in a hospital, and hospitals strip every illusion a person carries.
Standing there he understood something uncomfortable. Skills mean very little without the right people around you. So does intelligence. So does hard work. What matters in those moments is who you can call.
He had spent years building capability. He had never built a circle. And almost none of the entrepreneurs he knew had one either.
[ Read the Founder's Story ]
________________


H2: The story nobody would publish
There was a second moment.
When he tried to get his own business story published, the media was not interested. Not out of rudeness — simply because business media covers startups, funding and unicorns, while MSMEs stay invisible.
And MSMEs are the businesses that build families, cities and employment across this country.
That rejection produced a belief that still governs everything here: every honest business story deserves respect and visibility. Even if a business shuts down, its story should never die.
It also produced VyapaarJagat.com, a media platform for the businesses nobody else was covering.
________________


H2: Why a community, and not something easier
Events would have been simpler. Media was already built.
Events end. Media informs. Only community transforms.
Entrepreneurs did not need more speakers. They needed a place where they could be real without being judged — where the room understood the weight they carried because everyone in it carried something similar.
So the decision was made to build rooms rather than audiences.
Circles, not crowds. Trust, not transactions. Peers, not gurus.
That was the beginning of Peers Global.
________________


H2: What we built
Not a networking group with a new name. A working system.
Circles — rooms of the right entrepreneurs, one seat per business category, meeting on a fixed rhythm
The 10 Ways of Collaboration — a defined practice, so I can help you means something specific
The recognition system — Life Impact Score, Peer Standing and Peers Coin, so contribution is recorded and rewarded rather than merely appreciated
The Peers Code — six commitments that make a room safe enough to be honest in
The Unity App — the community running every day, not only on meeting day
A leadership structure built entirely from entrepreneurs — Founders, Directors, Industry Directors, Executive Directors, Chairs and Advisors, every one of them a working business owner
Each piece exists because something in the earlier version did not work without it.
[ Explore Our World ]
________________


H2: Where we are now

PAGE: DR. PRAVIN PARMAR
URL: /founder
 Menu: About → Dr. Pravin Parmar
Phase: 1
Meta title: Dr. Pravin Parmar | Founder, Peers Global
Meta description: Founder of Peers Global and the 1 Million Entrepreneurs International Forum. From a farmer family in Botad to building a global community of collaboration.
Primary keyword: Dr Pravin Parmar
Secondary keywords: peers global founder, entrepreneur community founder India
Primary CTA: Download Unity App
Secondary CTA: Read Our Story
Internal links: /our-story · /the-idea · /1-million-mission · /media · /contact
________________


HERO
H1: Dr. Pravin Parmar
Subline:
 Founder, Peers Global
Supporting line:
 "I come from a very poor farmer family. But I do not come from a weak mindset. That difference has shaped everything."
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: Botad
He grew up in a farmer family in Botad, watching life closely — hard work, uncertainty, seasons deciding income, and dignity without money.
When you grow up like that, you do not dream of shortcuts. You dream of stability, respect, and building something that lasts.
He also watched a village carry its own people through a failed season. It was ordinary there. It took him thirty years to see how rare it was elsewhere.
________________


H2: The decision that set the direction
He could not speak English. Government school background. When he decided he needed to learn, the teachers and the principal said no.
He stood up anyway.
It was never really about English. It was about deciding that his background would not determine his ceiling. Authority may control the rules, but he controls his direction.
He paid his own fees by teaching other students while still studying. The day you earn for yourself, life becomes very real. When you pay your own fees, excuses die quietly.
________________


H2: Building, and stopping
He built his own company. He was right about the product and early to the market. Payments did not come, growth stayed limited.
One day he admitted something honest: this business can run my present, but it cannot build my future.
Stopping was painful. It was also clear.
Failure is not when a business ends. Failure is dragging something that no longer belongs in your future because your ego will not let it go.
________________


H2: The hospital
A family crisis, a hospital, and every illusion stripped away.
Standing there he understood that skills mean very little without the right people around you. So does intelligence. So does hard work. What matters in those moments is who you can call.
He had built capability for years. He had never built a circle. And looking around at the entrepreneurs he knew, almost none of them had one either.
That was the moment Peers Global became inevitable.
________________


H2: What he built
Peers Global — the world's first community of collaboration, and the community this website belongs to
VyapaarJagat.com — a media platform for the MSMEs mainstream business media ignores
The 1 Million Entrepreneurs International Forum — a not-for-profit working toward one million lives impacted
Fempreneur and Greenpreneur — communities for women-led and sustainability-focused entrepreneurship
Each of them began with the same observation: entrepreneurs should not have to build alone.
________________


H2: What he is trying to leave behind
Not a company.
A community that keeps working after him — where an entrepreneur in a city he has never visited walks into a room, finds people who understand them, and stops carrying their business alone.
Impact is not money. Impact is confidence, visibility and support.
If one entrepreneur feels less alone. If one business finds clarity. If one founder finds the right peer. Impact has begun.
Numbers are milestones. Lives are the real metric.
________________


H2: Speaking and media
Dr. Parmar speaks on entrepreneurship, community building, collaboration and the MSME ecosystem.
[ Media & Speaking Enquiries ]
________________

"The right circle can change your life faster than the right idea."
Build Your Business. Build Your Relationships. Build Your Circle.

PRODUCTION NOTES
The three-way split — this is the risk
/the-idea, /our-story and /founder all draw on the same source material. Without a strict split they become three versions of one page and all three weaken.


	/the-idea
	/our-story
	/founder
	Subject
	The belief
	The organisation
	The person
	Voice
	First person, founder speaking
	Third person, institutional
	Third person, biographical
	Opens with
	The loneliness entrepreneurs feel
	How the community began
	Botad
	Owns
	Why entrepreneurs are alone · the four-line belief · what a Peer is
	Why community over events · what was built · where we are now · Mission
	The man · the English decision · closing a business · what he is leaving behind
	Ends on
	The 1 Million Mission
	What has not changed
	Legacy
	The hospital moment appears on all three. That is unavoidable — it is the origin of everything — but it is written differently each time: as revelation on /the-idea, as institutional turning point on /our-story, as biography on /founder. Keep those three treatments distinct when editing.
What /our-story needs from you
Real dates and a timeline. The page currently has no years in it, which makes it feel unanchored. Founded when. First Circle when. First city outside Ahmedabad when. First hundred Peers when.
A dated timeline is one of the strongest credibility devices available to an organisation, and this page is where it belongs.
What /founder needs from you
Photography, and the right kind. Not a corporate portrait against a grey background. Him in a Circle meeting, on a stage, with entrepreneurs. The page is about a person who built something for other people — the images should show the other people.
Confirm the doctorate. Dr. appears throughout. Make sure the credential is stated accurately if anyone asks about it.
What I left out of /founder, deliberately
The LinkedIn decision. It is a strong story in a podcast, where there is room to explain the reasoning. On a founder page it invites a question the page cannot fully answer, and it draws attention to a competitor platform. Leave it for interviews.
Design direction
/our-story — build the middle as a dated timeline. The Botad-to-hospital sequence works as flowing prose above it. Live counters near the end, after the argument rather than before it.
/founder — long-form editorial, generous whitespace, one strong photograph per section. The pull quotes are the strongest material here; set them full width. The Botad section should carry an image of him, not of a village.
SEO
/founder targets his own name, which will become a meaningful search term as the community grows. Add Person schema with role, organisation, and links to his verified profiles.
/our-story carries Organization schema with founding date, founder, location and logo. This is the page Google uses to understand what Peers Global is.
________________

A business that grows employs more people. Those people support families. Those families spend in their local economy. An entrepreneur who avoids a costly mistake keeps a team employed that would otherwise have been let go.
One introduction reaches far more than one person, and it keeps reaching for years.

PRODUCTION NOTES — ALL SIX PAGES
/contact — three things it must actually do
Route, do not collect. Seven categories, each going to a different person. A single inbox means partnership enquiries sit behind support tickets.
State a response time and meet it. Two working days is published on this page. If that cannot be held consistently, change the number rather than break the promise.
The grievance officer is legally required. Under Indian IT Rules, a business handling user data and payments must publish a named grievance officer with contact details. This is not optional and it is currently missing.
/investors — publish nothing you cannot defend
This is the one page where a soft number causes real damage. An investor who finds a figure they can disprove stops reading, and word travels in that community.
Only publish metrics that are current, verified and refreshable. If retention data is not yet meaningful, leave it out. A short honest page outperforms a padded one with a sophisticated reader.
Keep this page unpublished until you are genuinely raising. An investor page with no activity behind it reads as a company hoping rather than building.
/partner — the strongest section is who you decline
"We do not partner with organisations that want to sell into our Circles."
That sentence is worth more than the entire rest of the page. It tells a genuine partner that their association with Peers Global will mean something, because access is not for sale.
It also protects your Circles from exactly the pressure that erodes communities as they grow.
/social-impact — resist listing all seventeen SDGs
Naming three and evidencing each is credible. Listing all seventeen tells any informed reader that nobody thought about it seriously.
The framing on this page — that helping one entrepreneur reaches employees, families and local economies — is your genuine impact argument. It is stronger than any grant-language alternative.
Phase 2 discipline
Five of these six are Phase 2, and each fails badly when empty:
A newsroom with no news. A partner page with no partners. An initiatives page linking to sites that do not exist. An investor page with no numbers.
Keep them inactive in menu_items until each has real content. Publish /contact at launch — it is the only one of the six that is genuinely needed on day one.
________________


That completes the About menu, and every page in the seven-item header. Next: the footer pages, or the legal set?

 A working library for practitioners.

H2: One rule
Nothing appears here unless a named Peer has actually used it.
Not researched it. Not read about it. Used it, inside a real business, for long enough to know whether it works.
Every entry carries the name of the Peer who recommends it, their business, and what they used it for. If you want to know more, they are one connection request away in the Unity App.
That single rule is what separates this from every list of tools on the internet.

________________


H2: How an entry gets here
A Peer recommends it. Through the Unity App, naming the tool and what they used it for.
They state their experience. How long they have used it, what it replaced, and what it is genuinely good and bad at.
It is published with their name on it. Their recommendation, their reputation.
Other Peers can ask them about it. Directly, through the app.
Nobody pays to appear here. No vendor can buy a listing, and none of these are affiliate links.
________________


H2: What a good entry looks like
[Tool name] — Category
What it does: one clear line.
Recommended by [Name], [Business], [City]
 "We moved to it eighteen months ago from [previous]. It fixed [specific problem]. It is weak at [honest limitation]. Worth it if [the condition under which it works]."
[ Connect with [Name] in the app ]
The honest limitation is compulsory. A recommendation without one is marketing, and every experienced business owner reads it that way.
________________


H2: Recommend a tool
If something changed how your business runs, tell the community.
Submit it in the Unity App. Name the tool, what it replaced, what it fixed, and where it falls short.
Sharing what works is one of the ten Ways of Collaboration, and it earns Life Impact like any other contribution.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: Common questions
Do vendors pay to be listed?
 No. There are no paid listings, no sponsored entries and no affiliate links anywhere on the Watchlist.
Can I contact the Peer who recommended something?
 Yes. Send a connection request in the Unity App and ask them directly. That conversation is worth more than any review.
Can I recommend a tool I built?
 Only where it is disclosed. A Peer recommending their own product must state that clearly, and it is labelled accordingly.
Do I need to be a member to read this?
 No. The Watchlist is open. Connecting with the Peer who recommended something requires membership.
How often is it updated?
 Continuously. Entries are added as Peers submit them, and removed when a Peer no longer stands behind a recommendation.

PRODUCTION NOTES
Word count: ~750 · Type: DB-driven · Phase: 1
The rule is the entire product
Nothing appears here unless a named Peer has actually used it.
Every tools list on the internet is written by someone who has not used the tools. Yours is written by business owners who have, with their names attached and their reputations exposed.
Protect that rule absolutely. The first paid listing destroys the page permanently, and no amount of revenue is worth it. If a vendor offers money to appear, the answer is no.
The mandatory honest limitation
"It is weak at [X]" in every entry.
This is what makes the Watchlist credible. A page of unqualified praise reads as advertising regardless of who wrote it. A recommendation that names a genuine weakness reads as a real person talking.
Enforce it in the submission form — make the limitation a required field. A Peer who cannot name one has not used the tool seriously enough.
The strongest feature is the connection
"Connect with [Name] in the app."
Nobody else can offer this. Every other tool review is anonymous or from a stranger. Here, the person who recommended it is reachable, and you can ask them the question the review does not answer.
It is also a membership driver. A visitor reading the Watchlist for free will eventually want to ask someone a question, and that requires joining.
SEO — this is a genuine traffic asset
The Watchlist will attract people who have never heard of Peers Global. Best accounting software for small business India, ERP for manufacturing, CRM for MSME — high-volume commercial searches, currently dominated by affiliate content farms.
Yours has something none of them have: named practitioners with real businesses.
Phase 2: individual tool pages at /watchlist/{tool}, and category pages at /watchlist/{category}. Each one targets a specific search with content that is genuinely better than what ranks today.
Add Review schema with the named reviewer, and ItemList on category pages.
Design direction
The Peer's photograph is the most important element on every card. It is what makes this different from every other tools list. Face first, tool second.
Show the quote prominently. The tool description matters less than the practitioner's verdict.
Filters must work well — a visitor arriving from search wants their category immediately.
Developer instructions
resources table: tool name, category, description, recommending Peer ID, business, city, their verdict, the limitation, disclosure flag, published status.
Limitation is a required field. Enforce at database level.
Disclosure flag for a Peer recommending their own product, rendered visibly on the card.
Submission flows through the Unity App. A Peer can withdraw a recommendation at any time, and the entry comes down.
Track which entries drive the most connection requests. That tells you which categories to build out.

PAGE: THE GIVE-FIRST PRINCIPLE
PAGE: THE GIVE-FIRST PRINCIPLE
URL: /give-first
 Menu: Footer → How We Collaborate
Phase: 2
Meta title: The Give-First Principle | Peers Global
Meta description: Give before you ask. The founding rule of Peers Global, why it works, and how it turns a room of strangers into a community that produces.
Primary keyword: give first principle
Secondary keywords: giving in business networking, reciprocity in business, business community culture
Primary CTA: Download Unity App
Internal links: /the-language · /10-ways-of-collaboration · /the-currency · /culture-and-code · /circle-meeting-experience
________________


HERO
H1: Give First
Subline:
 You contribute before you ask. Always.
Supporting line:
 It is the founding rule of this community and the reason everything else works.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: The rule
Before you ask anything of this community, you give something to it.
An introduction. A referral. A lesson that took you years to learn. A vendor you have tested. An hour with someone facing a decision you have already made.
Not because it will be returned. Because that is the standard here, and every Peer around you is holding it too.
________________


H2: Why a rule and not a value
Most communities say they believe in giving. Very few produce it.
The reason is simple. In a room where everyone is waiting to see what they receive, nobody moves. Giving feels risky. Asking feels premature. So people exchange cards, say something polite, and nothing happens.
Give-First removes the waiting.
When contribution is the entry condition rather than a hoped-for outcome, the first move is already made. Every person in the room arrived intending to give, which means the room starts producing from the first meeting rather than the sixth.
A value is something people agree with. A rule is something people do.
________________


H2: What it actually solves
It makes asking easy
In most business rooms, asking for help feels like an imposition. You are conscious of what you are taking.
In a Circle where everyone has already given, asking is simply the other half of a conversation that was always going to happen. Nobody is keeping score, because everybody is contributing.
It removes the sellers
Someone who arrives to extract cannot sustain Give-First for long. Within a quarter, the room can see who gives and who only takes.
The rule filters without anyone having to enforce it.
It builds trust faster than time does
Trust normally takes years. Give-First compresses it, because the fastest way to trust someone is to watch them do something for another person that brings them nothing.
In a Circle, that happens in front of you every month.
________________


H2: How it works in practice
In the meeting. The Gratitude & Life Impact Round opens every Circle meeting with what people gave. Before anyone asks for anything, the room has heard an hour of contribution.
In the Give and the Ask. Every Peer states what they can offer before stating what they need. The order is deliberate.
In the app. Contributions are logged in Unity and confirmed by the Peer who received them. What you gave becomes a record.
In your standing. Your Life Impact Score, your Peer Standing and your Peers Coin all follow from what you gave, never from what you asked for.
[ See the Meeting Agenda ]
________________


H2: What it does not mean
It does not mean giving without limit. Peers run businesses. Nobody expects unlimited time or free work.
It does not mean never asking. Asking is how a community functions. Give-First governs the order, not the entitlement.
It does not mean keeping score. A Peer who gives while counting has misunderstood it. The record exists so the community can recognise contribution, not so you can invoice it.
It does not mean every month is equal. Some months you give more than you receive. Some months the reverse. Over a year it balances, and the room knows the difference between an honest quiet month and someone who never gives.
________________


H2: What it returns
Not in the way most people expect.
You will not get back what you gave, from the person you gave it to, on a timeline you can predict. That is not how it works.
What happens instead is that a room of entrepreneurs starts thinking about you.
When something crosses their desk that fits your business, you are who they think of. When someone asks them for a recommendation in your field, they give your name. When you finally do need something, the request lands among people who already know you as someone who gives.
That is the return, and it compounds.
Peers arrive expecting a transaction and discover something slower and considerably larger.
________________


H2: Where it came from
Business grows through relationships.
Relationships grow through trust.
Trust grows through contribution.
And contribution creates impact.
Read those from the bottom up and Give-First is the first step. Everything else in this community follows from it.
[ Read The Idea ]
________________


CLOSING
Give first. Everything else follows.
Build Your Business. Build Your Relationships. Build Your Circle.
[ DOWNLOAD UNITY APP ]
________________

The section that matters most
"What it does not mean."
Every serious entrepreneur reading a give-first philosophy has the same three objections: I do not have unlimited time, I cannot work for free, and I am not going to be the only one giving.
Answering all three before they are raised is what makes the rest of the page credible. Without that section, this reads as idealism to anyone over forty.
The honest bit about returns
"You will not get back what you gave, from the person you gave it to, on a timeline you can predict."
This is true, and saying it protects you. A member who joined expecting direct reciprocity and did not get it becomes a complaint. A member who was told plainly how the return actually works becomes patient.

PAGE: PEER-TO-PEER MEETINGS
URL: /peer-to-peer
 Menu: Footer → How We Collaborate
Phase: 2
Meta title: Peer-to-Peer Meetings | Peers Global
Meta description: The one-to-one meeting between two Peers is where most real collaboration at Peers Global begins. How they work and why they matter.
Primary keyword: one to one business meetings
Secondary keywords: business relationship building, entrepreneur one to one, peer meetings
Primary CTA: Download Unity App
Internal links: /circle-meeting-experience · /10-ways-of-collaboration · /unity · /circles · /give-first
________________


HERO
H1: Peer-to-Peer
Subline:
 The Circle meeting introduces you. This is where the relationship is built.
Supporting line:
 Two Peers, one hour, and no agenda except understanding each other's business properly.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: Where collaboration actually begins
A Circle meeting puts thirty entrepreneurs in a room together. That is valuable, and it is not enough.
You cannot understand someone's business from a four-minute showcase. You cannot know what they need, who would be right for them, or what you could genuinely do for them.
That understanding happens one to one.
Ask any Peer where their most significant collaboration came from and the answer is almost always the same. Not from the meeting. From a conversation with one person, afterwards.
________________


H2: What a Peer-to-Peer is
One hour. Two Peers. No agenda beyond understanding each other properly.
What you cover:
What their business actually does, beyond the description
Who their ideal customer is, specifically enough that you could recognise one
What they are working on this year
What is difficult right now
Who they are trying to reach
What they can offer that most people do not know about
And the same, from you.
What it is not: a sales meeting. Neither person is selling to the other. If a Peer-to-Peer becomes a pitch, both people have wasted an hour and one of them has damaged a relationship.
________________


H2: Why one hour with one person outperforms a room
You can be specific. In a room you describe your business generally. One to one you describe the exact client you want, the exact market you are entering, the exact problem you cannot solve.
You can be honest. Nobody says this quarter is difficult in front of thirty people. They say it across a table to one.
They can actually help. Vague understanding produces vague introductions. Specific understanding produces the right one.
It compounds. A Peer who understands your business properly keeps thinking about you for years. Every time something crosses their desk that fits, you are who they think of.
________________


H2: How they work
Book in the Unity App. Any Peer you are connected to. See their availability, book directly.
Meet. In person where possible, online where not. An hour is standard.
Come prepared. Look at their profile and their business before you arrive. Arriving cold wastes half the hour.
Give something. An introduction, a contact, a piece of knowledge, a resource. Give-First applies here more than anywhere.
Log what came of it. Any contribution either of you made gets recorded in Unity and confirmed by the person who received it.
[ See How Unity Works ]
________________


H2: How many
There is no quota, and imposing one would produce meetings held to hit a number rather than to build a relationship.
But the pattern among Peers who get the most from this community is consistent. They do them regularly, with everyone in their Circle over the first year, and then with Peers from other Circles and other cities as their needs change.
A Peer who attends twelve meetings a year and never sits down with anyone one to one has experienced perhaps a third of what this community offers.
________________


H2: Beyond your own Circle
Peer-to-Peers are not limited to the room you sit in.
Any Peer you are connected to in the Unity App — in any Circle, any city, any country — can be booked for a one-to-one. A manufacturer in another state. Someone who already operates in the market you are entering. A founder five years ahead of you.
Charter Peers can reach any Peer in the community directly, without a connection request first.
[ See Membership ]
________________


CLOSING
The room introduces you. The hour afterwards is where it starts.
Build Your Business. Build Your Relationships. Build Your Circle.
[ DOWNLOAD UNITY APP ]
________________

Why this page earns its place
The Peer-to-Peer is mentioned across five pages and explained fully on none. It is the single most important activity in the community and the strongest predictor of whether a member renews.
A Peer who does one-to-ones stays. A Peer who only attends meetings leaves in year two. This page exists to make that clear before someone joins, and to give Circle Directors something to send new Peers in week one.
The most useful section
What you cover. Six specific things, which turns a vague instruction — get to know each other — into a usable structure.
Give this list to every new Peer during onboarding. It is the difference between a productive first one-to-one and an hour of pleasantries.

PAGE: HOW A CIRCLE WORKS
PAGE: HOW A CIRCLE WORKS
URL: /how-a-circle-works
 Menu: Footer → How We Collaborate
Phase: 2
Meta title: How a Circle Works | Peers Global
Meta description: How a Peers Global Circle is composed, how seats work, how it runs and how it grows. The mechanics behind the room.
Primary keyword: how business circles work
Secondary keywords: business circle structure, entrepreneur group format, business networking group how it works
Primary CTA: Download Unity App
Internal links: /circles · /circle-meeting-experience · /circle-roles · /circles/find · /start-a-circle · /10-ways-of-collaboration
________________


HERO
H1: How a Circle Works
Subline:
 The mechanics behind the room.
Supporting line:
 How a Circle is composed, how seats work, how it runs, and how it grows.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: One seat, one business category
This is the rule everything else depends on.
Each Circle holds one business per category. If you hold the seat for industrial packaging, you are the only industrial packaging business in that room.
Three things follow from it.
You can speak openly. Nobody is studying your pricing, learning your process or waiting for you to reveal a client name.
You get the full flow. Every requirement in your category comes to you, because there is no one else to send it to.
Giving costs you nothing. When there is no competitor in the room, helping another Peer is straightforward. That is why generosity is normal here rather than exceptional.
A seat is not a subscription. It is a position in a specific room, and there is only one of it.
________________


H2: How a Circle is composed
Circles are built deliberately, seat by seat.
Complementary, never competing. Businesses that can serve each other's clients, solve each other's problems and open each other's markets.
Varied by function within a shared focus. An Industry Circle groups a sector but varies the business types inside it. A Purpose Circle groups a shared ambition but varies the industries.
Sized for depth. Large enough that every meeting has substance and enough categories are represented. Small enough that everyone genuinely knows your business.
Reviewed for fit. Every request is assessed by the Circle Director and the Membership Experience Committee before a seat is confirmed.
[ See Criteria & Process ]
________________


H2: The rhythm
Twelve meetings a year. A fixed schedule, published well in advance, so busy business owners can plan around it.
The same people, every time. This is the mechanism, not a side effect. Trust needs repetition, and repetition needs a calendar.
The same agenda. Four parts, in the same order, in every Circle in every city.
[ See the Meeting Agenda ]
________________


H2: What happens between meetings
A Circle meets twelve times a year and works all year.
Peer-to-Peer meetings. One to one, outside the Circle, to understand each other's business properly. Most real collaboration begins here.
The Unity App. Requirements shared, introductions made, contributions logged and confirmed, one-to-ones booked.
Follow-through. An introduction promised at a roundtable becomes a call the following week, and gets declared at the next Impact Round.
[ See Peer-to-Peer Meetings ]
________________


H2: Who runs it
Circle Founder — the entrepreneur who convened the room and set its standard
Circle Director — holds the culture, the rhythm and the growth of the Circle, and mentors its Chairs
Chairs and Committees — Peers who run the working life of the Circle
The Membership Experience Committee — reviews and approves every request for a seat
Every one of them is a working business owner.
[ See Circle Roles ]
________________


H2: How a Circle grows
Seats fill. Categories that are open get taken, and the room's coverage widens.
Relationships deepen. Year two produces considerably more than year one, because the room now knows each other properly.
Contribution compounds. Each collaboration makes the next one easier to see.
The Circle becomes known. In its city and its industry, as a room worth being in.
A Circle is not finished when its seats fill. That is when it starts working properly.
________________


H2: What keeps a Circle healthy
Attendance. Consistency is the foundation. A Peer who stops attending stops mattering to the room, and the room notices.
Contribution. Every Peer giving, not only the generous few.
Standards. The Peers Code held by everyone, and enforced by the Director when it is not.
Composition. The right entrepreneurs in the right seats, and honest conversations when someone is not the right fit.
Leadership. A Director who is present, and Chairs who are genuinely active.
[ Read the Peers Code ]
________________


H2: Joining a Circle
1. Subscribe to Peers Global in the Unity App
2. Attend a meeting as a guest
3. Request the Circle in the app
4. Your request is reviewed by the Circle Director and the Membership Experience Committee — usually within a week
5. On approval, pay the Circle Experience Fee
6. Your category is locked to you in that room
[ Find Your Circle ]
________________


CLOSING
The rules are simple. What they produce is not.
Build Your Business. Build Your Relationships. Build Your Circle.
[ DOWNLOAD UNITY APP ]
________________

PAGE: ROLES INSIDE A CIRCLE
URL: /circle-roles
 Menu: Footer → How We Collaborate

Meta title: Roles Inside a Circle | Peers Global
Meta description: Who runs a Peers Global Circle — the Founder, the Director, three committees, their Chairs and Leaders, and the Project Chairs.
Primary keyword: circle roles peers global

________________


HERO
H1: Roles Inside a Circle
Subline:
 A Circle is not run by one person.
Supporting line:
 Nineteen roles, three committees, and a room held together by the entrepreneurs inside it.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: Every role is held by a working business owner
Nobody here is staff.
The people who run a Circle run their own companies. They took the role because leading a room of accomplished entrepreneurs develops something no course teaches, and because they wanted to build something beyond their own business.
That is why the standard holds. It is being held by people who chose to hold it.
It is also how leadership begins here. Almost every Circle Director served on a committee first.
________________


H2: THE CIRCLE LEADERSHIP
Circle Founder
Convened the room.
Brought the first entrepreneurs together, defined what the Circle is for, and set its culture from the first meeting. Carries the first year, then remains Founder while a Director takes on the day-to-day.
[ Read the Circle Founder Role ]
________________


Circle Director
Holds the Circle.
Culture, rhythm, standards, growth and the health of the room. Chairs the meetings, mentors the three Chairs, and is responsible for what the Circle becomes.
A Circle becomes what its Director makes it.
[ Read the Circle Director Role ]
________________


H2: THE THREE COMMITTEES
Each committee is led by a Chair and three Leaders. Together they run the working life of the Circle.
________________


Business Growth Committee
Responsible for what the Circle produces.
Chair, Business Growth Committee
 Leads the committee and is accountable for the collaboration the Circle generates.
Business Growth Leader
 Drives referrals, introductions and business flow between Peers. Makes sure the Collaboration Roundtables produce named outcomes rather than conversation.
Recognition & PR Leader
 Ensures contribution is recognised — in the room, in the community and in the media. Works with VyapaarJagat and the Peers Global media ecosystem to give the Circle's Peers visibility.
Cross-Border Collaborations Leader (Unity)
 Connects the Circle beyond its own city. Opens collaboration with Peers in other Circles, other regions and other countries through the Unity App.
________________


Membership Experience Committee
Responsible for who is in the room, and how they experience it.
Chair, Membership Experience Committee
 Leads the committee and reviews every request to join the Circle, assessing fit and category availability.
Member Success Leader
 Looks after every Peer's experience — the new Peer finding their footing, the established one whose contribution has quietened, the entrepreneur going through a difficult year.
Peers Board of Advisory Leader
 Connects Peers to the senior advisors and mentors available across the community.
Peers Brand Showcase Leader
 Runs the Brand Showcase — the rotation, the preparation, and making sure each Peer's four minutes produce actions rather than impressions.
[ See Criteria & Process ]
________________


Events & Impact Committee
Responsible for what the Circle does beyond the meeting.
Chair, Events & Impact Committee
 Leads the committee and is accountable for the Circle's events and its impact.
Events & Experience Leader
 Runs the meetings and events themselves — venue, format, flow and the experience of everyone in the room.
Skill Development Leader
 Programmes the Impact Mentor Masterclasses. Chooses the subject the room needs and finds the person who has genuinely done it.
Partnership & Visitor Leader
 Manages guests, visitors and partnerships. The first person a visiting entrepreneur meets, and the one who makes sure they are welcomed properly.
________________


H2: PROJECT CHAIRS
Beyond the three standing committees, a Circle Director can appoint Project Chairs for specific initiatives.
Nine Project Chair roles are available for work that sits outside the regular rhythm — a major event, a community initiative, a partnership, a special programme.
[POPULATE with your nine Project Chair roles]
Why they exist: a Circle that only does its twelve meetings is a Circle that is not growing. Project Chairs are how a room takes on something ambitious, and how more Peers get a first taste of leading.
________________


H2: The full structure


	Role
	

	Circle Founder
	

	Circle Director
	Business Growth Committee
	Chair · Business Growth Leader · Recognition & PR Leader · Cross-Border Collaborations Leader
	Membership Experience Committee
	Chair · Member Success Leader · Peers Board of Advisory Leader · Peers Brand Showcase Leader
	Events & Impact Committee
	Chair · Events & Experience Leader · Skill Development Leader · Partnership & Visitor Leader
	Projects
	Up to nine Project Chairs, appointed by the Circle Director
	Fourteen standing roles in every Circle, plus projects.
________________


H2: How roles are filled
From within. Chairs and Leaders come from the Peers of that Circle.
By contribution, not tenure. Length of membership does not qualify anyone. Record does.
Through conversation. Nobody is appointed by surprise. The time, the responsibility and the fit are discussed openly first.
By invitation as often as by application. Most Chairs were approached rather than volunteering. Contribution is visible here, and people notice.
Project Chairs are appointed by the Circle Director as the work arises.
[ Apply to Lead ]
________________


H2: Why serve
You develop as a leader. Leading entrepreneurs who do not report to you is a discipline most business owners never practise.
You know everyone. The Peers running a Circle know every person in it, and every person knows them.
Your standing rises. Service is the highest-recognised form of contribution here.
It is where leadership begins. Almost every Circle Director, and many Founders, started on a committee.
You build something that lasts. A Circle you helped hold together continues changing businesses long after your term ends.
[ Explore Leadership ]

One of those roles is available in yours.

PRODUCTION NOTES
Word count: ~800 · Type: Static · Phase: 2
This structure is a genuine differentiator
Fourteen defined roles in every Circle, plus nine Project Chairs, is considerably more structured than most business communities operate.
Publishing it does three things:
It proves the community is built, not improvised. A named role structure signals an institution rather than a group that meets.
It shows a path. A prospective member sees fourteen ways to get involved beyond attending. That is a retention argument as much as an acquisition one.
It makes leadership feel reachable. Leader of Skill Development is a role someone can imagine holding in year one. Circle Director is not. The committee layer is where leadership actually begins here, and this page makes that visible.
Three roles worth highlighting elsewhere
Cross-Border Collaborations Leader. No other business community in India has this role. It is direct evidence of the local to global positioning and it belongs on /the-territory and /circles/purpose as well.
Recognition & PR Leader. Proves the media and visibility claims made across the site are structurally supported, not aspirational. Worth referencing on /membership/benefits.
Member Success Leader. Nobody expects a business community to have someone responsible for whether each individual member is thriving. It answers the what if my Circle is not delivering value concern before it is raised.
What I still need
The nine Project Chair roles. Currently a placeholder. Send the list and I will write them in.
Also updates /leadership/circle-director
That page currently says three Chairs of three committees generically. It should now name them:
The Chairs of the Business Growth, Membership Experience and Events & Impact Committees report to the Director. Nine Leaders sit beneath them. A Director develops all twelve, and appoints Project Chairs as the Circle takes on new work.
That single correction makes the Director role considerably more concrete.
SEO
Low search volume, high internal value. This page is what a Circle Director sends to a Peer who asks how they can get more involved.

PAGE: LIFE IMPACT SCORE
URL: /life-impact-score
 Menu: Footer → The Currency
Meta title: Life Impact Score | Peers Global
Meta description: Your Life Impact Score is the record of what you have given to other entrepreneurs — logged in the Unity App and confirmed by the Peer who received it.
Primary keyword: life impact score
Secondary keywords: contribution tracking business community, business giving record
Primary CTA: Download Unity App
________________


HERO
H1: Life Impact Score
Subline:
 The record of what you have given.
Supporting line:
 Logged as it happens. Confirmed by the person you helped.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: What it is
Every time you help another entrepreneur in this community, it can be recorded.
An introduction that opened a door. A referral that converted. Knowledge that saved someone eighteen months. A vendor you had already tested. An hour with a Peer going through a difficult month.
Each one is logged in the Unity App, confirmed by the Peer who received it, and added to your Life Impact Score.
Over a year it becomes a complete record of what you contributed — not what you claimed, not what you intended, but what other entrepreneurs confirmed you did for them.
________________


H2: Why we measure it
Because contribution that goes unseen slowly stops happening.
Not because people become selfish. Because effort without acknowledgement quietly fades, and a room where nobody notices who gives eventually becomes a room where fewer people do.
And because a community outgrows memory.
In a room of thirty, everyone knows who gives. Across thousands of entrepreneurs in many cities, nobody would — unless it is recorded.
The score exists so that generosity remains visible at scale.
________________


H2: What it is not
It is not a leaderboard. Peers are not ranked against each other, and nobody is publicly compared.
It is not attendance. Showing up is the entry fee. Contribution is what counts.
It is not permanent. Your score reflects an active, contributing member of this community. It ends when your membership does.
It cannot be bought. No subscription level, no payment and no seniority produces a single point.
________________


H2: What it becomes
Your Life Impact Score is the foundation of everything else in the community.
Peer Standing — the recognition and access you hold across the community
Peers Coin — rewards you can redeem in the Marketplace
Leadership eligibility — every role here follows contribution
[ Understand Peer Standing ] [ See Peers Coin ]
________________


H2: Where you see it
In the Unity App. Live, updating as contributions are confirmed, visible only to you and the community.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


CLOSING
Build Your Business. Build Your Relationships. Build Your Circle.
[ DOWNLOAD UNITY APP ]

H2: Contribution beyond collaboration
Teaching a masterclass. Sharing what you know with a room.

Leading. Founding a Circle, directing one, carrying an industry or a territory.

Attendance. Being in the room is the entry condition, not the contribution.

They give without being asked.

PAGE: HOW PEERS CONFIRM IMPACT
URL: /impact-confirmation
 Menu: Footer → The Currency
Meta title: How Impact Is Confirmed | Peers Global
Meta description: You cannot award yourself impact. Every contribution at Peers Global is confirmed by the Peer who received it.
Primary keyword: impact confirmation peers global
Primary CTA: Download Unity App
________________


HERO
H1: How Peers Confirm Impact
Subline:
 You cannot award yourself impact.
Supporting line:
 Every contribution is confirmed by the entrepreneur who received it.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: The rule that makes the record real
Anyone can claim they helped someone.
Here, the person you helped has to agree.
When a contribution is logged in the Unity App, the receiving Peer is notified and confirms it. Until they do, it counts for nothing.
That single step is the difference between a record and a claim.
________________


H2: How it works
1. A contribution is logged. By either Peer, in the Unity App.
2. The receiving Peer is notified. With what was logged and by whom.
3. They confirm, decline or amend. Honestly, in a room where both of them will be sitting next month.
4. On confirmation, it counts. Toward Life Impact Score, Peer Standing and Peers Coin.
________________


H2: Why it holds
Both people are in the same room. A Peer who inflates a contribution is asking someone they will see next month to endorse something untrue. Almost nobody does that.
Declining is normal. Sometimes an introduction did not lead anywhere, or the help was smaller than described. Amending or declining carries no penalty and no awkwardness.
Directors can see the pattern. A Peer whose contributions are frequently declined is having a different conversation with their Circle Director.
________________


H2: What this makes possible
Verified recognition. Every award, every standing, every Impact Creator named at the Summit is backed by confirmations from real people.
Honest reporting. When Peers Global publishes lives impacted, that number is built from confirmed contributions rather than estimates.
Trust in the room. Nobody wonders whether the Peer beside them is inflating their record.
[ Understand the Currency ]
________________


CLOSING
Build Your Business. Build Your Relationships. Build Your Circle.
[ DOWNLOAD UNITY APP ]
________________
