HERO
H1: Peer Standing
Subline:
 What the community recognises you for.
Supporting line:
 Earned through what you gave. Conferred by the people you gave it to.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: The closest thing to wealth in this community
Money buys a subscription. It buys a seat, an app and the right to be in the room.
It buys nothing else.
Standing — whether Peers open doors for you, whether your ask carries weight, whether you can lead — comes from a different currency entirely. It is earned by giving, and only other Peers can award it.
________________


H2: What standing changes
How you are seen. Peers across Circles and cities can see you are someone who gives. That reputation opens doors before you ask.
What you can reach. Access widens as standing grows — beyond your Circle, into your city, region and the wider community.
How your ask lands. When a Peer with strong standing asks the community for something, the community responds. Not from obligation, but because that person has already given.
Where you can lead. Every leadership role follows contribution. Standing is what makes a Peer a candidate.
How you are recognised. In your Circle every month, at city and national events, and in the annual recognition of the community's highest contributors.
________________


H2: How standing is built
Slowly, and that is why it is worth something.
Consistently. A Peer who gives every month outranks one who gave heavily once.
Across the ten Ways. Standing built on referrals alone is narrower than standing built across mentorship, knowledge, support and partnership.
Confirmed. Every point comes from someone who agreed you helped them.
________________


H2: What standing is not
Not a rank. Peers are not ordered against each other.
Not a purchase. No subscription level produces it.
Not permanent. Standing reflects an active contributing member. It ends when membership does.
Not a title. It is a reputation, and reputations are held rather than awarded.
________________

The most valuable thing you can build here is a reputation for giving.

PRODUCTION NOTES — ALL SIX
The split from /the-currency
/the-currency is the pillar page — the argument for why contribution is the currency, covering all three elements in summary.
These six are the detail pages. Each one takes a single element and answers the practical questions the pillar page cannot without becoming unreadable.
No section from /the-currency is reproduced verbatim on any of them. Each detail page adds material the pillar does not carry:
Page
	What it adds
	/life-impact-score
	What it is not · what it becomes
	/how-to-earn-impact
	The full earning table · what does not earn · how to earn more
	/impact-confirmation
	The four-step flow · why it holds · what it makes possible
	/peer-standing
	The five things standing changes · how it is built
	/peers-coin
	Why it exists · the four rules
	/marketplace
	The catalogue · the redemption flow
	Two things to confirm before publishing
Whether Peers Coin expires with membership. I have written it as expiring alongside Life Impact Score, consistent with what you confirmed earlier. If coins persist differently, /peers-coin needs correcting.
The Marketplace launch catalogue. /marketplace cannot publish without real items and real coin prices. Until then, keep it inactive rather than showing an empty shelf.
The section that protects the whole system
"Coins cannot be purchased."
It appears on /peers-coin and /marketplace, deliberately, in both places.
The moment a member believes coins might be buyable, the entire currency loses meaning — and so does the standing and recognition built on it. This is the one repetition across pages that is worth keeping.
SEO
/life-impact-score, /peer-standing and /peers-coin will rank for branded searches from members and prospects looking up what these terms mean.
/how-to-earn-impact is the practical page members return to. Make the earning table downloadable.
All six carry Article schema. /marketplace carries ItemList once populated.
Design
The Currency group should share a visual identity — a consistent gold treatment across all six, so a reader recognises they are inside one system.
/impact-confirmation needs the four-step flow as a diagram with real Unity screens. It is the most credibility-critical mechanic in the community and it should be shown, not described.

PRODUCTION NOTES
This page is an expansion tool, not a content page
Every registration is a data point telling you where demand exists ahead of supply.
A city with twenty registrations and no Circle is your next launch, and this page is the only place that information gets collected systematically.
Route registrations to the Executive Director for the region where one exists, and to central leadership where none does.
The honesty is the strategy
"A city with fifteen entrepreneurs asking is a city we can open properly. One with two is not yet."
Publishing the threshold does two things. It manages expectations so nobody waits indefinitely for a call. And it turns a passive registrant into an active one — someone who wants a Circle in their city now has a reason to tell fourteen other entrepreneurs to register too.
That is the cheapest expansion mechanism available to you.
The founder question on the form
"Would you consider founding the first Circle in your city?"
This single field separates a name on a waiting list from a genuine expansion opportunity. Anyone answering yes goes to an Executive Director immediately, not into a queue.
Circle Magazines — publish only when populated
An empty magazine shelf is worse than no page. If only two Circles have published so far, show those two — a real magazine from a real Circle carries the argument on its own.
The Recognition & PR Leader reference connects this page to the Circle role structure, which is worth doing wherever a footer page touches something the committees own.

PRODUCTION NOTES — THE MAP GROUP
One rule governs all five pages
Only show what actually exists.
No aspirational pins. No "coming soon" cities. No countries listed because expansion is planned there.
A visitor who finds a pin in their city and discovers there is no Circle will not return. An honest small map builds considerably more confidence than a large one full of intentions.
Build the empty state properly: a city or country with no Circle routes to /bring-to-my-city, not to a blank result.
These five pages are thin by design
They are data views, not arguments. The arguments live on /the-territory, which is the pillar page for the whole territory concept.
Each of these renders from the database with minimal copy around it. That keeps them useful without duplicating /the-territory.
/india and /international carry real SEO weight
Business community India, entrepreneur network India are high-volume searches. /india should be a genuine page with real numbers and named leadership, not a thin data view.
/international is Phase 3 in practice. Do not publish it until there is at least one live Circle outside India — a page about international expansion with no international presence is the kind of claim that undermines everything around it.
Developer
All five render from circles, cities and regions tables. Cache hourly.
Track every city searched with no result. That data, combined with /bring-to-my-city registrations, is your complete expansion picture.

PRODUCTION NOTES
/unity needs rewriting
The pillar page currently presents Unity as a directory with logging attached. What you have described is a professional network for entrepreneurs — timeline, followers, likes, comments, direct messaging, group chat, plus the collaboration and impact layer.
That is a much stronger product and a much stronger page. Three changes:
Add two features to the main list. Timeline & Community Feed, and Messaging & Circle Chat. Both are missing entirely and both are among the most compelling things in the app.
Change the framing. From the digital home of the community to something closer to a professional network where every person is a verified business owner who has agreed to the same code.
Lead with the feed. For most people the timeline is what makes an app worth opening daily. The directory is what they search when they need something. The feed is what brings them back when they do not.
Say the word and I'll rewrite /unity against this.
The strongest positioning line across these pages
"This is not social media."
No advertising. No algorithm optimising for outrage. No strangers. Every person is a verified business owner who has agreed to the Peers Code.
That paragraph is worth using well beyond /unity/timeline — on the pillar page, in app store copy, and in onboarding. Entrepreneurs are tired of professional networks that became content farms, and this says plainly that you built the opposite.
The retention argument these pages make
A Circle meets twelve times a year. That alone will not sustain a subscription.
The timeline, the chat and the connections are what make Unity a daily habit — and a daily habit is what makes a member renew.
Worth reflecting on /membership/benefits, which currently undersells the app considerably.
Two things to confirm
Whether the timeline is community-wide or connection-only. I have written it as community-wide, with following as the personalisation layer. A connection-only feed will feel empty for a new member in week one, which is exactly when they decide whether the app is worth opening.
Whether Asks posted to the timeline earn impact when someone responds. They should — it is the same contribution as one made in a meeting, and rewarding it will drive the behaviour that makes the feed valuable.
Developer
Circle group chat is the highest-value retention feature in the app. A Circle with an active chat renews. One without it does not. Prioritise it accordingly.
Notification design matters more than the features themselves. A Peer who is notified when someone in their Circle posts an Ask they can answer will open the app. One who is notified about everything will turn notifications off within a week.
________________


Say the word on the /unity rewrite, or I continue to Growth & Learning.

H2: Learning from practitioners, not presenters
Every Circle meeting includes an Impact Mentor Masterclass.
Not a motivational talk. Not a general overview. One subject, taught properly, by someone who has done it.
A Peer who entered the market you are considering. Someone who built the hiring system you need. An Industry Director explaining a regulatory change they have already dealt with.

Peers. Sharing what you know is one of the ten Ways of Collaboration, and teaching a masterclass earns Life Impact like any other contribution.
Industry Directors. Bringing sector knowledge across every Circle where their industry sits.
Invited experts. Senior figures brought in by Circle Directors, who invite them as hosts of a room rather than as supplicants.
One rule. Nobody teaches something they have not done, and no masterclass is ever a disguised sales presentation.

PAGE: MENTORS & GUIDES
URL: /mentors
 Menu: Footer → Growth & Learning
Meta title: Mentors & Guides | Peers Global
Meta description: Mentorship at Peers Global — experienced entrepreneurs guiding Peers through the decisions they have already faced.
Primary keyword: business mentorship India

HERO
H1: Mentors & Guides
Subline:
 Someone who has already been where you are going.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: Mentorship is one of the ten Ways
Not a programme bolted onto the community. One of the ten defined forms of collaboration, practised throughout it.
Mentorship is knowledge over time. Not one conversation, but a sustained relationship where an experienced entrepreneur commits to another's growth.
It is the deepest form of collaboration here, and the one that changes lives most permanently.
[ See the 10 Ways ]
________________


H2: How mentorship happens here
Inside your Circle. Sitting monthly with entrepreneurs further along than you is mentorship, whether or not anyone calls it that.
Through Peer-to-Peers. A regular one-to-one with someone ten years ahead, sustained over years.
Through the Peers Board of Advisory. Every Circle has a Peers Board of Advisory Leader who connects Peers to senior advisors across the community.
Through Global Advisors. Senior entrepreneurs whose experience is available to the whole community.
[ See Circle Roles ]
________________


H2: Finding a mentor
Search the community. In the Unity App, by industry, city, capability and standing.
Ask your Circle Director. They know the room and the wider community, and they will point you at the right person.
Ask directly. Most experienced entrepreneurs say yes when asked properly — specifically, with a clear reason, and with respect for their time.
________________


H2: Becoming a mentor
You do not need thirty years. You need to be further along than someone else on something specific.
An entrepreneur three years ahead is often a better mentor than one thirty years ahead, because they remember the problem clearly.
Mentorship earns Life Impact, and sustained mentorship is among the most highly recognised contributions in this community.
[ Download Unity App ]

PAGE: NEW PEER GUIDE
URL: /new-peer-guide
 Menu: Footer → Growth & Learning
Meta title: New Peer Guide | Peers Global
Meta description: Your first ninety days at Peers Global — what to do, in what order, to get the most from this community.
Primary keyword: new member guide peers global
Primary CTA: Download Unity App
________________


HERO
H1: Your First 90 Days
Subline:
 What to do, in what order.
Supporting line:
 The Peers who start giving in month one grow fastest here. This is how.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: Week one
Complete your profile properly. Not a job title — what your business actually does, who your ideal customer is, and what you can offer other Peers. A thin profile means nobody knows how to help you.
Read the Peers Code. Six commitments. They are not ceremonial.
Explore the timeline. See what the community is doing, who is asking for what, and how Peers talk to each other here.
Send five connection requests. Start with your own Circle.
________________


H2: Month one
Attend your first meeting. Arrive early. Stay after. The conversations either side of the meeting matter as much as the meeting.
Book three one-to-ones. With Peers from your own Circle. An hour each, no agenda beyond understanding each other's business properly.
Give something. An introduction, a contact, a piece of knowledge, a vendor recommendation. Anything.
Log it. In the Unity App, confirmed by the Peer who received it. Your record starts here.
[ See Peer-to-Peer Meetings ]
________________


H2: Months two and three
Complete one-to-ones with your whole Circle. By month three, everyone in that room should understand your business well enough to recognise an opportunity for you.
Post an Ask. Something specific you need. To your Circle, and to the wider community on the timeline.
Respond to someone else's Ask. Even when you cannot solve it, an introduction to someone who can is a contribution.
Attend a masterclass outside your Circle. Register through the app.
Join a committee. This is where leadership begins here, and it is the fastest way to know everyone.
[ See Circle Roles ]
________________


H2: The one thing that decides everything
Give before you ask.
The Peers who receive the most from this community are, without exception, the ones who gave the most to it — and who started giving before they had received anything.
That is not a philosophy. It is what the record shows.
[ See the Give-First Principle ]
________________


H2: What to avoid in your first ninety days
Waiting to see what you get. A quarter spent observing is a quarter wasted, and the room will read it correctly.
Selling. The fastest way to lose a room. Nobody in your Circle is a prospect.
Attending only. A Peer who comes to twelve meetings and never books a one-to-one has experienced perhaps a third of this community.
Staying inside your Circle. Your Circle is your home, not your limit. The wider community is in the app.
________________


H2: Who to ask
Your Circle Director — anything about the room, the standard or your fit
Your Member Success Leader — anything about your experience as a Peer
In the Unity App — a real person answers
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


PRODUCTION NOTES — ALL SIX
/new-peer-guide is the highest-value page in this group
It is a retention page, not an acquisition page, and retention is where a subscription community lives or dies.
A member who books three one-to-ones and gives something in month one renews. A member who attends twelve meetings passively does not — and they leave believing the community failed them.
This page should be sent to every new Peer on day one, and Member Success Leaders should work from it. Make it downloadable as a checklist.
Consider moving it from the footer into the Membership menu, or linking it from /membership/criteria at the onboarding step.
The recurring pattern across this group
Four of these six pages — Watchlist, Bookshelf, AI Tools, Playbooks — share one mechanic: a named Peer, their honest verdict including the weakness, and a connection request to ask them more.
That mechanic is what makes them credible and what makes them different from every content farm covering the same subjects. Keep it identical across all four so a reader recognises the pattern.
Enforce the honest limitation as a required field in every submission form.
The committee links matter
/masterclasses names the Skill Development Leader. /mentors names the Peers Board of Advisory Leader.
Connecting footer pages back to specific Circle roles does two things — it proves the role structure is real, and it tells a Peer exactly who to approach.
Do this wherever a footer page touches something a committee owns.
SEO
/ai-tools has the most search potential in this group. AI tools for small business India is high volume, and everything currently ranking is affiliate content. Yours has named practitioners.
/bookshelf and /playbooks need individual item pages in Phase 3 to compete properly.
/masterclasses and /mentors are internal-value pages. Low volume, high relevance.
Publishing discipline
/playbooks, /bookshelf and /ai-tools all fail badly when empty.
Minimum to publish: six real entries with named Peers. Below that, keep them inactive.

1 · THE PEERS CODE
1 · THE PEERS CODE
URL: /culture-and-code (already written as a content page — this is the formal version for legal reference)
________________


The Peers Code
Six commitments. Every Peer makes them. Every leader upholds them.
01. Give first. Contribution comes before any ask. We help before we are asked, and without calculating the return.
02. Show up. Trust is built by the same people meeting the same people, consistently, over time.
03. Tell the truth. Especially when it is uncomfortable. A Peer who only agrees with you is of no use to your business.
04. Protect the room. What is shared inside a Circle stays inside it.
05. Respect every Peer. Regardless of the size of their business, the length of their membership or the language they speak.
06. Carry the culture. Every Peer is responsible for the experience of every other Peer.
________________


Status of the Code
The Peers Code forms part of the Membership Terms and is binding on every member from the date membership begins.
Breach may result in suspension or cancellation of membership without refund, in accordance with the Membership Terms.
The Code applies in Circle meetings, in the Unity App, at events, in one-to-one meetings, and wherever a member represents or is identified with Peers Global.
Confidentiality under Commitment 04 survives the end of membership.
________________

2 · COMMUNITY GUIDELINES
URL: /community-guidelines
 Effective from: [DATE]
The Peers Code states what we commit to. These Guidelines state how it applies in practice. They form part of the Membership Terms.
________________


1 · In a Circle meeting
1.1 Arrive on time and stay for the full meeting.
1.2 Come prepared with something to give and something to ask.
1.3 Listen fully when another Peer is speaking.
1.4 Do not sell from the floor. A Circle meeting is not a sales opportunity.
1.5 Keep phones away for the duration.
1.6 Welcome every guest as though they already belong.
1.7 Inform your Circle Director in advance if you cannot attend.
1.8 Where you send a substitute, inform your Circle Director in advance. The substitute is bound by these Guidelines.
1.9 Do not record any meeting, session or masterclass without prior written permission from Peers Global.
________________


2 · In collaboration
2.1 Refer only a Peer you would genuinely recommend.
2.2 Follow up on every introduction you receive, whether or not it converts.
2.3 Close the loop and tell the Peer who helped you what came of it.
2.4 Never misuse a contact given to you in trust.
 2.5 Deliver what you promised, on the timeline you promised.
2.6 Log contributions honestly, and confirm honestly.
2.7 Do not log a contribution that did not occur, or inflate one that did.
2.8 Conduct your own due diligence before entering any commercial arrangement with another member.
________________


3 · In the Unity App
3.1 Post as you would speak in the room.
3.2 Respond to Asks you can genuinely help with.
3.3 Use direct messaging to connect, never to pitch.
 3.4 No forwards, no broadcasts, no bulk messaging.
3.5 What is shared in a Circle chat stays in that chat.
3.6 Do not post content that is false, misleading, defamatory, obscene, discriminatory, or that infringes any third-party right.
3.7 Do not post advertising, promotional material or solicitations.
3.8 Do not scrape, copy, export or extract member information by any means.
3.9 Report anything that breaches these Guidelines.
________________


4 · Across the community
4.1 Represent Peers Global with dignity, inside and outside the room.
4.2 Represent your business, role and credentials accurately at all times.
4.3 Bring in entrepreneurs who will strengthen the community.
4.4 Keep member information confidential.
4.5 Respect every Peer regardless of the size of their business.
4.6 Raise concerns directly with your Circle Director.
4.7 Do not use the Peers Global name, marks or your membership to imply endorsement of your business, products or services.
4.8 Where you hold a leadership role, you may represent Peers Global within the scope of that role only. That right ends when the role ends.
________________


5 · Confidentiality
5.1 Information shared within a Circle meeting, Circle chat, committee, one-to-one meeting or any Peers Global forum is confidential.
5.2 This includes business information, financial position, commercial plans, personal circumstances, difficulties disclosed, and the identity and details of other members.
5.3 This obligation is binding on every member and survives the end of membership.
5.4 Breach may result in immediate cancellation without refund, and may give rise to a claim by the affected member.
________________


6 · Prohibited conduct
Membership may be suspended or cancelled without refund for:
6.1 Using the community, the room or the app primarily to sell
6.2 Breach of confidentiality
6.3 Misuse, scraping, export or commercial exploitation of member data
6.4 Misrepresentation of your business, role, credentials or identity
6.5 Harassment, discrimination, threatening or abusive conduct
6.6 Conduct that damages the community or another member
6.7 Sustained absence without commitment
6.8 Non-payment of any fee due
6.9 Any conduct that renders continued membership inappropriate
________________


7 · Raising a concern
7.1 Raise it with your Circle Director first. Most issues are resolved in one conversation.
7.2 If it concerns your Circle Director, raise it with the Circle Founder or your Executive Director.
7.3 For anything serious, or if you are not satisfied, use the Grievance Redressal process.
________________


8 · How concerns are handled
8.1 Concerns are handled quietly, fairly and directly.
8.2 The member concerned will be given an opportunity to respond.
8.3 The response may range from a conversation to removal from a Circle to cancellation of membership.
8.4 Decisions are made by the relevant Circle Director, Executive Director or Peers Global, and are final subject to the Grievance Redressal process.
8.5 No refund arises from any cancellation for breach.

(k) all fees, and revision of any fee for future periods
(l) removal or refusal of any Content
(m) investigation of complaints and the response to them
(n) features, functionality and availability of the Platform
19.2 Where a decision affects an existing paid membership, it applies from the next renewal, except where it arises from a breach of the Peers Code, the Community Guidelines or these Terms.

4 · MEMBERSHIP TERMS
URL: /membership-terms
 Effective from: [DATE]
These Membership Terms govern your membership of Peers Global. They apply in addition to the Terms of Use, Privacy Policy, Community Guidelines and Peers Code.
________________


1 · Nature of membership
1.1 Membership is personal and individual. It belongs to you as an individual, not to your company.
1.2 Membership is not corporate and cannot be held in the name of a business entity.
1.3 Membership is not transferable to any other person, including a business partner, family member, successor, or purchaser of your business, and cannot be sold or assigned.
1.4 Two or more individuals from the same business may each hold their own membership.
1.5 Membership grants access to the community and the Platform. It does not grant any ownership, equity, security, or entitlement to any commercial outcome.
________________


2 · Two subscriptions
2.1 Membership operates through two separate subscriptions.
2.2 The Peers Global subscription provides access to the Platform, the Unity App, the community, the Peer directory, the recognition system and the Marketplace.
2.3 A Circle subscription provides membership of a specific Circle and is subject to a separate Circle Experience Fee.
2.4 The Peers Global subscription does not include Circle membership.
2.5 Circle meetings, Collaboration Roundtables and Impact Mentor Masterclasses are provided under a Circle subscription only.
________________


3 · Fees
3.1 Peer subscription: ₹18,000 per year, exclusive of applicable tax.
3.2 Charter Peer subscription: ₹1,00,000 per year, exclusive of applicable tax.
3.3 Circle Experience Fee: varies by Circle and by city. The applicable amount is notified on approval of your Circle request.
3.4 Event fees: apply per event and are shown before registration.
3.5 Visitor fees: apply where a member brings a visitor to any meeting or event.
3.6 There is no joining or admission fee.
 3.7 All fees are exclusive of GST in India and of equivalent taxes in other jurisdictions. The final amount including tax is shown before payment.
3.8 We may revise any fee at any time. A revision applies from your next renewal and never to a period already paid for. You will be notified before renewal.
3.9 Instalment options are available in some countries where our payment gateway supports them, and are shown at checkout.
________________


4 · Subscription periods and renewal
4.1 The Peers Global subscription runs for twelve months from the date it is confirmed.
4.2 A Circle subscription runs for twelve months from the date you join that Circle.
4.3 These periods run independently and may not align.
4.4 Your Peers Global subscription must remain active for your Circle membership to continue. If it lapses, your Circle membership is cancelled with it, without refund of any Circle Experience Fee.
4.5 The Peers Global subscription is renewed by you in the Unity App. Renewal notifications are issued in advance.
4.6 Circle renewal is subject to approval by the Circle Director and the Membership Experience Committee. Renewal is not automatic and is not guaranteed.
4.7 We are under no obligation to renew any membership.
________________


5 · Charter Peer
5.1 Charter Peer is a separate subscription, not an upgrade path from an existing subscription.
5.2 The Charter Peer subscription is charged in full. No deduction, credit or adjustment is made against any existing Peer subscription.
5.3 Charter Peer benefits include: direct visibility of full member profiles and contact details across the community; attendance at up to twelve cross-region Circle meetings per year in addition to your own Circle; access to the Charter Circle; first priority of approach for leadership roles, media and speaking opportunities; and access to Charter-only gatherings.
5.4 First priority is a priority of approach, not a guarantee. No leadership appointment, media coverage, feature or speaking opportunity is guaranteed at any subscription level.
5.5 Charter-only events and all other events carry their own fees. No event access is complimentary at any subscription level.
________________


6 · Circle membership
6.1 Circle membership requires a request submitted through the Unity App.
6.2 Every request is reviewed by the Circle Director and the Membership Experience Committee of that Circle. Approval is at their discretion.
6.3 Payment of the Circle Experience Fee follows approval. You are never asked to pay for a seat that has not been confirmed.
6.4 One business per category per Circle. On confirmation, your business category is held by you in that Circle for the membership period.
6.5 Category definitions, boundaries and business classification are determined by Peers Global.
6.6 Category exclusivity applies only within that Circle, not across the community.
6.7 Where two or more individuals from the same business hold membership, they may hold seats in the same Circle under the same category.
________________


7 · Primary Circle
7.1 The first Circle you join becomes your primary Circle and must be an Industry Circle.
7.2 Your primary Circle is displayed on your profile across the Platform.
7.3 Your primary Circle must be renewed for your membership of any other Circle to continue.
7.4 You may not change your primary Circle. It changes only where the Circle does not renew your membership, in which case your remaining Circle becomes primary, or you select which becomes primary.
________________


8 · Number of Circles
8.1 A member may hold membership of two Circles as standard — one Industry Circle (primary) and one Purpose Circle.
8.2 An Industry Circle is mandatory as the primary Circle.
8.3 Additional Purpose Circles may be permitted subject to Circle Director approval.
8.4 Each Circle requires its own request, approval and Circle Experience Fee.
________________


9 · Attendance and substitution
9.1 Members are expected to attend consistently.
9.2 A representative may attend in your place with advance notice to your Circle Director. The substitute is bound by the Community Guidelines and by confidentiality.
9.3 Visitors may attend on payment of the applicable visitor fee, registered through the Unity App.
9.4 Sustained absence without commitment may result in removal from a Circle without refund.
________________


10 · Recognition system
10.1 Life Impact Score, Peer Standing and Peers Coin are community recognition mechanisms.
10.2 They hold no monetary value. They are not a currency, a security, a prepaid instrument or a financial product. They cannot be exchanged for money, sold, traded or transferred between members.
10.3 Contribution is recorded in the Unity App and must be confirmed by the member who received it. A member cannot award themselves impact.
10.4 Peers Coin may be redeemed only in the Peers Global Marketplace, subject to availability.
10.5 Peers Coin cannot be purchased.
10.6 Earning rates, weightings, redemption values, catalogue and availability are determined by Peers Global and may be changed at any time.
10.7 Life Impact Score, Peer Standing and Peers Coin are tied to active membership and expire when membership ends. No compensation arises.
10.8 We may investigate, adjust or cancel any contribution record we consider inaccurate, inflated or fraudulent.

11 · Leadership roles
11.1 Leadership roles are open to members and are appointed by Peers Global and its appointed leadership.
11.2 Appointment, reappointment and duration of any role are at our discretion and are not guaranteed.
11.3 A leader may be removed from a role at any time.
11.4 A leader may represent Peers Global only within the scope of their role. That right ends when the role ends.
11.5 Holding a leadership role carries no fee and confers no ownership, equity or entitlement.

5 · REFUND & CANCELLATION POLICY
URL: /refund-policy
 Effective from: [DATE]
________________


1 · All fees are non-refundable
1.1 The Peers Global subscription, the Charter Peer subscription, the Circle Experience Fee, all event fees and all visitor fees are non-refundable.
1.2 This applies in all circumstances, including voluntary cancellation, non-renewal, cancellation for breach, non-attendance, removal from a Circle, or a change in your business or personal circumstances.
________________


2 · Why
Membership is not a service delivered on a schedule. It is access to a community, and value arrives differently for every member — a business-changing introduction in month one for some, relationships that mature over a year for others.
Because value cannot be measured by time elapsed, it cannot be refunded by time remaining.
This is why we encourage every entrepreneur to download the Unity App, explore the community and attend an event before subscribing. Decide before you pay.
________________


3 · Cancellation by you
3.1 You may cancel at any time through the Unity App or by contacting us.
3.2 Your membership ends immediately.
3.3 No refund arises for any unused period.
 3.4 Life Impact Score, Peer Standing and Peers Coin expire.
________________


4 · Cancellation by us
4.1 We may cancel your membership immediately for breach of the Membership Terms, the Peers Code or the Community Guidelines.
4.2 No refund arises.
 4.3 We may remove you from a Circle while retaining Platform membership. No refund of the Circle Experience Fee arises.
________________


5 · Non-renewal
5.1 If you do not renew, membership ends at the close of your period.
5.2 There is no penalty and no further charge.
5.3 Life Impact Score, Peer Standing and Peers Coin expire.
________________


6 · Exceptions
6.1 Duplicate payments are refunded in full. Contact us with the transaction details within 30 days.
6.2 Failed payments where a subscription was never activated are refunded by the payment gateway according to its timelines.
6.3 No other exception applies.
________________


7 · Events
7.1 Event fees are non-refundable.
7.2 Where we cancel an event and do not reschedule or provide an online alternative, the event fee is refunded or credited at our discretion.
7.3 Where an event is rescheduled, postponed or moved online, no refund arises.
7.4 Non-attendance does not give rise to any refund.
________________


8 · Force majeure
Where a meeting or event cannot proceed due to circumstances beyond our reasonable control, it may be rescheduled or moved online. Fees are not refunded.
________________


9 · Processing

WHAT YOU MUST SUPPLY
Blocking launch:
1. Grievance Officer — name, email, contact hours
2. Full registered address
3. Effective date for all documents
4. Security claims confirmed by your CTO
5. Hosting and data storage jurisdictions
Decisions for your lawyer:
6. Arbitration clause — include or not
7. Liability cap for non-paying users (₹10,000 suggested)
8. Refund processing window (7–14 days suggested)
9. Cooling-off treatment under consumer law
10. Peers Coin characterisation — the most important item on this list
________________


THREE THINGS TO TAKE TO SPECIALISTS
Peers Coin. Clause 10 of the Membership Terms is drafted to characterise it as recognition rather than a financial instrument — no monetary value, not purchasable, not transferable, not exchangeable. Get a specific opinion from your CA and lawyer on GST treatment and the accounting treatment of unredeemed coins before the Marketplace launches. Also: never call it a wallet or a balance in the app.
The no-refund position. Drafted as absolute with the reasoning stated. Under the Consumer Protection Act 2019 an absolute term can be challenged as unfair. Your lawyer should decide whether a narrow cooling-off window is worth adding as protection.
Anti-scraping. Clause 5.3(a) and 5.4 of the Terms of Use are the most commercially important clauses in this entire set. Your product is a directory of verified business owners. Have your lawyer make them as strong as enforceable, including an express right to injunctive relief.


2 · LEARNING
2 · LEARNING
URL: /learning · Phase 2 · Absorbs /masterclasses, /playbooks, /mentors
Meta title: Learning at Peers Global | Masterclasses, Playbooks & Mentorship Meta description: Impact Mentor Masterclasses, business playbooks and mentorship — all from entrepreneurs who built the thing they are teaching. Primary keyword: business learning for entrepreneurs India CTA: Download Unity App
________________


HERO
H1: Learning
Subline: From people who built the thing they are teaching.
Supporting line: Not theory. What actually works.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: The L in LSR
Peers Global runs on the LSR Growth Model — Learning, Sales and Resources.
Learning comes first because everything else follows from it. A Peer who understands their market better makes better decisions, asks better questions, and gives better advice to the room.
All of it comes from practitioners. Nobody here teaches something they have not done.
________________


H2: IMPACT MENTOR MASTERCLASSES
One expert. One subject. Twenty minutes of pure, applicable insight.
Every Circle meeting includes one.
Not a motivational talk. Not a general overview. One subject, taught properly, by someone who has done it — and short enough that it stays practical.
Subjects come from what the room actually needs, programmed by the Skill Development Leader on each Circle's Events & Impact Committee.
What gets taught:
Business operations — pricing, systems, processes, cash flow Growth — market entry, scaling, distribution, expansion People — hiring, second-line leadership, retention, culture Finance — funding routes, working capital, investor readiness Compliance — regulation, filings, licences, sector requirements Technology — automation, AI, tools that genuinely work Industry-specific — programmed by Industry Directors for their sector
Who teaches: Peers, Industry Directors, and invited experts brought in by Circle Directors.
Twelve masterclasses a year, in every Circle.
________________


H2: BUSINESS PLAYBOOKS
What worked, written down by the person it worked for.
A playbook is what a masterclass becomes when it is written down properly.
Each one is produced by a Peer who solved something in their own business — the steps they took, what they got wrong first, what they would do differently, and what it cost.
Every playbook carries the name of the entrepreneur who wrote it, their business and their city. If you want to ask them something the playbook does not cover, they are one connection request away.
Available on:
Growth — entering a new city, market or country People — first key hire, second-line leadership, retention Finance — raising your first round, working capital, investor readiness Operations — systems, processes, ERP, quality Sales — moving from founder-led sales to a team Compliance — filings, licences, sector regulation Succession — handing over, selling, next generation
Write one. A masterclass reaches one room. A playbook reaches every Peer, in every city, for years.
________________


H2: MENTORS & GUIDES
Someone who has already been where you are going.
Mentorship is one of the ten Forms of Collaboration. Not a programme bolted on — a defined practice, used throughout the community.
Where mentorship happens here:
Inside your Circle. Sitting monthly with entrepreneurs further along than you is mentorship, whether or not anyone calls it that.
Through one-to-ones. A regular Peer-to-Peer with someone ten years ahead, sustained over years.
Through the Peers Board of Advisory. Every Circle has a Peers Board of Advisory Leader who connects Peers to senior advisors across the community.
Finding a mentor: search the community in the Unity App by industry, city, capability and standing. Or ask your Circle Director — they know the room.
Becoming a mentor: you do not need thirty years. You need to be further along than someone else on something specific. An entrepreneur three years ahead is often a better mentor than one thirty years ahead, because they remember the problem clearly.
Mentorship counts as one life impacted, like every other Form.
________________


H2: Teaching is contribution
Sharing what you know is one of the ten Forms of Collaboration.
Teach a masterclass. Write a playbook. Mentor a Peer. Each one counts, and each one builds your standing across the community.
Speak to your Circle Director or your Skill Development Leader.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


CLOSING
Learn from experience, not theory.

What a city gives you. One Circle gives you a room. A city gives you a community.
As Circles grow within a city, Peers reach far beyond their own meeting. MindMeld cross-Circle city meetups bring entrepreneurs from IT, MSME, finance, export, real estate, healthcare, IPO and more into one room.
City leadership: Industry Directors hold their sector across every Circle in the city. Area Executive Directors carry defined areas within it.
________________


H2: Districts, States and Countries
Business does not respect city limits. A Peer sells into three cities, sources from a fourth and hires from a fifth.
The territory exists so the community works the way business already does.

4 · AWARDS & RECOGNITION
URL: /awards · Phase 2 · Absorbs /awards/categories, /awards/winners, /awards/impact-creators, /awards/circle-of-the-year, /recognition
Meta title: Awards & Recognition | Peers Global Meta description: We recognise contribution, not size. The Peers who gave the most, the collaborations that produced the most, the Circles that changed the most lives. Primary keyword: entrepreneur awards India CTA: Download Unity App
________________


HERO
H1: Awards & Recognition
Subline: We do not recognise the largest business in the room.
Supporting line: We recognise the entrepreneur who did the most for everyone else in it.
[ Download Unity App ]
________________


H2: Recognition is earned in public
Every action at Peers Global is logged in the Unity App and confirmed by the Peer who received it.
That record is the basis of every award given here.
Nobody nominates themselves. Nobody lobbies. Recognition follows what a Peer actually gave, confirmed by the people they gave it to.
1 Action = 1 Life Impacted.
________________


H2: THE CATEGORIES
Life Impactor of the Year — the Peer with the highest confirmed impact across the community
Top Impact Creators — the community's highest contributors, recognised annually
Collaboration of the Year — the single collaboration that produced the most, between two Peers who met in a Circle
Circle of the Year — the room that gave the most, grew the strongest and changed the most lives
Circle Director of the Year — the Director who built the strongest room
Circle Founder of the Year — the entrepreneur who convened a community where none existed
Industry Director of the Year — the strongest sector built across the community
Executive Director of the Year — the territory that grew the furthest
Powerhouse Member of the Year — the committee role held best
[CONFIRM against the actual category list]
________________


H2: How winners are chosen
From confirmed impact records in the Unity App.
Not from nominations. Not from popularity. From what Peers actually gave, verified by the entrepreneurs who received it.
Circle of the Year is measured on confirmed collaboration, growth, retention and lives impacted.
________________


H2: Recognition happens all year
Awards are annual. Recognition is continuous.
In your Circle, every month. The meeting opens with the Gratitude & Life Impact Round, where each Peer declares their impact out loud.
In the app, continuously. Milestone badges as your contribution reaches each level. National rankings across the whole community.
At city and regional events. Contribution named publicly.
At the Annual Awards & Recognition Ceremony. Once a year, the whole community present.
________________

Recognition here is earned in public and displayed in public.

H2: Why a community runs a media platform
Business media covers startups, funding and unicorns.
The businesses that quietly build families, cities and employment across this country stay largely invisible — not for lack of substance, but because nobody tells their story.
That is why Peers Global has its own media, and why Visibility & PR is one of the ten Forms of Collaboration rather than an afterthought.

7 · SOCIAL IMPACT
URL: /social-impact · Phase 2 · Absorbs /foundation, /sdgs, /social-impact/mentor, /social-impact/volunteer, /impact-reports
Meta title: Social Impact | Peers Global Meta description: How Peers Global creates impact — entrepreneurs helping entrepreneurs, employment created, businesses sustained, communities strengthened. Primary keyword: entrepreneurship social impact India CTA: Download Unity App
________________


HERO
H1: Social Impact
Subline: The most effective social work we do is helping one entrepreneur help another.
________________


H2: How impact actually works here
Impact at Peers Global is not charity, and it is not separate from the business of the community.
A business that grows employs more people. Those people support families. Those families spend in their local economy. An entrepreneur who avoids a costly mistake keeps a team employed that would otherwise have been let go.
One introduction reaches far more than one person, and it keeps reaching for years.
That is why the number we track is lives impacted rather than businesses grown.
1 Action = 1 Life Impacted.
________________


H2: Where we focus
MSME strength. The businesses that build employment across this country, supported with the visibility, knowledge and connections they are usually denied.
Women-led enterprise. Through Fempreneur.
Sustainable business. Through Greenpreneur and the ESG Circles.
First-generation entrepreneurs. People building without a family template, a network or capital behind them.
Young entrepreneurs. Building early, and leading people older than themselves.
________________


H2: The 1 Million Entrepreneurs International Forum
A Section 8 not-for-profit, carrying the work that sits outside a commercial community.
Mentorship programmes for entrepreneurs who cannot yet afford a seat in a room Skill development in partnership with institutions Support for first-generation entrepreneurs Ecosystem initiatives with government and institutional programmes
[POPULATE with actual programmes]
________________


H2: The SDGs we contribute to
Three goals. Evidenced.
SDG 8 — Decent Work and Economic Growth Businesses that grow employ more people. [EVIDENCE]
SDG 9 — Industry, Innovation and Infrastructure MSME strength, formalisation and technology adoption. [EVIDENCE]
SDG 5 — Gender Equality Women-led enterprise through Fempreneur. [EVIDENCE]
We name three and evidence them rather than listing all seventeen.
________________


H2: Get involved
Mentor an entrepreneur. Give an hour to someone who has nobody to ask — a first-generation founder, someone in a skill development programme, someone at the stage you remember. Counts toward your Impact Score.
Volunteer. Time, skills or capacity given to the Forum's work — programme delivery, skill sessions, event support, industry expertise, institutional connections.
Partner with the Forum. For organisations working with the same entrepreneurs.
[ Register Interest ]
________________


H2: Impact reports
[Report archive — download by year]
Every figure is compiled from actions logged and confirmed by Peers in the Unity App.

"Success is not just what you earn. It is how many lives you impact."

HERO

________________


H2: Who we partner with
Institutions and associations — chambers, industry bodies and trade associations working with the same entrepreneurs
Financial institutions — banks, NBFCs and investors serving MSMEs and growing businesses
Technology and service providers — businesses whose products genuinely help entrepreneurs grow
Educational institutions — universities and business schools building entrepreneurship programmes
Government and public bodies — MSME development, skill programmes and ecosystem initiatives
Brands — organisations wanting genuine access to a business-owner audience
________________


H2: What partnership looks like
Event partnership — conclaves, summits, city gatherings and the Annual Awards
Knowledge partnership — masterclasses and content for the community
Ecosystem partnership — programmes built together for entrepreneurs
Media partnership — across VyapaarJagat, Vyapaar Jagat TV, the podcast and Circle magazines
________________


H2: Sponsorship
Rooms of business owners, not an audience of attendees.
What can be sponsored: the Annual Summit · regional conclaves · city gatherings · masterclass series · the Annual Awards & Recognition Ceremony · Leadership Retreats · community initiatives
What sponsors get: visibility across the community · presence at events · association with a community built on standards · access to genuine business owners
What sponsors do not get: member data · a sales platform · the right to pitch a Circle
________________


H2: Advertise with us
Across the Peers Global media ecosystem — VyapaarJagat.com, Vyapaar Jagat TV, the podcast, Circle magazines and the Coffee Table Book.
Not in the Unity App. There is no advertising in the app and there never will be.
________________


H2: Who we decline
We do not partner with organisations that want to sell into our Circles.
A Circle meeting is not an audience, and access to Peers is not a product we sell. Partnerships that treat it that way are declined, however commercially attractive.
That is why a partnership with Peers Global means something.

Subline: Rooms of entrepreneurs who came to learn something usable.
________________


H2: Who we book
People who have done the thing.
A Peer who solved something in their own business. An industry figure who has navigated the change everyone else is facing. Someone with specific, practical knowledge worth twenty minutes of a room's attention.
________________


H2: Who we do not book
Motivational speakers.
Anyone whose session is a route to selling.
Anyone speaking about something they have studied rather than built.
Publishing what we decline is what makes the platform worth standing on.

15 · THE PEERS BOARD OF ADVISORY
URL: /leadership/advisory-board · Phase 2
Meta title: The Peers Board of Advisory | Peers Global CTA: Download Unity App
________________


H1: The Peers Board of Advisory
Subline: Senior entrepreneurs whose experience is available to the whole community.
________________


H2: Every institution needs a memory
Communities lose themselves gradually.
A standard is relaxed once for a good reason. A shortcut is taken because growth demands it. Nobody notices any single decision, and five years later the thing that made it worth building is gone.
The Board of Advisory exists to prevent that.
________________


H2: What Advisors do
Guide the direction of the community — where it expands, what it builds, what it declines
Protect what must not change as it grows
Mentor senior leadership — Founders, Directors, Industry Directors and Executive Directors
Open institutional relationships at a level the community could not reach alone
Support Peers directly, through each Circle's Peers Board of Advisory Leader
________________


H2: Who they are
[Named Advisors — photograph, business, city, and one line on what they advise on]
________________


H2: Who becomes an Advisor
Entrepreneurs who have built and led something significant over decades, and who are at a stage where guiding matters more to them than operating.
Advisors are invited, not appointed.
________________


H2: What it gives
Experience that outlives your own company.
Most business knowledge dies with the business that produced it. Thirty years of judgement, of mistakes paid for, of instincts about people and markets — almost all of it disappears when a founder steps back.
This is where yours keeps working.
________________


Do not publish without at least three named, photographed advisors. An unnamed advisory board is worse than none.
________________

17 · DISCLAIMER
URL: /disclaimer · Phase 1
Meta title: Disclaimer | Peers Global
________________


H1: Disclaimer
________________


1 · General information only
Content on this website and in the Unity App is general information. It does not constitute professional, financial, legal, tax, investment or business advice. Obtain independent advice before acting on anything.
________________


2 · No guarantee of outcome
Peers Global provides access to a community, a platform and a structure for collaboration.
We do not guarantee business, revenue, referrals, introductions, partnerships, funding, media coverage, speaking opportunities, leadership appointment, or any commercial outcome.
Any figures, stories, testimonials or examples published are historic accounts of individual experience and are not a representation of what any member will receive.
________________


3 · Member-to-member dealings
Peers Global is not a party to any dealing between members.
We do not verify, endorse or warrant any member's business, credentials, financial standing, capability or the quality of their work.
We accept no liability for any loss, dispute or claim arising from any dealing between members. Members are responsible for their own due diligence.
________________


4 · Member content
Stories, recommendations, reviews, playbooks and posts contributed by members represent the view of the individual, not of Peers Global. We do not verify claims made in member content.
________________


5 · Third parties
Products, services and tools featured in the Marketplace, the Watchlist and member recommendations are provided by third parties. We do not control, verify or warrant them. Your dealings with any third party are at your own risk.
________________


6 · Recognition system
Impact Score, Peer Standing and Peers Coin are community recognition mechanisms. They hold no monetary value, cannot be exchanged for money, and cannot be transferred. Earning rates, redemption values and availability are determined by Peers Global and may change.
________________


7 · Events and programmes
Programme, speakers, format, venue and timing are subject to change. Events may be rescheduled or moved online. Event fees are non-refundable.
________________


8 · Circle membership
Circle membership is subject to approval by the Circle Director and the Membership Experience Committee. Category availability is not guaranteed and is subject to change.
________________


9 · Leadership
Leadership appointments are made at the decision of Peers Global and its appointed leadership. Appointment, reappointment and duration of any role are not guaranteed.
________________


10 · Impact figures
Impact figures are compiled from actions logged and confirmed by members within the Unity App.
________________


11 · Changes
We may amend this disclaimer at any time. The current version is always published.
________________
